\geq(\tilde{\delta}-\sigma^{2}-4\frac{e^{-\frac{B}{\alpha_{1}}}}{\tilde{\delta}})\iint(\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\psi_{B},

where we used (
5.21
) so

\left|\frac{2\psi_{B}^{\prime\prime}\psi_{B}-[\psi_{B}^{\prime}]^{2}}{4\psi_{B}}\right|\begin{cases}\leq\sigma^{2}\psi_{B}&\quad\text{ for }y_{1}<-\frac{B}{2},\\
=0&\quad\text{ for }y_{1}\geq-\frac{B}{2}.\\
\end{cases}

The choice (
5.1
) and a sufficiently large value of
B
ensure
that the coefficient on the right is at least
\widetilde{\delta}/2
.
Consequently,

\iint[(\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2})\psi_{B}+\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{i,B}-3Q^{2}\varepsilon^{2}\psi_{B}]\geq\frac{\tilde{\delta}}{2}\iint[(\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2})\psi_{B}+\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{i,B}]

so
\mathcal{N}_{i}\lesssim\mathcal{F}_{i}.

∎

5.4
Proof of Lemma
5.7
:

We first perform the computations for smooth, rapidly decaying functions; the
identities extend to the regularity class used in the proposition by a standard
approximation argument. Throughout this proof, primes denote derivatives with
respect to
y_{1}
. Set

\mathcal{H}(\varepsilon):=-(\psi_{B}\varepsilon_{y_{1}})_{y_{1}}-(\psi_{B}\varepsilon_{y_{2}})_{y_{2}}+\phi_{i,B}\varepsilon-\psi_{B}\bigl[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q_{b}^{3}\bigr]

and

N(\varepsilon):=(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q_{b}^{3}.

Using (
4.2
), we decompose

f_{1}=f_{1,1}+f_{1,2}+f_{1,3}+f_{1,4}+f_{1,5}+f_{1,6},

where

\displaystyle f_{1,1}

\displaystyle=2\iint\bigl\{-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon-N(\varepsilon)\bigr\}_{y_{1}}\mathcal{H}(\varepsilon),

\displaystyle f_{1,2}

\displaystyle=2\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\iint\Lambda Q_{b}\,\mathcal{H}(\varepsilon),

\displaystyle f_{1,3}

\displaystyle=2\left(\frac{(x_{1})_{s}}{\lambda}-1\right)\iint(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)_{y_{1}}\mathcal{H}(\varepsilon),

\displaystyle f_{1,4}

\displaystyle=2\frac{(x_{2})_{s}}{\lambda}\iint(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)_{y_{2}}\mathcal{H}(\varepsilon),

\displaystyle f_{1,5}

\displaystyle=-2b_{s}\iint\bigl(\chi_{b}+\gamma y_{1}\chi_{b}^{\prime}\bigr)P\,\mathcal{H}(\varepsilon),

\displaystyle f_{1,6}

\displaystyle=2\iint\Psi_{b}\,\mathcal{H}(\varepsilon).

Step 1: Estimate of
f_{1,1}
.

Since
\psi_{B}=\psi_{B}(y_{1})
, we may write

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(\varepsilon)={}

\displaystyle\psi_{B}\bigl[-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon-N(\varepsilon)\bigr]-\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}+(\phi_{i,B}-\psi_{B})\varepsilon.

Consequently,

\displaystyle f_{1,1}={}

\displaystyle 2\iint\bigl[-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon-N(\varepsilon)\bigr]_{y_{1}}\bigl[-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon-N(\varepsilon)\bigr]\psi_{B}

\displaystyle+2\iint\bigl[-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon-N(\varepsilon)\bigr]_{y_{1}}\bigl[-\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}+(\phi_{i,B}-\psi_{B})\varepsilon\bigr]

\displaystyle=:{}

\displaystyle f_{1,1}^{(i)}+f_{1,1}^{(ii)}.

The first term is the direct source of the second-order dissipation. Indeed,
without estimating anything,

\displaystyle f_{1,1}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=-\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\bigl[-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon-N(\varepsilon)\bigr]^{2}

\displaystyle=-\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}[-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon]^{2}-\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\Bigl\{\bigl[-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon-N(\varepsilon)\bigr]^{2}-[-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon]^{2}\Bigr\}.

Thus the nonlinear square is treated as a perturbation of the genuinely
non-positive term in the first line.

We next compute the linear square. Since the weights depend only on
y_{1}
,

\displaystyle-\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}[-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon]^{2}={}

\displaystyle-\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\bigl(\varepsilon_{y_{1}y_{1}}^{2}+2\varepsilon_{y_{1}y_{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{y_{2}y_{2}}^{2}+2\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}+2\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle+\iint(\psi_{B}^{\prime\prime\prime}-\psi_{B}^{\prime})\varepsilon^{2}+\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime\prime\prime}\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2}.

Here we used

\displaystyle 2\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon\varepsilon_{y_{1}y_{1}}

\displaystyle=-2\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}+\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime\prime\prime}\varepsilon^{2},

\displaystyle 2\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon\varepsilon_{y_{2}y_{2}}

\displaystyle=-2\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle-2\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon_{y_{1}y_{1}}\varepsilon_{y_{2}y_{2}}

\displaystyle=-2\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon_{y_{1}y_{2}}^{2}+\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime\prime\prime}\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2}.

For the second part, separating the linear and nonlinear terms gives

\displaystyle f_{1,1}^{(ii)}={}

\displaystyle 2\iint(-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon)_{y_{1}}\bigl[-\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}+(\phi_{i,B}-\psi_{B})\varepsilon\bigr]

\displaystyle+2\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}N(\varepsilon)_{y_{1}}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}-2\iint(\phi_{i,B}-\psi_{B})N(\varepsilon)_{y_{1}}\varepsilon.

A direct integration by parts yields

\displaystyle 2\iint(-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon)_{y_{1}}\bigl[-\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}+(\phi_{i,B}-\psi_{B})\varepsilon\bigr]

\displaystyle\quad=-2\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\bigl(\varepsilon_{y_{1}y_{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{y_{1}y_{2}}^{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad+\iint\bigl[\psi_{B}^{\prime\prime\prime}-2\psi_{B}^{\prime}-3(\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}-\psi_{B}^{\prime})\bigr]\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}-\iint(\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}-\psi_{B}^{\prime})\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\iint\bigl[(\phi_{i,B}^{\prime\prime\prime}-\psi_{B}^{\prime\prime\prime})-(\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}-\psi_{B}^{\prime})\bigr]\varepsilon^{2}.

Finally, the identity

\displaystyle-N(\varepsilon)_{y_{1}}\varepsilon={}

\displaystyle\left[\frac{(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{4}-Q_{b}^{4}}{4}-(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}\varepsilon\right]_{y_{1}}-\bigl[N(\varepsilon)-3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon\bigr](Q_{b})_{y_{1}}

gives

\displaystyle-2\iint(\phi_{i,B}-\psi_{B})N(\varepsilon)_{y_{1}}\varepsilon

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\iint(\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}-\psi_{B}^{\prime})\Bigl[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{4}-Q_{b}^{4}-4(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}\varepsilon\Bigr]

\displaystyle\qquad-2\iint(\phi_{i,B}-\psi_{B})\bigl[N(\varepsilon)-3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon\bigr](Q_{b})_{y_{1}}.

Combining the preceding identities, we obtain the exact formula

\displaystyle f_{1,1}={}

\displaystyle-\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\bigl(3\varepsilon_{y_{1}y_{1}}^{2}+4\varepsilon_{y_{1}y_{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{y_{2}y_{2}}^{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle+\iint\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}\bigl[-\psi_{B}^{\prime}-3\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+\psi_{B}^{\prime\prime\prime}\bigr]+\iint\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2}\bigl[-\psi_{B}^{\prime}-\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+\psi_{B}^{\prime\prime\prime}\bigr]

\displaystyle+\iint\varepsilon^{2}\bigl[-\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+\phi_{i,B}^{\prime\prime\prime}\bigr]

\displaystyle-\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\Bigl\{[-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon-N(\varepsilon)]^{2}-[-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon]^{2}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\iint(\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}-\psi_{B}^{\prime})\Bigl[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{4}-Q_{b}^{4}-4(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}\varepsilon\Bigr]

\displaystyle-2\iint(\phi_{i,B}-\psi_{B})\bigl[N(\varepsilon)-3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon\bigr](Q_{b})_{y_{1}}+2\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}N(\varepsilon)_{y_{1}}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}.

We split the spatial integral into

\{y_{1}<-B/2\},\qquad\{|y_{1}|\leq B/2\},\qquad\{y_{1}>B/2\},

and denote the corresponding contributions by

f_{1,1}^{<}
,
f_{1,1}^{\sim}
, and
f_{1,1}^{>}
.

The left region
\{y_{1}<-B/2\}
.

We shall use the following estimate only in this region.

Lemma 5.10
.

There exists
\mathcal{D}_{B}>0
, depending only on
B
, such that

\displaystyle\iint f^{2}|\nabla f|^{2}\psi_{B}^{\prime}+\iint f^{4}\psi_{B}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathcal{D}_{B}\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\left[\iint(|D^{2}f|^{2}+f^{2})\psi_{B}^{\prime}+\iint(|\nabla f|^{2}+f^{2})\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\right],

(5.37)

and

\displaystyle\iint|f|^{6}\psi_{B}^{\prime}\leq\mathcal{D}_{B}\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\left[\iint(|D^{2}f|^{2}+f^{2})\psi_{B}^{\prime}+\iint(|\nabla f|^{2}+f^{2})\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\right].

(5.38)

Its proof is given in Appendix
A
,
Lemma
A.2
.

By (
5.26
) and
(
5.11
),

\displaystyle-\psi_{B}^{\prime}-3\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+\psi_{B}^{\prime\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{5}{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime},

\displaystyle-\psi_{B}^{\prime}-\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+\psi_{B}^{\prime\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime},

\displaystyle-\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+\phi_{i,B}^{\prime\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\leq-\kappa\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}.

We now estimate the four nonlinear remainders.

First, by (
5.20
), we have

|\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}-\psi_{B}^{\prime}|\lesssim\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}.

Moreover,

\left|(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{4}-Q_{b}^{4}-4(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}\varepsilon\right|\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}+Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon^{2}.

Applying Lemma
4.3
with the weight

\theta=\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}
, which satisfies (
5.18
), and
using Lemma
3.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\iint_{y_{1}<-B/2}(\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}-\psi_{B}^{\prime})\Bigl[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{4}-Q_{b}^{4}-4(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}\varepsilon\Bigr]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\iint\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{4}+\iint_{y_{1}<-B/2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}+\bigl(e^{-B/\alpha_{1}}+b^{2}\bigr)\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}.

We next estimate the term containing
(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}
. Since

N(\varepsilon)-3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon=3Q_{b}\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{3},

we split it into a quadratic and a cubic contribution. By
(
5.20
) and (
5.15
),

|\phi_{i,B}-\psi_{B}|\lesssim\phi_{i,B}\lesssim C(B)\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\qquad(y_{1}<-B/2).

Therefore, using Lemma
4.3
with

\theta=\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\iint_{y_{1}<-B/2}|\phi_{i,B}-\psi_{B}|\,|(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}|\,|\varepsilon|^{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim C(B)\iint\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}|\varepsilon|^{3}

\displaystyle\leq C(B)\left(\iint\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{4}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim C(B)\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}.

For the quadratic contribution, Lemma
3.3
gives

|Q_{b}(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}|\lesssim e^{-2|y_{1}|/\alpha_{2}}+b^{2}.

For the exponentially decaying part,
the explicit left-tail form of the weights gives

|\phi_{i,B}-\psi_{B}|\lesssim e^{\frac{B}{2\alpha_{1}}-\frac{y_{1}}{\alpha_{1}}}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}.

Since
y_{1}\leq-B/2
and

\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}=1/200
by (
3.1
),

\displaystyle e^{\frac{B}{2\alpha_{1}}-\frac{y_{1}}{\alpha_{1}}}e^{-2|y_{1}|/\alpha_{2}}

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{B}{2\alpha_{1}}+y_{1}(\frac{2}{\alpha_{2}}-\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}})}\leq e^{-\frac{B}{200\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}}.

For the
b^{2}
-part we instead use

|\phi_{i,B}-\psi_{B}|\lesssim C(B)\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}
. Hence

\displaystyle\iint_{y_{1}<-B/2}|\phi_{i,B}-\psi_{B}|\,|Q_{b}(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}|\varepsilon^{2}\lesssim\left(e^{-\frac{B}{200\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}}+C(B)b^{2}\right)\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}.

Combining the quadratic and cubic contributions, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\iint_{y_{1}<-B/2}(\phi_{i,B}-\psi_{B})\bigl[N(\varepsilon)-3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon\bigr](Q_{b})_{y_{1}}\right|\lesssim C(B)\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}+\left(e^{-\frac{B}{200\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}}+C(B)b^{2}\right)\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}.

This computation includes the cut-off-transition region
I_{b}
: there

Q_{b}=O(|b|)
and the cut-off derivative in
(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}
is

O(|b|^{1+\gamma})
, so its product is

O(|b|^{2+\gamma})=O(b^{2})
.

We now treat the term containing
N(\varepsilon)_{y_{1}}
. On

\{y_{1}<-B/2\}
, set

q_{B}:=e^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}+|b|.

Lemma
3.3
and the definition of
Q_{b}
imply

|Q_{b}|+|(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}|\lesssim q_{B}
. Expanding the derivative,

\displaystyle N(\varepsilon)_{y_{1}}={}

\displaystyle 3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}+6Q_{b}(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}\varepsilon+3(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}\varepsilon^{2}+6Q_{b}\varepsilon\varepsilon_{y_{1}}+3\varepsilon^{2}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\left|\iint_{y_{1}<-B/2}\psi_{B}^{\prime}N(\varepsilon)_{y_{1}}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}\right|\lesssim q_{B}^{2}\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}(\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})+q_{B}\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\bigl(|\varepsilon|\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}|\varepsilon_{y_{1}}|\bigr)+\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}.

By Cauchy–Schwarz and (
5.37
),

\displaystyle\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}|\varepsilon|\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}\right)^{1/2},

\displaystyle\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2}|\varepsilon_{y_{1}}|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2}\right)^{1/2},

and both terms are bounded by

\mathcal{D}_{B}^{1/2}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\left[\iint(|D^{2}\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\psi_{B}^{\prime}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right].

After decreasing
\nu^{*}
so that

\mathcal{D}_{B}^{1/2}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\ll 1
, and using

q_{B}^{2}\lesssim q_{B}
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\left|\iint_{y_{1}<-B/2}\psi_{B}^{\prime}N(\varepsilon)_{y_{1}}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}\right|\lesssim\left(\mathcal{D}_{B}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+q_{B}\right)\left[\iint(|D^{2}\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\psi_{B}^{\prime}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right].

Finally, we estimate the nonlinear perturbation of the dissipative square.
Since

N(\varepsilon)=3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon+3Q_{b}\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{3}

and

q_{B}|\varepsilon|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\bigl(|\varepsilon|^{3}+q_{B}^{2}|\varepsilon|\bigr),

we have

|N(\varepsilon)|\lesssim|\varepsilon|^{3}+q_{B}^{2}|\varepsilon|.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\bigl[-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon-N(\varepsilon)\bigr]^{2}-[-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon]^{2}\right|\lesssim(|D^{2}\varepsilon|+|\varepsilon|)(|\varepsilon|^{3}+q_{B}^{2}|\varepsilon|)+|\varepsilon|^{6}+q_{B}^{4}\varepsilon^{2}.

The most delicate term is bounded by Cauchy–Schwarz and (
5.38
):

\displaystyle\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}|D^{2}\varepsilon|\,|\varepsilon|^{3}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}|D^{2}\varepsilon|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}|\varepsilon|^{6}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{D}_{B}^{1/2}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\left[\iint(|D^{2}\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\psi_{B}^{\prime}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right].

Moreover, by (
5.37
) and (
5.38
),

\displaystyle\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{D}_{B}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\left[\iint(|D^{2}\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\psi_{B}^{\prime}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right],

\displaystyle\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}|\varepsilon|^{6}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{D}_{B}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\left[\iint(|D^{2}\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\psi_{B}^{\prime}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right],

while Cauchy–Schwarz gives

q_{B}^{2}\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\bigl(|D^{2}\varepsilon||\varepsilon|+\varepsilon^{2}\bigr)+q_{B}^{4}\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2}\lesssim q_{B}^{2}\left[\iint(|D^{2}\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\psi_{B}^{\prime}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right].

Combining these bounds yields

\displaystyle\left|\iint_{y_{1}<-B/2}\psi_{B}^{\prime}\Bigl\{[-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon-N(\varepsilon)]^{2}-[-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon]^{2}\Bigr\}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\left[\mathcal{D}_{B}\bigl(\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\bigr)+q_{B}^{2}\right]\left[\iint(|D^{2}\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\psi_{B}^{\prime}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right].

The coefficient is arbitrarily small after
B
is fixed and
\nu^{*}
is
chosen sufficiently small.

Given
\delta_{0}>0
, choose
B
sufficiently large and then
\nu^{*}

sufficiently small. The preceding estimates give

\displaystyle f_{1,1}^{<}\leq{}

\displaystyle-2\iint_{y_{1}<-B/2}\varepsilon_{y_{1}y_{1}}^{2}\psi_{B}^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}\iint_{y_{1}<-B/2}\varepsilon_{y_{2}y_{2}}^{2}\psi_{B}^{\prime}-2\iint_{y_{1}<-B/2}\varepsilon_{y_{1}y_{2}}^{2}\psi_{B}^{\prime}

\displaystyle-2\iint_{y_{1}<-B/2}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}\iint_{y_{1}<-B/2}\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}-\frac{\kappa}{2}\iint_{y_{1}<-B/2}\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+\delta_{0}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}.

The last term is global because Lemma
5.10
has a
global right-hand side; it will be absorbed only after the central and
right-region coercive estimates are added.

The right region
\{y_{1}>B/2\}
.

Here
\psi_{B}=1
, and hence
\psi_{B}^{\prime}=0
. Therefore

\displaystyle f_{1,1}^{>}={}

\displaystyle-3\iint_{y_{1}>B/2}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}-\iint_{y_{1}>B/2}\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+\iint_{y_{1}>B/2}\varepsilon^{2}(-\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+\phi_{i,B}^{\prime\prime\prime})

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\iint_{y_{1}>B/2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\Bigl[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{4}-Q_{b}^{4}-4(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}\varepsilon\Bigr]

\displaystyle-2\iint_{y_{1}>B/2}(\phi_{i,B}-1)\bigl[N(\varepsilon)-3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon\bigr](Q_{b})_{y_{1}}.

By (
5.9
),

\iint_{y_{1}>B/2}\varepsilon^{2}(-\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+\phi_{i,B}^{\prime\prime\prime})\leq-\kappa\iint_{y_{1}>B/2}\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}.

Using

\left|(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{4}-Q_{b}^{4}-4(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}\varepsilon\right|\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}+Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon^{2},

we estimate the two pieces separately. Lemma
4.3
, applied
with the weight
\theta=\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}
satisfying
(
5.18
), gives

\iint_{y_{1}>B/2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{4}\lesssim\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\iint(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}.

On the same region, Lemma
3.3
and the exponential decay of

P
give

\|Q_{b}\|_{L^{\infty}(y_{1}>B/2)}^{2}\lesssim e^{-B/\alpha_{1}}+b^{2}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\iint_{y_{1}>B/2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\Bigl[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{4}-Q_{b}^{4}-4(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}\varepsilon\Bigr]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}+\bigl(e^{-B/\alpha_{1}}+b^{2}\bigr)\iint_{y_{1}>B/2}\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}.

Since
\phi_{i,B}\geq 1
on the right, we have

|\phi_{i,B}-1|\leq\phi_{i,B}
. The transition bounds and the
polynomial branch give

\frac{\phi_{i,B}}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}\leq C_{i}B\quad(B/2<y_{1}<B),\qquad\frac{\phi_{i,B}}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}=\frac{y_{1}}{i}\quad(y_{1}\geq B).

Consequently, the decay of
Q_{y_{1}}
gives, for all sufficiently large

B
,

\sup_{y_{1}>B/2}\frac{|(\phi_{i,B}-1)Q_{y_{1}}|}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}\leq C_{i}(1+B)e^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})},

whereas the corresponding contribution from
bP_{y_{1}}
is at most

C(B)|b|
. Here
\chi_{b}=1
on the right. Using

N(\varepsilon)-3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon=3Q_{b}\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{3}
,

\|Q_{b}\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
, and Lemma
4.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\iint_{y_{1}>B/2}(\phi_{i,B}-1)\bigl[N(\varepsilon)-3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon\bigr](Q_{b})_{y_{1}}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\left[(1+B)e^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}+C(B)|b|\right]\left(\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right).

The coefficient involving only the soliton tends to zero as
B
grows.
Thus, with
B
large and
\nu^{*}
small depending on
\delta_{0}
, the
right-region estimate is

\displaystyle f_{1,1}^{>}\leq{}

\displaystyle-2\iint_{y_{1}>B/2}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}\iint_{y_{1}>B/2}\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}-\frac{\kappa}{2}\iint_{y_{1}>B/2}\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+\delta_{0}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}.

The central region
\{|y_{1}|<B/2\}
.

Here

\psi_{B}=1,\qquad\phi_{i,B}=1+e^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}\varphi(y_{1}).

The exact expansion is

\displaystyle f_{1,1}^{\sim}={}

\displaystyle-e^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}\Bigg\{\iint_{|y_{1}|<B/2}(3\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2})\varphi^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad+\iint_{|y_{1}|<B/2}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}-\varphi^{\prime\prime\prime}-3Q^{2}\varphi^{\prime}+6QQ_{y_{1}}\varphi\bigr)\varepsilon^{2}\Bigg\}+R_{\mathrm{Vir}}(\varepsilon),

where

\displaystyle R_{\mathrm{Vir}}(\varepsilon)=e^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}\iint_{|y_{1}|<B/2}\Bigl\{

\displaystyle 3(Q_{b}^{2}-Q^{2})\varepsilon^{2}\varphi^{\prime}-3(Q_{b}^{2}-Q^{2})_{y_{1}}\varepsilon^{2}\varphi

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon^{4}\varphi^{\prime}+4Q_{b}\varepsilon^{3}\varphi^{\prime}-2(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}\varepsilon^{3}\varphi\Bigr\}.

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
v\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
satisfy

(v,Q)=(v,\varphi\Lambda Q)=(v,\varphi Q_{y_{1}})=(v,\varphi Q_{y_{2}})=0.

There exist
\mu_{0}>0
and
B_{0}>0
such that, for
B\geq B_{0}
,

\displaystyle\iint_{|y_{1}|<B/2}\Bigl[(3v_{y_{1}}^{2}+v_{y_{2}}^{2})\varphi^{\prime}+(\varphi^{\prime}-\varphi^{\prime\prime\prime}-3Q^{2}\varphi^{\prime}+6QQ_{y_{1}}\varphi)v^{2}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\mu_{0}\iint_{|y_{1}|<B/2}(|\nabla v|^{2}+v^{2})\varphi^{\prime}-\frac{C}{\mu_{0}}e^{B/(4\alpha_{1})}\iint v^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}.

The proof is given in Appendix
B
, Lemma
B.11
.
Applying Lemma
5.11
to
v=\varepsilon
and multiplying by

e^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}
gives

\displaystyle f_{1,1}^{\sim}\leq{}

\displaystyle-\mu_{0}\iint_{|y_{1}|<B/2}(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+\frac{C}{\mu_{0}}e^{-B/(4\alpha_{1})}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}+R_{\mathrm{Vir}}(\varepsilon).

The profile estimates and Lemma
3.3
give, uniformly in
B
,

|Q_{b}^{2}-Q^{2}|+|(Q_{b}^{2}-Q^{2})_{y_{1}}|\lesssim|b|\qquad(|y_{1}|<B/2).

We estimate the five terms in
R_{\mathrm{Vir}}
separately. Since

e^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}\varphi^{\prime}=\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}
in the central region,

\displaystyle e^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}\left|\iint 3(Q_{b}^{2}-Q^{2})\varepsilon^{2}\varphi^{\prime}\right|\lesssim|b|\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}.

Moreover,

\frac{\varphi(y_{1})}{\varphi^{\prime}(y_{1})}=\alpha_{1}\bigl(1+e^{-y_{1}/\alpha_{1}}\bigr)\lesssim C(B)\qquad(|y_{1}|<B/2),

so

\displaystyle e^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}\left|\iint 3(Q_{b}^{2}-Q^{2})_{y_{1}}\varepsilon^{2}\varphi\right|\lesssim C(B)|b|\iint\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}=C(B)|b|\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}.

For the quartic term, Lemma
4.3
with

\theta=\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}
gives

e^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}\iint_{|y_{1}|<B/2}\varepsilon^{4}\varphi^{\prime}=\iint_{|y_{1}|<B/2}\varepsilon^{4}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\lesssim\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}.

Since
Q_{b}
is bounded on the central region, the same lemma and
Cauchy–Schwarz imply

\displaystyle e^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}\left|\iint Q_{b}\varepsilon^{3}\varphi^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\iint\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}|\varepsilon|^{3}\lesssim\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}.

Finally,
(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}
is bounded by Lemma
3.3
, and

e^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}\varphi\lesssim C(B)\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}
; hence

\displaystyle e^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}\left|\iint(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}\varepsilon^{3}\varphi\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim C(B)\iint\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}|\varepsilon|^{3}\lesssim C(B)\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}.

Collecting these estimates,

|R_{\mathrm{Vir}}(\varepsilon)|\lesssim C(B)\bigl(|b|+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\bigr)\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}.

For this fixed
B
, choose
\nu^{*}
small enough that the last bound
is at most
\delta_{0}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}
. We retain it as a global
error:

f_{1,1}^{\sim}\leq-\mu_{0}\iint_{|y_{1}|<B/2}(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+\frac{C}{\mu_{0}}e^{-B/(4\alpha_{1})}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}+\delta_{0}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}.

Now sum the three regional inequalities. Choose
\delta_{0}
and then

B
so that

\delta_{0}<\frac{1}{12}\min\{1/4,\kappa/2,\mu_{0}\},\qquad\frac{C}{\mu_{0}}e^{-B/(4\alpha_{1})}<\frac{1}{4}\min\{1/4,\kappa/2,\mu_{0}\}.

The three global errors and the last localized mass term are then
absorbed into the full dissipation. For a sufficiently small
\mu>0
,
this yields

f_{1,1}\leq-\mu\iint\Bigl[(\varepsilon_{y_{1}y_{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{y_{2}y_{2}}^{2})\psi_{B}^{\prime}+(\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\Bigr].

(5.39)

Step 2: Estimate of
f_{1,2}
.

Write

f_{1,2}=2\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\bigl(\mathcal{I}_{Q}+b\mathcal{I}_{P}\bigr),

where
\mathcal{I}_{Q}
and
\mathcal{I}_{P}
are obtained by replacing

\Lambda Q_{b}
by
\Lambda Q
and
\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)
, respectively.
Since
L\Lambda Q=-2Q
and
(\varepsilon,Q)=0
, we have

(L\varepsilon,\Lambda Q)=0
. Integrating the Laplacian terms by parts
and using

3Q^{2}\varepsilon-\psi_{B}N(\varepsilon)=(1-\psi_{B})N(\varepsilon)-\bigl[N(\varepsilon)-3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon\bigr]-3(Q_{b}^{2}-Q^{2})\varepsilon,

we obtain the exact identity

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{Q}={}

\displaystyle\iint\Bigl[-(\Lambda Q)_{y_{1}}\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon+(1-\psi_{B})\Delta(\Lambda Q)\varepsilon-\Lambda Q(1-\phi_{i,B})\varepsilon\Bigr]

\displaystyle+\iint\Lambda Q(1-\psi_{B})N(\varepsilon)-\iint\Lambda Q\bigl[N(\varepsilon)-3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon\bigr]-3\iint\Lambda Q(Q_{b}^{2}-Q^{2})\varepsilon.

We repeatedly use the weighted Cauchy–Schwarz inequality

\left|\iint F\varepsilon\right|\leq\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}\left(\iint\frac{F^{2}}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}\right)^{1/2}.

By Lemma
3.3
, (
5.23
), and
(
5.22
),

\left|\iint_{y_{1}<-B/2}(1-\psi_{B})\Delta(\Lambda Q)\varepsilon\right|\lesssim e^{-\frac{B}{2}[\sigma(M-1/2)+1/\alpha_{1}]}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2},

(5.40)

and similarly

\left|\iint(\Lambda Q)_{y_{1}}\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon\right|\lesssim e^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}.

Using
(\varepsilon,\varphi\Lambda Q)=0
, we write

\iint\Lambda Q(1-\phi_{i,B})\varepsilon=\iint\Lambda Q\bigl(1+\varphi e^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}-\phi_{i,B}\bigr)\varepsilon.

The bracket vanishes on
|y_{1}|<B/2
; on the left we use
(
5.14
), and on the right we use
Lemma
3.3
and the polynomial definition of
\phi_{i,B}
.
Cauchy–Schwarz therefore gives

\left|\iint\Lambda Q(1-\phi_{i,B})\varepsilon\right|\lesssim Be^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}.

(5.41)

For the nonlinear terms, Lemma
3.3
and the weight comparisons
imply
|\Lambda Q|\lesssim C(B)\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}
. Since

N(\varepsilon)-3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon=3Q_{b}\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{3}
,

\displaystyle\left|\iint\Lambda Q\bigl[N(\varepsilon)-3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon\bigr]\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim C(B)\iint\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\bigl(|Q_{b}|\varepsilon^{2}+|\varepsilon|^{3}\bigr).

The first integral is bounded by
C(B)\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}
.
For the second, Cauchy–Schwarz and Lemma
4.3
, applied
with
\theta=\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}
, give

\iint\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}|\varepsilon|^{3}\leq\left(\iint\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{4}\right)^{1/2}\lesssim\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}.

Thus, after retaining the explicit constant from the global comparison

|\Lambda Q|\lesssim Be^{B/(2\alpha_{1})}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}
, we obtain

\left|\iint\Lambda Q\bigl[N(\varepsilon)-3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon\bigr]\right|\lesssim Be^{B/(2\alpha_{1})}\bigl(\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}+\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}\bigr).

(5.42)

For the profile difference, weighted Cauchy–Schwarz gives

\displaystyle\left|\iint\Lambda Q(Q_{b}^{2}-Q^{2})\varepsilon\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}\left(\iint\frac{(\Lambda Q)^{2}(Q_{b}^{2}-Q^{2})^{2}}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}\right)^{1/2}.

The profile bounds give
|Q_{b}^{2}-Q^{2}|\lesssim|b|e^{-a|y_{2}|}
for
some
a>0
. The longitudinal exponential decay required in this
integral is supplied by
\Lambda Q
, through Lemma
3.3
.
Together with the lower bound for
\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}
, this gives

\left|\iint\Lambda Q(Q_{b}^{2}-Q^{2})\varepsilon\right|\lesssim|b|e^{B/(4\alpha_{1})}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}.

Finally,
1-\psi_{B}
is supported in
\{y_{1}<-B/2\}
. For the term
linear in
\varepsilon
, write

Q_{b}^{2}=Q^{2}+2b\chi_{b}PQ+b^{2}\chi_{b}^{2}P^{2}.

On this left half-plane, the profile estimates imply, for some
a>0
,

|Q|+|\Lambda Q|\leq Ce^{y_{1}/\alpha_{1}}e^{-a|y_{2}|},\qquad|\chi_{b}P|\leq Ce^{-a|y_{2}|},\qquad\frac{1}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}\leq\alpha_{1}e^{B/(2\alpha_{1})}e^{-y_{1}/\alpha_{1}}.

Weighted Cauchy–Schwarz therefore gives

\displaystyle\left|\iint\Lambda Q(1-\psi_{B})Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\left(e^{-B/\alpha_{1}}+|b|e^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}+b^{2}\right)\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}.

For the quadratic and cubic terms, the weight comparisons and
Lemma
4.3
yield

\left|\iint\Lambda Q(1-\psi_{B})\bigl(3Q_{b}\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{3}\bigr)\right|\lesssim C(B)\left(\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right).

Keeping the quadratic contribution at this order and combining these
bounds with the preceding estimates, we obtain

|\mathcal{I}_{Q}|\lesssim Be^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}+C(B)\Bigl(\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}+|b|\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\Bigr).

We next estimate
\mathcal{I}_{P}
. An integration by parts gives the exact
formula

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{P}={}

\displaystyle-\iint\Delta\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)\psi_{B}\varepsilon-\iint[\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)]_{y_{1}}\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon

\displaystyle+\iint\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)\phi_{i,B}\varepsilon-\iint\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)\psi_{B}N(\varepsilon).

On the support of
\chi_{b}
,

|\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)|+|\Delta\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)|+|[\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)]_{y_{1}}|\lesssim\begin{cases}(1+|y_{2}|)e^{-|y_{2}|/2},&-2|b|^{-\gamma}\leq y_{1}\leq 0,\\
(1+|y_{1}|+|y_{2}|)e^{-(|y_{1}|+|y_{2}|)/2},&y_{1}\geq 0.\end{cases}

For the first two linear terms, weighted Cauchy–Schwarz gives, for
example,

\displaystyle\left|\iint\Delta\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)\psi_{B}\varepsilon\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}\left(\iint\frac{|\Delta\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)|^{2}\psi_{B}^{2}}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}\right)^{1/2}.

On
[-2|b|^{-\gamma},-B/2-1]
, we use

\frac{\psi_{B}^{2}}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}\lesssim e^{\sigma(y_{1}+MB)},

(5.43)

so the integral in
y_{1}
is uniformly bounded. On

[-B/2-1,-B/2]
, the lower bound

\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\gtrsim e^{-B/\alpha_{1}}
is sufficient; on

[-B/2,B/2]
, we use the explicit formula

\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}=\alpha_{1}^{-1}e^{y_{1}/\alpha_{1}-B/(2\alpha_{1})}
; and on

[B/2,+\infty)
, the profile decays exponentially while

\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}
is bounded below by a polynomial weight. The term
containing
\psi_{B}^{\prime}
is handled in exactly the same four regions using
(
5.23
). Hence

\left|\iint\Delta\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)\psi_{B}\varepsilon\right|+\left|\iint[\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)]_{y_{1}}\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon\right|\lesssim C(B)\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}.

(5.44)

For the mass term, Cauchy–Schwarz gives

\displaystyle\left|\iint\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)\phi_{i,B}\varepsilon\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}\left(\iint\frac{|\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)|^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{2}}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}\right)^{1/2}.

On
(-2|b|^{-\gamma},-B/2-1]
, the profile is bounded in
y_{1}
and
exponentially localized in
y_{2}
, while (
5.16
)
gives

\frac{\phi_{i,B}^{2}}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}\lesssim e^{\varpi B}.

The interval has length
O(|b|^{-\gamma})
, and hence

\iint_{-2|b|^{-\gamma}<y_{1}<-B/2-1}\frac{|\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)|^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{2}}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}\lesssim C(B)|b|^{-\gamma}.

The transition, central, and right regions contribute only
C(B)
by
the same weight bounds used in (
5.44
). Therefore,

\left|\iint\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)\phi_{i,B}\varepsilon\right|\lesssim C(B)|b|^{-\gamma/2}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}.

(5.45)

It remains to estimate the nonlinear term. Using

N(\varepsilon)=3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon+3Q_{b}\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{3},

weighted Cauchy–Schwarz and the same support computation as above give

\left|\iint\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)\psi_{B}Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon\right|\lesssim C(B)|b|^{-\gamma/2}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}.

For the quadratic term, split
Q_{b}=Q+b\chi_{b}P
. The part containing

Q
is exponentially localized and is bounded by

C(B)\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}
, hence by

C(B)\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}
under (
H1
); the tail part
is bounded, using (
5.25
), by

C(B)|b|\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}
. Finally,
Lemma
4.3
, with
\theta=\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}
, gives

\left|\iint\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)\psi_{B}\varepsilon^{3}\right|\lesssim C(B)\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}.

Consequently,

\left|\iint\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)\psi_{B}N(\varepsilon)\right|\lesssim C(B)\left(|b|^{-\gamma/2}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}+(\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}+|b|)\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right),

and therefore

|\mathcal{I}_{P}|\lesssim C(B)\left(|b|^{-\gamma/2}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}+(\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}+|b|)\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right).

From (
4.23
) and (
5.29
),

\left|\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right|\lesssim e^{B/(4\alpha_{1})}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}+b^{2}+\min\left\{e^{B/(2\alpha_{1})}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i},\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\mathcal{N}_{i}\right\}.

(5.46)

The preceding estimates give

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}_{Q}+b\mathcal{I}_{P}|\lesssim{}

\displaystyle Be^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}+C(B)\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}

\displaystyle+C(B)|b|^{1-\gamma/2}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}+C(B)(\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}+|b|)\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}.

For the first three terms we use the version of
(
5.46
) containing

e^{B/(2\alpha_{1})}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}
;
for the last term we use the alternative containing

\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\mathcal{N}_{i}
. Thus, after multiplication,

\displaystyle|f_{1,2}|\lesssim{}

\displaystyle Be^{-B/(4\alpha_{1})}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}+C(B)\delta(\nu^{*})\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}

\displaystyle+C(B)b^{2}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}+C(B)\delta(\nu^{*})\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}.

Here we used (
H1
), (
5.28
), and the fact that

1-\gamma/2>0
. Fixing
B
large and then reducing
\nu^{*}
absorbs all
terms except
b^{2}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}
, for which

b^{2}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}\leq\frac{\mu}{200}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}+Cb^{4}.

Consequently, we obtain

|f_{1,2}|\leq\frac{\mu}{100}\iint(\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+Cb^{4}.

(5.47)

Remark 5.12
.

The orthogonality condition

(\varepsilon,\varphi\Lambda Q)=0
is essential in
(
5.41
). A direct estimate of

\iint\Lambda Q(1-\phi_{i,B})\varepsilon
would yield a term of order

e^{-B/(4\alpha_{1})}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}
. After multiplication by the
modulation coefficient, this produces an error of the size of the
energy rather than a small multiple of the dissipation. The exact
cancellation in the central region removes this leading contribution.

Step 3: Estimates of
f_{1,3}
and
f_{1,4}
.

Set

\mathcal{H}_{0}(\varepsilon):=-(\psi_{B}\varepsilon_{y_{1}})_{y_{1}}-(\psi_{B}\varepsilon_{y_{2}})_{y_{2}}+\phi_{i,B}\varepsilon.

We first verify the exact identity used for
f_{1,3}
. Since

\displaystyle(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}\bigl[N(\varepsilon)-3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon\bigr]+\varepsilon_{y_{1}}N(\varepsilon)=\frac{1}{4}\partial_{y_{1}}\Bigl[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{4}-Q_{b}^{4}-4Q_{b}^{3}\varepsilon\Bigr],

we have

\displaystyle-\iint\psi_{B}(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)_{y_{1}}N(\varepsilon)=\frac{1}{4}\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\Bigl[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{4}-Q_{b}^{4}-4Q_{b}^{3}\varepsilon\Bigr]-3\iint\psi_{B}Q_{b}^{2}(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}\varepsilon.

Moreover, using
(L\varepsilon,Q_{y_{1}})=0
,

\displaystyle\iint Q_{y_{1}}\mathcal{H}_{0}(\varepsilon)={}

\displaystyle\iint Q_{y_{1}}\Bigl[-\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}+(1-\psi_{B})\Delta\varepsilon-(1-\phi_{i,B})\varepsilon+3Q^{2}\varepsilon\Bigr].

Combining the last term with

-3\iint\psi_{B}Q_{b}^{2}(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}\varepsilon
gives

\displaystyle 3\iint Q^{2}Q_{y_{1}}\varepsilon-3\iint\psi_{B}Q_{b}^{2}(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}\varepsilon=3\iint(1-\psi_{B})Q^{2}Q_{y_{1}}\varepsilon-\iint\psi_{B}\varepsilon\,\partial_{y_{1}}(Q_{b}^{3}-Q^{3}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\iint(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)_{y_{1}}\mathcal{H}(\varepsilon)={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\Bigl[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{4}-Q_{b}^{4}-4Q_{b}^{3}\varepsilon\Bigr]

\displaystyle+\iint(b\chi_{b}P+\varepsilon)_{y_{1}}\mathcal{H}_{0}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+\iint Q_{y_{1}}\Bigl[-\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}+(1-\psi_{B})(\Delta\varepsilon+3Q^{2}\varepsilon)-(1-\phi_{i,B})\varepsilon\Bigr]

\displaystyle-\iint\psi_{B}\varepsilon\,\partial_{y_{1}}(Q_{b}^{3}-Q^{3}).

This displays both the term

3(1-\psi_{B})Q^{2}\varepsilon
and the negative sign in the last
integral.

For the first integral,
0\leq\psi_{B}^{\prime}\lesssim\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}
by
(
5.20
). Hence Lemma
4.3
, applied with

\theta=\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}
, and Lemma
3.3
give

\displaystyle\left|\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}\Bigl[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{4}-Q_{b}^{4}-4Q_{b}^{3}\varepsilon\Bigr]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}+\bigl(e^{-B/\alpha_{1}}+b^{2}\bigr)\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}.

For the profile-gradient term, integrating the second derivatives in

\mathcal{H}_{0}
onto
(b\chi_{b}P)_{y_{1}}
gives a sum of the same three
weighted pairings that appear in (
5.44
) and
(
5.45
). The factor
b
, together with the support length

O(|b|^{-\gamma})
, therefore yields

\left|\iint(b\chi_{b}P)_{y_{1}}\mathcal{H}_{0}(\varepsilon)\right|\lesssim C(B)|b|^{1-\gamma/2}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}.

For the pure radiation term, direct integration by parts gives the exact
identity

\iint\varepsilon_{y_{1}}\mathcal{H}_{0}(\varepsilon)=-\frac{1}{2}\iint\psi_{B}^{\prime}|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\iint\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2},

and hence its absolute value is bounded by

C\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}
.

We now treat the terms containing
Q_{y_{1}}
. First,

\displaystyle\left|\iint Q_{y_{1}}\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\iint\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint Q_{y_{1}}^{2}\frac{(\psi_{B}^{\prime})^{2}}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}^{1/2},

by Lemma
3.3
and (
5.23
). Next, after
two integrations by parts,

\iint Q_{y_{1}}(1-\psi_{B})\Delta\varepsilon=\iint\Delta\bigl[Q_{y_{1}}(1-\psi_{B})\bigr]\varepsilon,

which is controlled by weighted Cauchy–Schwarz, Lemma
3.3
,
and (
5.22
), exactly as in (
5.40
). The
additional term

3(1-\psi_{B})Q^{2}Q_{y_{1}}\varepsilon
has stronger exponential decay and
is estimated in the same way.

For the weight correction, the orthogonality condition

(\varepsilon,\varphi Q_{y_{1}})=0
gives

\iint Q_{y_{1}}(1-\phi_{i,B})\varepsilon=\iint Q_{y_{1}}\bigl(1+\varphi e^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}-\phi_{i,B}\bigr)\varepsilon.

The bracket vanishes on
|y_{1}|<B/2
, while the two exterior regions are
estimated as in (
5.41
); thus

\left|\iint Q_{y_{1}}(1-\phi_{i,B})\varepsilon\right|\lesssim Be^{-B/(2\alpha_{1})}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}.

Finally,

\partial_{y_{1}}(Q_{b}^{3}-Q^{3})=3\bigl[Q_{b}^{2}(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}-Q^{2}Q_{y_{1}}\bigr].

Lemma
3.3
and the definition of
Q_{b}
give the weighted bound

\iint\frac{\psi_{B}^{2}|\partial_{y_{1}}(Q_{b}^{3}-Q^{3})|^{2}}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}\lesssim C(B)b^{2},

and hence

\left|\iint\psi_{B}\varepsilon\,\partial_{y_{1}}(Q_{b}^{3}-Q^{3})\right|\lesssim C(B)|b|\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}.

Using the analogue of (
5.46
) for

|(x_{1})_{s}/\lambda-1|
, which follows from
(
4.23
), we conclude

f_{1,3}\leq\frac{\mu}{100}\iint(\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+Cb^{4}.

(5.48)

Since the weights are independent of
y_{2}
, the transverse identity is

\displaystyle\iint(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)_{y_{2}}\mathcal{H}(\varepsilon)={}

\displaystyle\iint(b\chi_{b}P+\varepsilon)_{y_{2}}\mathcal{H}_{0}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+\iint Q_{y_{2}}\Bigl[-\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}+(1-\psi_{B})(\Delta\varepsilon+3Q^{2}\varepsilon)-(1-\phi_{i,B})\varepsilon\Bigr]

\displaystyle-\iint\psi_{B}\varepsilon\,\partial_{y_{2}}(Q_{b}^{3}-Q^{3}).

Here

\iint\varepsilon_{y_{2}}\mathcal{H}_{0}(\varepsilon)=0
,

LQ_{y_{2}}=0
, and

(\varepsilon,\varphi Q_{y_{2}})=0
. The preceding estimates and the
modulation bound for
(x_{2})_{s}/\lambda
give

|f_{1,4}|\leq\frac{\mu}{100}\iint(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+Cb^{4}.

(5.49)

Step 4: Estimate of
f_{1,5}
.

Set

\zeta_{b}:=\chi_{b}+\gamma y_{1}\chi_{b}^{\prime}.

We first record the exact formula obtained after integrating the linear
terms by parts. Since
\psi_{B}
depends only on
y_{1}
,

\displaystyle\iint\zeta_{b}P\,\mathcal{H}(\varepsilon)={}

\displaystyle-\iint\Delta(\zeta_{b}P)\psi_{B}\varepsilon-\iint(\zeta_{b}P)_{y_{1}}\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon+\iint\zeta_{b}P\phi_{i,B}\varepsilon-\iint\zeta_{b}P\psi_{B}N(\varepsilon).

Indeed,

\displaystyle-\iint\zeta_{b}P(\psi_{B}\varepsilon_{y_{1}})_{y_{1}}

\displaystyle=-\iint\bigl[\psi_{B}(\zeta_{b}P)_{y_{1}}\bigr]_{y_{1}}\varepsilon,

\displaystyle-\iint\zeta_{b}P(\psi_{B}\varepsilon_{y_{2}})_{y_{2}}

\displaystyle=-\iint\psi_{B}(\zeta_{b}P)_{y_{2}y_{2}}\varepsilon.

Consequently,

\displaystyle f_{1,5}=-2b_{s}\Bigg[

\displaystyle-\iint\Delta(\zeta_{b}P)\psi_{B}\varepsilon-\iint(\zeta_{b}P)_{y_{1}}\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon+\iint\zeta_{b}P\phi_{i,B}\varepsilon-\iint\zeta_{b}P\psi_{B}N(\varepsilon)\Bigg].

For the three linear terms, weighted Cauchy–Schwarz gives

\displaystyle\left|\iint\Delta(\zeta_{b}P)\psi_{B}\varepsilon\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}\left(\iint\frac{|\Delta(\zeta_{b}P)|^{2}\psi_{B}^{2}}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}\right)^{1/2},

\displaystyle\left|\iint(\zeta_{b}P)_{y_{1}}\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}\left(\iint\frac{|(\zeta_{b}P)_{y_{1}}|^{2}(\psi_{B}^{\prime})^{2}}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}\right)^{1/2},

\displaystyle\left|\iint\zeta_{b}P\phi_{i,B}\varepsilon\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}\left(\iint\frac{|\zeta_{b}P|^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{2}}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}\right)^{1/2}.

On
[-2|b|^{-\gamma},-B/2]
, the functions

\zeta_{b}P
and their required derivatives are bounded and exponentially
localized in
y_{2}
. The estimates
(
5.43
), (
5.23
), and
(
5.17
) make the corresponding
y_{1}
-integrals uniformly
finite. On
[-B/2,+\infty)
, the conclusion follows from the defining
bounds for the weights and the exponential decay of
P
and its

y_{1}
-derivatives. Hence

\displaystyle\left|\iint\Delta(\zeta_{b}P)\psi_{B}\varepsilon\right|+\left|\iint(\zeta_{b}P)_{y_{1}}\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon\right|+\left|\iint\zeta_{b}P\phi_{i,B}\varepsilon\right|\lesssim C(B)\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}.

For the nonlinear term, expand

N(\varepsilon)=3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon+3Q_{b}\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{3}.

The term linear in
\varepsilon
is estimated by weighted
Cauchy–Schwarz:

\left|\iint\zeta_{b}P\psi_{B}Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon\right|\lesssim C(B)\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}.

For the quadratic term, split
Q_{b}=Q+b\chi_{b}P
. The part containing
Q

is exponentially localized and is bounded by

C(B)\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}
, hence by

C(B)\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}
under (
H1
). The tail part
is bounded by

C(B)|b|\iint\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2}\lesssim C(B)|b|\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i},

where we used (
5.25
). Finally,
Cauchy–Schwarz and Lemma
4.3
, applied with

\theta=\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}
, give

\displaystyle\iint|\zeta_{b}P|\psi_{B}|\varepsilon|^{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim C(B)\iint\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}|\varepsilon|^{3}\lesssim C(B)\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\iint\zeta_{b}P\psi_{B}N(\varepsilon)\right|\lesssim C(B)\left[\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}+(\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}+|b|)\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right].

By (
4.24
) and (
5.29
),

\displaystyle|b_{s}|\lesssim{}

\displaystyle e^{B/(2\alpha_{1})}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}+b^{2}+\min\left\{e^{B/(2\alpha_{1})}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i},\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\mathcal{N}_{i}\right\}.

Multiplying the last two estimates, all terms containing at least one
factor
\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}
,

\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}
, or
|b|
are bounded, by (
H1
), by a
small multiple of
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}
, except for

b^{2}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}
. For this term we use

b^{2}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}\leq\frac{\mu}{200}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}+Cb^{4}.

Thus

|f_{1,5}|\leq\frac{\mu}{100}\iint(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+Cb^{4}.

(5.50)

Step 5: Estimate of
f_{1,6}
.

Integrating the linear terms by parts gives the exact identity

\displaystyle f_{1,6}=2\Bigg[

\displaystyle-\iint\Delta\Psi_{b}\,\psi_{B}\varepsilon-\iint(\Psi_{b})_{y_{1}}\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon+\iint\Psi_{b}\phi_{i,B}\varepsilon-\iint\Psi_{b}\psi_{B}N(\varepsilon)\Bigg].

Weighted Cauchy–Schwarz yields

\displaystyle\left|\iint\Delta\Psi_{b}\,\psi_{B}\varepsilon\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}\left(\iint\frac{|\Delta\Psi_{b}|^{2}\psi_{B}^{2}}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}\right)^{1/2},

\displaystyle\left|\iint(\Psi_{b})_{y_{1}}\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}\left(\iint\frac{|(\Psi_{b})_{y_{1}}|^{2}(\psi_{B}^{\prime})^{2}}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}\right)^{1/2},

\displaystyle\left|\iint\Psi_{b}\phi_{i,B}\varepsilon\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}\left(\iint\frac{|\Psi_{b}|^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{2}}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}\right)^{1/2}.

Lemma
4.1
, together with
(
5.43
), (
5.23
), and
(
5.17
), implies

\iint\frac{|\Delta\Psi_{b}|^{2}\psi_{B}^{2}+|(\Psi_{b})_{y_{1}}|^{2}(\psi_{B}^{\prime})^{2}+|\Psi_{b}|^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{2}}{\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}}\lesssim C(B)b^{4}.

Indeed, the core part of
\Psi_{b}
is
O(b^{2})
. Its transition part is

O(|b|^{1+\gamma})
and is supported where

y_{1}\sim-|b|^{-\gamma}
; the exponential factors in
(
5.43
), (
5.23
), and
(
5.17
) then give

e^{-c|b|^{-\gamma}}\lesssim|b|^{m}
for every fixed
m>0
.
Consequently,

\displaystyle\left|\iint\Delta\Psi_{b}\,\psi_{B}\varepsilon\right|+\left|\iint(\Psi_{b})_{y_{1}}\psi_{B}^{\prime}\varepsilon\right|+\left|\iint\Psi_{b}\phi_{i,B}\varepsilon\right|\lesssim C(B)b^{2}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}.

For the nonlinear term, expand
N(\varepsilon)
as above. The part
linear in
\varepsilon
is controlled by the same weighted
Cauchy–Schwarz estimate and contributes

C(B)b^{2}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}
. For the quadratic and cubic
parts, the core component of
\Psi_{b}
is again
O(b^{2})
, whereas the
transition component is
O(|b|^{1+\gamma})
. Using
(
5.25
) and Lemma
4.3
with

\theta=\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\iint\Psi_{b}\psi_{B}N(\varepsilon)\right|\lesssim{}

\displaystyle C(B)b^{2}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}+C(B)|b|^{1+\gamma}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}.

Finally,

b^{2}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}\leq\frac{\mu}{200}\mathcal{N}_{i,\mathrm{loc}}+Cb^{4},

while the second term is a small multiple of

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}
by (
H1
). Therefore,

|f_{1,6}|\leq\frac{\mu}{100}\iint(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+Cb^{4}.

(5.51)

Combining (
5.39
), (
5.47
), (
5.48
), (
5.49
),
(
5.50
), and (
5.51
), and decreasing
\mu
once more, yields

f_{1}\leq-\mu\iint(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+Cb^{4}.

5.5
Proof of Lemma
5.8
:

It remains to estimate the scaling-drift contribution
f_{2}
. Recall

f_{2}=2\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\iint\Lambda\varepsilon\Big(-(\psi_{B}\varepsilon_{y_{1}})_{y_{1}}-(\psi_{B}\varepsilon_{y_{2}})_{y_{2}}+\varepsilon\phi_{i,B}-\psi_{B}[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q_{b}^{3}]\Big)-j\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\mathcal{F}_{i}.

Integration by parts gives

2\iint\Lambda\varepsilon([-(\psi_{B}\varepsilon_{y_{1}})]_{y_{1}}+[-(\psi_{B}\varepsilon_{y_{2}})]_{y_{2}})=\iint(2\psi_{B}-y_{1}\psi_{B}^{\prime})(\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2}),

and

2\iint\Lambda\varepsilon\phi_{i,B}\varepsilon=-\iint y_{1}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2}.

We have the following identity for
p>1
and
\Lambda^{(p)}=\frac{2}{p-1}+y\cdot\nabla
:

\begin{split}\iint\Lambda^{(p)}\varepsilon\psi_{B}[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{p}-Q_{b}^{p}]&=\frac{1}{p+1}\iint\Big(\frac{4}{p-1}\psi_{B}-y_{1}\psi_{B}^{\prime}\Big)\\
&\qquad\times[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{p+1}-Q_{b}^{p+1}-(p+1)Q_{b}^{p}\varepsilon]\\
&\quad-\iint\psi_{B}\Lambda^{(p)}Q_{b}[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{p}-Q_{b}^{p}-pQ_{b}^{p-1}\varepsilon].\end{split}

This identity allows us to rewrite
f_{2}
as

\begin{split}f_{2}&=\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\iint[(2-j)\psi_{B}-y_{1}\psi_{B}^{\prime}](\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2})-\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\iint[j\phi_{i,B}+y_{1}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}]\varepsilon^{2}\\
&-\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\iint\frac{1}{2}[(2-j)\psi_{B}-y_{1}\psi_{B}^{\prime}][(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{4}-Q_{b}^{4}-4Q_{b}^{3}\varepsilon]\\
&+\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\iint 2\psi_{B}\Lambda Q_{b}[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q_{b}^{3}-3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon].\end{split}

1. Estimates of Non-Problematic Terms.

We begin by bounding the terms in regions where the weights do not exhibit dangerous polynomial growth.
Using the bound on the weights (
5.25
) and (
5.27
), specifically
|(2-j)\psi_{B}-y_{1}\psi_{B}^{\prime}|\lesssim e^{\varpi B}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime},
we have:

\Big|\iint[(2-j)\psi_{B}-y_{1}\psi_{B}^{\prime}](\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2})\Big|\leq C(B)\iint(\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2})\phi_{i,B}^{\prime},

and for the nonlinear term involving the potential we employ the weighted Sobolev Lemma
4.3
to get

\displaystyle\Big|\iint[(2-j)\psi_{B}-

\displaystyle y_{1}\psi_{B}^{\prime}][(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{4}-Q_{b}^{4}-4Q_{b}^{3}\varepsilon]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C(B)\iint\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{4}+C(B)\iint\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(B)\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}+C(B)\|Q_{b}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\iint\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}.

Similarly, for the interaction with
\Lambda Q_{b}
:

\displaystyle\Big|\iint 2\psi_{B}\Lambda Q_{b}

\displaystyle[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q_{b}^{3}-3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon]\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Lambda Q_{b}\|_{L^{\infty}}C(B)\iint|\varepsilon|^{3}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+\|Q_{b}\Lambda Q_{b}\|_{L^{\infty}}e^{\frac{B}{\alpha_{1}}}\iint\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C(B)\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}+C(B)\iint\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}.

2. Analysis of the Weight
j\phi_{i,B}+y_{1}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}
.

We first observe that
j\phi_{i,B}+y_{1}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}=B(j+i)\frac{y_{1}^{i}}{B^{i}}
for
y_{1}>B.

•

For
y_{1}<B/2
: We bound the term using (
5.15
), interpolation, and the fact that
\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}
decays exponentially on
(-\infty,0]
:

\displaystyle\iint_{\{y_{1}<\frac{B}{2}\}}|j\phi_{i,B}+y_{1}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}|\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim C(B)\iint_{\{y_{1}<\frac{B}{2}\}}\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\Big(\iint_{\{y_{1}<\frac{B}{2}\}}y_{1}^{4}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\iint\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\Big)^{\frac{3}{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C(B)\iint\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\iint\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2}\Big)^{\frac{3}{4}}.

•

For
B/2<y_{1}<B
: Since the domain is compact, then

\iint_{\{B/2<y_{1}<B\}}|j\phi_{i,B}+y_{1}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}|\varepsilon^{2}\lesssim C(B)\iint\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2}.

3. The Problematic Region.

On
\{y_{1}>B\}
, the remaining contribution is

\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}(i+j)\iint_{y_{1}>B}\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{i,B}.

Here the weight grows polynomially as
y_{1}^{i}
and could overwhelm the
dissipation. We control this term using the a priori conditions
(
H2
) and (
H3
), together with interpolation. Denote

\mathcal{N}_{i}^{>}=\iint_{y_{1}>B}y_{1}^{i}\varepsilon^{2},\qquad\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}^{>}=\iint_{y_{1}>B}y_{1}^{i-1}\varepsilon^{2},\qquad\mathcal{N}_{K_{i}}^{>}=\iint_{y_{1}>B}y_{1}^{K_{i}}\varepsilon^{2}.

Using (
5.46
), we have

\big|\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\Big|\lesssim|b|+e^{\frac{B}{4\alpha_{1}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Set
m=K_{i}-i
. Interpolation gives

\mathcal{N}_{i}^{>}\leq(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}^{>})^{\frac{m}{m+1}}(\mathcal{N}_{K_{i}}^{>})^{\frac{1}{m+1}},

(5.52)

therefore

|b|\mathcal{N}_{i}^{>}\leq\frac{\mu}{200B^{i}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}^{>}+C_{\mu,B,i}|b|^{m+1}\mathcal{N}_{K_{i}}^{>}.

Assumptions (
H1
)–(
H3
) then imply

\displaystyle|b|^{m+1}\mathcal{N}_{K_{i}}^{>}

\displaystyle\leq 2|b|^{m+1}(1+\lambda^{-K_{i}})

\displaystyle\leq 2\bigl(|b|^{m+1}+\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\frac{K_{i}}{c}}|b|^{m+1-\frac{K_{i}}{c}}\bigr)\lesssim|b|^{4},

since by the definition of
K_{i}>\frac{c(i+3)}{c-1}
, we have
m+1,m+1-\frac{K_{i}}{c}\geq 4,
hence

|b|\mathcal{N}_{i}^{>}\leq\frac{\mu}{200B^{i}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}^{>}+C_{B}b^{4}\leq\frac{\mu}{200B}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}+C_{B}b^{4}.

From (
5.52
), we have

(\mathcal{N}_{i}^{>})^{2}\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}^{>}[(\mathcal{N}_{i}^{>})^{m-1}\mathcal{N}_{K_{i}}^{>}]^{\frac{1}{m}},

thus

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{N}_{i}^{>}\leq\frac{\mu}{400Be^{\frac{B}{4\alpha_{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}+C_{\mu,B,i}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}^{>}[(\mathcal{N}_{i}^{>})^{m-1}\mathcal{N}_{K_{i}}^{>}]^{\frac{1}{m}}.

From (
H1
) and (
H2
), we have that

\mathcal{N}_{i}^{>}\lesssim B^{i-1}\mathcal{N}_{i}\leq B^{i-1}\min\{\nu^{*},\mathcal{C}_{i}\lambda^{c}\},

(5.53)

For
\lambda\geq 1,
using (
H3
) and (
5.53
) we have

(\mathcal{N}_{i}^{>})^{m-1}\mathcal{N}_{K_{i}}^{>}\leq 4B^{(i-1)(m-1)}(\nu^{*})^{m-1}.

For
0<\lambda\leq 1,
we have

\sup_{0<\lambda\leq 1}\min\{\nu^{*},\mathcal{C}_{i}\lambda^{c}\}^{m-1}\lambda^{-K_{i}}\leq\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\frac{K_{i}}{c}}(\nu^{*})^{m-1-\frac{K_{i}}{c}}.

We conclude

\sup_{s\in[0,s_{0}]}(\mathcal{N}_{i}^{>})^{m-1}\mathcal{N}_{K_{i}}^{>}\leq 4B^{(i-1)(m-1)}\bigl[(\nu^{*})^{m-1}+\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\frac{K_{i}}{c}}(\nu^{*})^{m-1-\frac{K_{i}}{c}}\bigr],

with
m-1-\frac{K_{i}}{c}>0
from the definition of
K_{i}.

Consequently, after reducing
\nu^{*}
depending on the fixed
\mathcal{C}_{i}
,

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{N}_{i}^{>}\leq\frac{\mu}{400Be^{\frac{B}{4\alpha_{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}+\frac{\mu}{400e^{\frac{B}{\alpha_{1}}}B^{i}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}^{>}\leq\frac{\mu}{200Be^{\frac{B}{4\alpha_{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}.

Here we used
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}^{>}\leq B^{i-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}/i
, which follows from

\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}=iB^{1-i}y_{1}^{i-1}
on
y_{1}>B
.
We conclude

(i+j)\Bigg|\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\Bigg|\iint_{y_{1}>B}\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{i,B}\leq\frac{\mu(i+j)}{50B}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}+C_{B}b^{4}\leq\frac{\mu}{100}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}+C_{B}b^{4}.

Combining all the estimates for the terms in
f_{2}
, using the bound (
4.8
) and the a priori estimate (
H1
) so
|\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}|\lesssim|b|+\mathcal{N}_{i,loc}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}
and
\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\delta(\nu^{*}),
by Young’s inequality, we finalize the estimate

\begin{split}f_{2}\leq&\delta(\nu^{*})|b|\Big(\iint\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}\Big)^{\frac{3}{4}}+Cb^{4}+\frac{\mu}{100}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\\
&\leq\frac{\mu}{4}\iint(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\phi_{i,B}^{\prime}+Cb^{4}.\end{split}

The proof of Lemma
5.8
is complete.

Chapter 6
Closing the Bootstrap

6.1
Initial Data Decomposition

We first discuss whether the solution of the equation (
2.3
) remains inside the
L^{2}-
modulated tube if we start with small initial data. Take universal constant
\alpha_{0}\ll\alpha^{*}\ll\nu_{0},
with
\nu_{0}
taken as in Lemma
4.2
. Fix a natural number
n\geq 2
and let
u_{0}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\cap\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{0}}^{(n)}
and
u(t)
be solution associated with this initial data on the maximal existence interval
[0,T).
Define the distance to the soliton manifold

d(v)=\inf_{\lambda_{0}>0,x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}}\left\lVert v-\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}}Q\Bigg(\frac{\cdot-x_{1}}{\lambda_{0}},\frac{\cdot-x_{2}}{\lambda_{0}}\Bigg)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Since
d(u_{0})<\alpha_{0}<\alpha^{*},
thus
u_{0}\in\mathcal{T}_{\alpha^{*}}.
Now define
t^{*}=\sup\{t\in[0,T):u(t^{\prime})\in\mathcal{T}_{\alpha^{*}}\text{ for all }0\leq t^{\prime}<t\}
and we observe that
t^{*}\geq 0.
We know that the distance function
d
is
1-
Lipschitz in
L^{2},
i.e.
|d(v)-d(w)|\leq\|v-w\|_{L^{2}}.
By local well-posedness, there exists
\tau>0
such that on
[0,\tau],

\|u(t)-u_{0}\|_{L^{2}}<\frac{\alpha^{*}-\alpha_{0}}{2}.
Therefore, on
[0,\tau],

d(u(t))<\alpha^{*},
hence
t^{*}>0.
Denote the corresponding
s-
variable of
t^{*}
by
s(t^{*})=s^{*}.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
u_{0}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\cap\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{0}}^{(n)}
and consequently
u_{0}\in\mathcal{T}_{\alpha^{*}}.
After applying decomposition from Proposition
4.2
, we have

\|\varepsilon(0)\|_{L^{2}}+|b(0)|+|1-\lambda(0)|+\mathcal{N}_{m}(0)\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}),\text{ for }1\leq m\leq n-1,

\iint_{y_{1}>0}y_{1}^{n}\varepsilon^{2}(0)\leq 1+\delta(\alpha_{0}).

Proof.

First, write
u_{0}=Q+\eta_{0},
with
\|\eta_{0}\|_{H^{1}}<\alpha_{0}
and
\iint_{y_{1}>0}y_{1}^{n}\eta_{0}^{2}(y_{1},y_{2})<1.
It means there exists a decomposition of
u_{0}
with
\lambda^{\sharp}(0)=1,x^{\sharp}_{1}(0)=0,x^{\sharp}_{2}(0)=0.
By applying Proposition
4.2
with
\nu,\alpha\sim\alpha_{0}
, we obtain a decomposition
(\lambda,x_{1},x_{2},b,\varepsilon)
with

\|\varepsilon(0)\|_{H^{1}}+|b(0)|+|1-\lambda(0)|+|(x_{1}(0),x_{2}(0))|\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}).

Denote
y=(y_{1},y_{2}),b(0)=b_{0},\lambda(0)=\lambda_{0},x_{0}=(x_{1,0},x_{2,0})=(x_{1}(0),x_{2}(0)),\varepsilon(0,y)=\varepsilon_{0}(y).

Write
\varepsilon_{0}(y)=a(y)+r(y)
with
a(y)=\lambda_{0}\eta_{0}(\lambda_{0}y+x_{0}),r(y)=\lambda_{0}Q(\lambda_{0}y+x_{0})-Q_{b_{0}}(y)
and
z=(z_{1},z_{2})=\lambda_{0}y+x_{0}.
First, we note that

\iint_{y_{1}>0}y_{1}^{n}a^{2}(y)dy=\lambda_{0}^{-n}\iint_{z_{1}>x_{1,0}}(z_{1}-x_{1,0})^{n}\eta_{0}(z)^{2}dz.

For
p,q\in\mathbb{R}
, write
p_{+}=\max\{p,0\}
. The elementary
inequality

\bigl|(p-q)_{+}^{n}-p_{+}^{n}\bigr|\leq C_{n}|q|\bigl(p_{+}^{n-1}+|q|^{n-1}\bigr)

gives

\displaystyle\iint_{y_{1}>0}y_{1}^{n}a^{2}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle=\lambda_{0}^{-n}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(z_{1}-x_{1,0})_{+}^{n}\eta_{0}^{2}(z)\,dz

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{0}^{-n}\bigg[\iint z_{1,+}^{n}\eta_{0}^{2}+C_{n}|x_{1,0}|\iint z_{1,+}^{n-1}\eta_{0}^{2}+C_{n}|x_{1,0}|^{n}\|\eta_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\bigg].

By interpolation,

\iint z_{1,+}^{n-1}\eta_{0}^{2}\leq\left(\iint z_{1,+}^{n}\eta_{0}^{2}\right)^{\frac{n-1}{n}}\|\eta_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2}{n}}\lesssim\alpha_{0}^{\frac{2}{n}}.

Since

\iint z_{1,+}^{n}\eta_{0}^{2}<1,\quad|\lambda_{0}-1|+|x_{1,0}|\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}),

we obtain

\iint_{y_{1}>0}y_{1}^{n}a^{2}(y)\,dy\leq 1+\delta(\alpha_{0}).

Furthermore, by the exponential decay of
Q
and of
P
on

\{y_{1}>0\}
,

\iint_{y_{1}>0}y_{1}^{n}r^{2}(y)\,dy\lesssim|\lambda_{0}-1|^{2}+|x_{0}|^{2}+|b_{0}|^{2}\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}).

Therefore, using
\varepsilon_{0}=a+r
,

\displaystyle\iint_{y_{1}>0}y_{1}^{n}\varepsilon_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\iint_{y_{1}>0}y_{1}^{n}a^{2}+2\left(\iint_{y_{1}>0}y_{1}^{n}a^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{y_{1}>0}y_{1}^{n}r^{2}\right)^{1/2}+\iint_{y_{1}>0}y_{1}^{n}r^{2}

(6.1)

\displaystyle\leq 1+\delta(\alpha_{0}),

after redefining the generic function
\delta(\alpha_{0})
.
Now, since
0\leq\psi_{B}\leq 1,
we have

\iint|\nabla\varepsilon_{0}|^{2}\psi_{B}\lesssim\|\varepsilon_{0}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}).

On
y_{1}<0,
we have
\iint_{y_{1}<0}\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\phi_{m,B}\lesssim\|\varepsilon_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}).

On
y_{1}>0,
we have
\phi_{m,B}(y_{1})\lesssim 1+y_{1}^{m},
then

\iint_{y_{1}>0}\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\phi_{m,B}\lesssim\|\varepsilon_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\iint_{y_{1}>0}y_{1}^{m}\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

By Hölder’s inequality and (
6.1
), for
m\leq n-1
we have

\iint_{y_{1}>0}y_{1}^{m}\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\leq\Big(\iint_{y_{1}>0}y_{1}^{n}\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{m}{n}}\Big(\iint\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\Big)^{1-\frac{m}{n}}\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}).

We conclude that
\mathcal{N}_{m}(0)\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}).

∎

6.2
Monotonicity of Energy Functional

Fix
i\geq 1
and
\mathcal{C}_{i}>1
, and recall
K_{i}=\lfloor\frac{c(i+3)}{c-1}\rfloor+1.
We suppose that
u_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{0}}^{(K_{i})}.
We define the a priori conditions

\displaystyle|b(s)|+\mathcal{N}_{i}(s)+\|\varepsilon(s)\|_{L^{2}}\leq\nu^{*},

(
H1
i
)

\displaystyle\frac{|b(s)|+\mathcal{N}_{i}(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}\leq\mathcal{C}_{i},

(
H2
i
)

\displaystyle\iint_{y_{1}>0}\varepsilon^{2}(s)y_{1}^{K_{i}}\leq 2\Big(1+\lambda^{-K_{i}}(s)\Big).

(
H3
i
)

with
\nu^{*}
chosen small enough for the applications of
Proposition
5.6
at the finitely many levels
m\leq i

as explained below.

We define the maximal time
s^{**}_{i}
compatible with the trapped regime as

s^{**}_{i}=\sup\Big\{0\leq s<s^{*},\,u\text{ satisfies }\textbf{H1}_{i}-\textbf{H2}_{i}-\textbf{H3}_{i}\text{ on }[0,s]\Big\}.

As a corollary of Lemma
6.1
,
s_{i}^{**}>0.
For the
t-
variable, we define
t_{i}^{**}>0
such that
s(t_{i}^{**})=s_{i}^{**}.

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
c
be the constant defined in (
2.2
). Then the
following estimates hold.

i)

Dispersive bounds.

For every
1\leq m\leq i
and
0\leq s_{1}\leq s_{2}<s_{i}^{**}
,

\mathcal{N}_{m}(s_{2})+\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\iint\bigl(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\bigr)\phi_{m,B}^{\prime}\,ds\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{m}(s_{1})+|b^{3}(s_{1})|+|b^{3}(s_{2})|.

(6.2)

Moreover, for every
0<\alpha<3
, there exists
\nu_{\alpha}>0

such that, if
0<\nu^{*}\leq\nu_{\alpha}
in (
H1i
), then

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{N}_{m}(s_{2})}{\lambda^{\alpha c}(s_{2})}

\displaystyle+\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{\iint\bigl(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\bigr)\phi_{m,B}^{\prime}}{\lambda^{\alpha c}(s)}\,ds

(6.3)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{N}_{m}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{\alpha c}(s_{1})}+\frac{|b^{3}(s_{1})|}{\lambda^{\alpha c}(s_{1})}+\frac{|b^{3}(s_{2})|}{\lambda^{\alpha c}(s_{2})}.

ii)

Control of the scaling dynamics.

Let

\tilde{\lambda}(s)=\lambda(s)(1-J_{1}(s)),

where
J_{1}
is defined in Lemma
4.5
.
If
i\geq 2
, then, on
[0,s_{i}^{**})
,

\left|\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{s}}{\tilde{\lambda}}+b\right|\lesssim b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{3,\mathrm{loc}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}.

(6.4)

iii)

Control of the dynamics of
b
.

For
0\leq s_{1}\leq s_{2}<s_{i}^{**}
,

\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}b^{2}(s)\,ds\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{1}(s_{1})+|b(s_{1})|+|b(s_{2})|.

(6.5)

If, in addition,
i\geq 3
, then

\left|\frac{b(s_{2})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{2})}-\frac{b(s_{1})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{1})}\right|\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{1})}+\frac{b^{2}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{1})}+\frac{b^{2}(s_{2})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{2})}.

(6.6)

Proof.

i).

For
m\leq i
, the weight comparisons give

\mathcal{N}_{m}\leq C_{m,i}\mathcal{N}_{i}
, with
C_{i,i}=1
,
so (
H2i
)
supplies (
H2
) at level
m
with the fixed constant

\mathcal{C}_{m}=\max\{1,C_{m,i}\}\mathcal{C}_{i}>1
.
Choose the common
\nu^{*}
so that
\max\{1,C_{m,i}\}\nu^{*}

lies below the level-
m
smallness threshold for every
m\leq i
.
Then (
H1
) holds at these levels, while
Hölder’s inequality transfers (
H3i
) with the same
coefficient
2
, since
K_{m}\leq K_{i}
and
\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\leq 1
.
Choosing
B
sufficiently large and
\nu^{*}
sufficiently small for
this finite collection, we may apply
Proposition
5.6
at each level
m\leq i
.

By (
4.8
), (
5.29
), and
(
H1i
), for every
0<\epsilon<c
there exists

C_{\epsilon}>0
such that, after choosing
\nu^{*}
sufficiently small
depending on
\epsilon
,

(c-\epsilon)b^{2}\leq-b_{s}+C_{\epsilon}\mathcal{N}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}+C_{1}\nu^{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}.

(6.7)

Integrating (
5.32
) with
j=0
and using the
coercivity (
5.33
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{m}(s_{2})

\displaystyle+\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\iint\bigl(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\bigr)\phi_{m,B}^{\prime}\,ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{F}_{m}(s_{2})+\mu\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\iint\bigl(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\bigr)\phi_{m,B}^{\prime}\,ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{F}_{m}(s_{1})+\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}b^{4}(s)\,ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{m}(s_{1})+\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}b^{4}(s)\,ds.

Taking
\epsilon=c-1
in (
6.7
),
multiplying by
b^{2}
, and using (
H1i
), we find

\displaystyle\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}b^{4}(s)\,ds

\displaystyle\leq-\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}b^{2}b_{s}\,ds+C_{\epsilon}(\nu^{*})^{2}\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\mathcal{N}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}\,ds+C_{1}(\nu^{*})^{3}\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\left[-\frac{b^{3}(s)}{3}\right]_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}+C_{2}(\nu^{*})^{2}\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\bigl(\mathcal{N}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\bigr)\,ds

\displaystyle\leq|b^{3}(s_{1})|+|b^{3}(s_{2})|+C_{3}(\nu^{*})^{2}\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\iint\bigl(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\bigr)\phi_{m,B}^{\prime}\,ds.

Choosing
\nu^{*}
sufficiently small allows the last term to be
absorbed, which proves (
6.2
).

For (
6.3
), integrate
(
5.32
) with
j=\alpha c
and again use
(
5.33
):

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{N}_{m}(s_{2})}{\lambda^{\alpha c}(s_{2})}

\displaystyle+\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{\iint\bigl(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\bigr)\phi_{m,B}^{\prime}}{\lambda^{\alpha c}(s)}\,ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{F}_{m}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{\alpha c}(s_{1})}+\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{b^{4}(s)}{\lambda^{\alpha c}(s)}\,ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{N}_{m}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{\alpha c}(s_{1})}+\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{b^{4}(s)}{\lambda^{\alpha c}(s)}\,ds.

Set

\epsilon_{\alpha}=\frac{c}{2}\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{3}\right).

Multiplying (
6.7
) by

b^{2}/\lambda^{\alpha c}
and integrating by parts gives

\displaystyle(c-\epsilon_{\alpha})\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{b^{4}}{\lambda^{\alpha c}}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\left[-\frac{b^{3}}{3\lambda^{\alpha c}}\right]_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}-\frac{\alpha c}{3}\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{b^{3}}{\lambda^{\alpha c}}\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\,ds

\displaystyle\quad+\widetilde{C}_{\epsilon_{\alpha}}(\nu^{*})^{2}\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}}{\lambda^{\alpha c}}\,ds.

Since

\displaystyle-\frac{\alpha c}{3}\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{b^{3}}{\lambda^{\alpha c}}\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\,ds

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha c}{3}\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{b^{4}}{\lambda^{\alpha c}}\,ds

\displaystyle-\frac{\alpha c}{3}\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{b^{3}}{\lambda^{\alpha c}}\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\,ds,

the modulation estimate (
4.8
) and
(
H1i
) yield

\displaystyle(c-\epsilon_{\alpha})\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{b^{4}}{\lambda^{\alpha c}}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{b^{3}(s_{1})}{3\lambda^{\alpha c}(s_{1})}\right|+\left|\frac{b^{3}(s_{2})}{3\lambda^{\alpha c}(s_{2})}\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\alpha c}{3}\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{b^{4}}{\lambda^{\alpha c}}\,ds+C_{4}(\nu^{*})^{1/2}\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{b^{4}}{\lambda^{\alpha c}}\,ds

\displaystyle\quad+C_{4}(\nu^{*})^{1/2}\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{\iint\bigl(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\bigr)\phi_{m,B}^{\prime}}{\lambda^{\alpha c}}\,ds.

Consequently,

\displaystyle c\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{3}\right)-\frac{C_{4}(\nu^{*})^{1/2}}{c}\right]\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{b^{4}}{\lambda^{\alpha c}}\,ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|b^{3}(s_{1})|}{\lambda^{\alpha c}(s_{1})}+\frac{|b^{3}(s_{2})|}{\lambda^{\alpha c}(s_{2})}

\displaystyle\quad+\delta(\nu^{*})\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{\iint\bigl(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\bigr)\phi_{m,B}^{\prime}}{\lambda^{\alpha c}}\,ds.

For
\nu^{*}\leq\nu_{\alpha}
sufficiently small, the coefficient on
the left is positive. Substitution into the preceding energy
estimate and absorption of the last term prove
(
6.3
). Notice that, as
\nu^{*}\to 0
,

\alpha
may be chosen arbitrarily close to
3
; below it is enough
to fix one
\alpha>2
.

ii).

By the definition of
J_{1}
,

|J_{1}|\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{3,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}\lesssim\delta(\nu^{*}).

Set

E_{J_{1}}:=(J_{1})_{s}+\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}J_{1}-\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right).

Lemma
4.5
gives

|E_{J_{1}}|\lesssim b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{3,\mathrm{loc}}+\delta(\nu^{*})\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}.

Since
|J_{1}|\ll 1
,

\left|\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{s}}{\tilde{\lambda}}+b\right|=\left|\frac{E_{J_{1}}+bJ_{1}}{1-J_{1}}\right|\lesssim b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{3,\mathrm{loc}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\lesssim b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{2},

which proves (
6.4
). In particular,

\tilde{\lambda}(s)>0
on
[0,s_{i}^{**})
for every
i\geq 2
.

iii).

Integrating (
6.7
) on
[s_{1},s_{2}]
and
using (
6.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}b^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq-\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}b_{s}\,ds+C\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\mathcal{N}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}\,ds+\delta(\nu^{*})\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\,ds

(6.8)

\displaystyle\lesssim|b(s_{1})|+|b(s_{2})|+\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\iint\bigl(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\bigr)\phi_{1,B}^{\prime}\,ds

\displaystyle\lesssim|b(s_{1})|+|b(s_{2})|+\mathcal{N}_{1}(s_{1}),

which proves (
6.5
).

For the second estimate, recall
J
from
Lemma
4.6
. By (
4.29
),

|J(s)|\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{3,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}(s)\leq(\nu^{*})^{1/2}.

Hence (
4.30
) and
(
4.8
) imply

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d}{ds}\left(\frac{b}{\lambda^{c}}e^{J}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=e^{J}\left|\frac{d}{ds}\left(\frac{b}{\lambda^{c}}\right)+\frac{b}{\lambda^{c}}J_{s}\right|

(6.9)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\frac{b}{\lambda^{c}}\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\mathcal{N}_{3,\mathrm{loc}}^{1/2}\right|+\frac{|b|^{3}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}+\mathcal{N}_{3,\mathrm{loc}}}{\lambda^{c}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|b|^{3}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{3}}{\lambda^{c}}.

Let
s,s^{\prime}\in[s_{1},s_{2}]
. Integrating (
6.9
), using
(
6.3
) with
\alpha=1
, together with

|1-e^{J}|\lesssim|J|\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{2}^{1/2},

(
6.8
), (
6.2
), and
(
H1i
), gives

\displaystyle\left|\frac{b(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}-\frac{b(s^{\prime})}{\lambda^{c}(s^{\prime})}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{s}^{s^{\prime}}\frac{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{3}(s^{\prime\prime})}{\lambda^{c}(s^{\prime\prime})}\,ds^{\prime\prime}+\int_{s}^{s^{\prime}}\frac{|b^{3}(s^{\prime\prime})|}{\lambda^{c}(s^{\prime\prime})}\,ds^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{|b(s)|}{\lambda^{c}(s)}|1-e^{J(s)}|+\frac{|b(s^{\prime})|}{\lambda^{c}(s^{\prime})}|1-e^{J(s^{\prime})}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{1})}+\delta(\nu^{*})\sup_{\tau\in[s_{1},s_{2}]}\frac{|b(\tau)|}{\lambda^{c}(\tau)}.

Taking the supremum and minimum over
[s_{1},s_{2}]
, and then
absorbing the last term, yields

\sup_{[s_{1},s_{2}]}\frac{|b|}{\lambda^{c}}\lesssim\min_{[s_{1},s_{2}]}\frac{|b|}{\lambda^{c}}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{1})}.

Applying the preceding estimate with
s=s_{1}
and
s^{\prime}=s_{2}
, and
retaining the endpoint bounds produced by
|1-e^{J}|
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\frac{b(s_{2})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{2})}-\frac{b(s_{1})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{1})}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{1})}+\frac{|b^{3}(s_{1})|}{\lambda^{c}(s_{1})}+\frac{|b^{3}(s_{2})|}{\lambda^{c}(s_{2})}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\min_{[s_{1},s_{2}]}\frac{|b|}{\lambda^{c}}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{1})}\right)\Bigl(|b(s_{1})|+|b(s_{2})|+\mathcal{N}_{2}(s_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 122.34685pt+\mathcal{N}_{2}^{1/2}(s_{1})+\bigl(\mathcal{N}_{2}(s_{1})+|b^{3}(s_{1})|+|b^{3}(s_{2})|\bigr)^{1/2}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{1})}+\frac{b^{2}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{1})}+\frac{b^{2}(s_{2})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{2})},

which proves (
6.6
).
∎

As a corollary, we have from (
5.28
) and (
6.2
), as
0\leq s_{1}\leq s_{2}<s^{**}_{i},
for
i\geq 2
and
1\leq m\leq i-1,

\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\mathcal{N}_{m}(s)ds\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{m+1}(s_{1})+|b^{3}(s_{1})|+|b^{3}(s_{2})|,

(6.10)

and from (
6.3
),

\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{m}(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}ds\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{N}_{m+1}(s_{1})+|b^{3}(s_{1})|}{\lambda^{c}(s_{1})}+\frac{|b^{3}(s_{2})|}{\lambda^{c}(s_{2})}.

(6.11)

6.3
Bootstrap argument

In this section, we prove the propagation of the a priori estimates until the exit time from the modulated tube. From now on, we choose parameters
\alpha_{0}\ll\alpha^{*}\ll\nu^{*}\ll 1
and
u_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{0}}^{(K_{i})}
for
i\geq 4.

Lemma 6.3
.

For every
S<s^{**}_{i}
and every
0\leq r\leq s\leq S,
we have

\tilde{\lambda}(r)\leq 4\max\{1,\tilde{\lambda}(s)\}.

Consequently, there exists
C_{0}>4
such that

\lambda(r)\leq C_{0}\max\{1,\lambda(s)\}.

Proof.

First, we assign specific names to the constants appearing in Proposition
6.2
. Let
L_{1},L_{2},L_{3}>0
such that

\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3}(s_{2})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{2})}\leq L_{1}\Bigg(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{1})}+\frac{|b^{3}(s_{1})|}{\lambda^{c}(s_{1})}+\frac{|b^{3}(s_{2})|}{\lambda^{c}(s_{2})}\Bigg),

\left|\frac{(\tilde{\lambda})_{s}}{\tilde{\lambda}}+b\right|\leq L_{2}\left(b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{2}\right),

\left|\frac{b(s_{2})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{2})}-\frac{b(s_{1})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{1})}\right|\leq L_{3}\Bigg(\frac{b^{2}(s_{2})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{2})}+\frac{b^{2}(s_{1})+\mathcal{N}_{3}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{1})}\Bigg).

Now choose a new constant
\tilde{L}
large enough such that

\tilde{L}\geq 8\max\{L_{3},L_{1}L_{2}\}.

(6.12)

We choose
\nu^{*}
small enough such that
L_{j}\nu^{*}\leq\frac{1}{4}
for
j=1,2,3
and
\tilde{L}\nu^{*}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Case 1.
Assume

|b(s)|\leq\tilde{L}(\mathcal{N}_{3}(s)+b^{2}(s))\text{ for all }0\leq s\leq S.

Since
\nu^{*}
is sufficiently small, we obtain

|b(s)|\leq 2\tilde{L}\mathcal{N}_{3}(s).

We have from (
6.4
)

\Bigg|\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{s}}{\tilde{\lambda}}(s)\Bigg|\lesssim|b(s)|+\mathcal{N}_{2}(s)\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{3}(s),

hence by (
6.2
), (
H1
) and Lemma
6.1
,

\Bigg|\log\Big(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}(s)}{\tilde{\lambda}(0)}\Big)\Bigg|\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{4}(0)+|b^{3}(s)|+|b^{3}(0)|\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0})+\delta(\nu^{*})\leq\log 2.

(6.13)

Again, from Lemma
6.1
,
|\tilde{\lambda}(0)-1|\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}),
together with (
6.13
),

\frac{1}{4}\leq\tilde{\lambda}(s)\leq 4.

Case 2.
Define
\hat{s}=\inf\{s\in[0,S]:|b(s)|\geq\tilde{L}(\mathcal{N}_{3}(s)+b^{2}(s))\}.
If the inequality already holds at
s=0,
then
\hat{s}=0.
By continuity, at
\hat{s}
we have

\mathcal{N}_{3}(\hat{s})+b^{2}(\hat{s})\leq\frac{|b(\hat{s})|}{\tilde{L}}.

On
[0,\hat{s}),
the argument of Case 1 applies. Now, for
s\in[\hat{s},S],
we have

\left|\frac{b(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}-\frac{b(\hat{s})}{\lambda^{c}(\hat{s})}\right|\leq L_{3}\Bigg(\frac{b^{2}(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}+\frac{b^{2}(\hat{s})+\mathcal{N}_{3}(\hat{s})}{\lambda^{c}(\hat{s})}\Bigg)\leq L_{3}\nu^{*}\left|\frac{b(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}\right|+\frac{L_{3}}{\tilde{L}}\left|\frac{b(\hat{s})}{\lambda^{c}(\hat{s})}\right|,

and therefore we get

\left|\frac{b(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}-\frac{b(\hat{s})}{\lambda^{c}(\hat{s})}\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}\left|\frac{b(\hat{s})}{\lambda^{c}(\hat{s})}\right|.

Since
\lambda>0,
we obtain
\operatorname{sign}b(s)=\operatorname{sign}b(\hat{s}),
for all
s\in[\hat{s},S).

Moreover, we have that

\frac{1}{2}\left|\frac{b(\hat{s})}{\lambda^{c}(\hat{s})}\right|\leq\left|\frac{b(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}\right|\leq\frac{3}{2}\left|\frac{b(\hat{s})}{\lambda^{c}(\hat{s})}\right|.

The stronger preceding inequality also gives

\left|\frac{b(\hat{s})}{\lambda^{c}(\hat{s})}\right|\leq\frac{1+L_{3}\nu^{*}}{1-L_{3}/\tilde{L}}\left|\frac{b(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}\right|\leq\frac{10}{7}\left|\frac{b(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}\right|\leq\frac{3}{2}\left|\frac{b(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}\right|.

From (
6.3
), we have

\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3}(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}\leq L_{1}\Bigg(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3}(\hat{s})+|b^{3}(\hat{s})|}{\lambda^{c}(\hat{s})}+\frac{|b^{3}(s)|}{\lambda^{c}(s)}\Bigg)\leq\Big(\frac{3L_{1}}{2\tilde{L}}+L_{1}(\nu^{*})^{2}\Big)\left|\frac{b(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}\right|,

therefore, from (
6.4
) and (
6.12
),

\Bigg|\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{s}}{\tilde{\lambda}}+b\Bigg|\leq L_{2}\Big(\frac{3L_{1}}{2\tilde{L}}+L_{1}(\nu^{*})^{2}+\nu^{*}\Big)|b(s)|\leq\frac{1}{2}|b|.

This in turn implies that if
b(\hat{s})>0,
then
\tilde{\lambda}
decreases after
\hat{s}
and if
b(\hat{s})<0,
then
\tilde{\lambda}
increases after
\hat{s}.
Together with
\tilde{\lambda}\sim 1
before
\hat{s},
this proves that

\tilde{\lambda}(r)\leq 4\max\{1,\tilde{\lambda}(s)\},\text{ for }0\leq r\leq s\leq S.

Since
\tilde{\lambda}\sim\lambda,
we obtain the corresponding estimate for
\lambda
.

∎

We introduce a dynamic control of the tail similar to the result of Lemma 3.7 in
[
38
]
. We prove it for completeness.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
N\geq 1
. Define the
C^{3}
function

\phi_{N}=\begin{cases}0,&\quad y_{1}\leq 0,\\
y_{1}^{N}&\quad y_{1}\geq 1,\end{cases}

with
\phi_{N}^{\prime}\geq 0.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
5.6
, we have

\frac{1}{\lambda^{N}}\frac{d}{ds}\left\{\lambda^{N}\iint\phi_{N}\varepsilon^{2}\right\}\lesssim b^{4}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}.

Proof.

We use the modulated flow equation (
4.2
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{ds}\iint\phi_{N}\varepsilon^{2}={}

\displaystyle\iint\phi_{N}\varepsilon\left[\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\Lambda\varepsilon+\bigl(-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon-(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}+Q_{b}^{3}\bigr)_{y_{1}}\right]

\displaystyle+\iint\phi_{N}\varepsilon\left[\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\Lambda Q_{b}+\left(\frac{(x_{1})_{s}}{\lambda}-1\right)(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)_{y_{1}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 156.49014pt\left.+\frac{(x_{2})_{s}}{\lambda}(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)_{y_{2}}+\Phi_{b}+\Psi_{b}\right].

where
\Psi_{b}=[(-\Delta Q_{b}+Q_{b}-Q_{b}^{3})_{y_{1}}-b\Lambda Q_{b}]
and
\Phi_{b}=-b_{s}(\chi_{b}+\gamma y_{1}(\chi_{b})_{y_{1}})P.

For the linear term, we compute by integration by parts and using the fact
y_{1}\phi_{N}^{\prime}=N\phi_{N}
for
y_{1}\geq 1
and
\phi_{N}^{\prime\prime\prime}\ll\phi_{N}^{\prime}
for
y_{1}
large enough to derive the bound

\displaystyle\iint\phi_{N}\varepsilon\left[\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\Lambda\varepsilon+(-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon)_{y_{1}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\iint y_{1}\phi_{N}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\iint\phi_{N}^{\prime}\bigl(3\varepsilon_{y_{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{y_{2}}^{2}\bigr)+\frac{1}{2}\iint(\phi_{N}^{\prime\prime\prime}-\phi_{N}^{\prime})\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq-\frac{N}{2}\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\iint\phi_{N}\varepsilon^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\iint\phi_{N}^{\prime}\bigl(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\bigr)+C_{N}\mathcal{N}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}.

Recall that
|Q|+|Q_{y_{1}}|+|P|+|P_{y_{1}}|\lesssim e^{-\frac{|y_{1}|}{\alpha_{1}}-C_{\alpha_{1}}|y_{2}|},
for
y_{1}>0,
and Lemma

4.4

\text{Mod}(s):=\left|\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right|+\left|\frac{(x_{1})_{s}}{\lambda}-1\right|+\left|\frac{(x_{2})_{s}}{\lambda}\right|\lesssim b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{1,loc}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\nu^{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}.

In addition, (
5.29
) gives

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{i}
, so that
(
4.8
) and (
H1
) imply the uniform bound

\operatorname{Mod}(s)\lesssim\nu^{*}
. Also,
