\Big|\iint\phi_{N}\varepsilon\Lambda Q_{b}\Big|+\Big|\iint\phi_{N}\varepsilon(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}\Big|+\Big|\iint\phi_{N}\varepsilon(Q_{b})_{y_{2}}\Big|\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{1,loc}^{\frac{1}{2}},

and
|\iint\phi_{N}\varepsilon\varepsilon_{y_{1}}|=|\frac{1}{2}\iint\phi_{N}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2}|.

Therefore

\text{Mod}(s)\Big(\mathcal{N}_{1,loc}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\iint\phi_{N}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2}\Big)\lesssim b^{4}+\mathcal{N}_{1,loc}+\delta(\nu^{*})\Big(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}+\iint\phi_{N}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2}\Big).

From (
4.1
), we obtain

\Big|\iint\phi_{N}\varepsilon\Psi_{b}\Big|\lesssim b^{4}+\mathcal{N}_{1,loc}.

From Lemma
4.4
,

\Big|\iint\phi_{N}\varepsilon\Phi_{b}\Big|\lesssim|b_{s}|\mathcal{N}_{1,loc}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim b^{4}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}.

For the nonlinear term, using that
|Q_{b}|+|(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}|\lesssim e^{-\frac{|y_{1}|}{2}}
for
y_{1}>0,
we bound

\left|\iint\phi_{N}\varepsilon[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q_{b}^{3}]_{y_{1}}\right|\lesssim\iint_{y_{1}>0}e^{-\frac{|y_{1}|}{2}}(\varepsilon^{2}+|\varepsilon|^{3})+\iint\phi^{\prime}_{N}\varepsilon^{4},

\lesssim\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}+\iint\phi^{\prime}_{N}\varepsilon^{4},

where in the last inequality we used the weighted Sobolev Lemma
4.3
.
For the last term, set

\theta_{N}(y_{1})=\phi_{N}^{\prime}(y_{1})+\omega(y_{1}),\qquad\omega(y_{1})=\operatorname{sech}(y_{1}/\alpha_{1}).

The function
\omega
is positive and has bounded logarithmic derivative.
On
[0,1]
it has a positive minimum, whereas
\phi_{N}^{\prime\prime}
is bounded;
for
y_{1}\geq 1
the logarithmic derivative of the polynomial

\phi_{N}^{\prime}
is bounded, and for
y_{1}\leq 0
we have
\phi_{N}^{\prime}=0
.
It follows that
|\theta_{N}^{\prime}|\leq C_{N}\theta_{N}
. Moreover,

\omega\leq C(B)\phi_{1,B}^{\prime}
: this follows from the exponential
lower bound on the left, positivity on bounded intervals, and the
constant positive slope on the right. Lemma
4.3
,
applied to the weight
\theta_{N}
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, therefore gives

\iint\phi_{N}^{\prime}\varepsilon^{4}\lesssim_{N,B}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\left(\iint\phi_{N}^{\prime}(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\right).

Hence, using
\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\leq\nu^{*},
we obtain

\left|\iint\phi_{N}\varepsilon[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q_{b}^{3}]_{y_{1}}\right|\lesssim\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}+\delta(\nu^{*})\iint\phi^{\prime}_{N}(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}).

These estimates prove the claimed differential inequality.

∎

Lemma 6.5
.

Using the notation above, we have
t_{i}^{**}=t^{*}
.

Proof.

Take
t<t_{i}^{**}.
Since
u(t)\in\mathcal{T}_{\alpha^{*}}
, by Lemma
4.2
with
\nu=\alpha^{*},
we get that
|b(t)|\lesssim\delta(\alpha^{*})\ll\nu^{*}
. As
\left|\iint u_{0}^{2}-\iint Q^{2}\right|\leq\alpha_{0}\ll\nu^{*}
by the choice of the initial data, then the conservation of mass (
4.34
) gives

\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim|b|+\left|\iint u_{0}^{2}-\iint Q^{2}\right|\lesssim\delta(\alpha^{*})+\alpha_{0}\ll\frac{(\nu^{*})^{2}}{4}.

From Lemma
6.1
, we have
\mathcal{N}_{i}(0)\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}).
We conclude by (
6.2
),

\mathcal{N}_{i}(s)\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{i}(0)+|b^{3}(0)|+|b^{3}(s)|\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0})+\delta(\alpha^{*})\ll\nu^{*}.

So we improved (
H1i
) on
[0,t_{i}^{**}].

From (
6.6
), Lemma
6.1
and the improved bound
|b(s)|\lesssim\delta(\alpha^{*})
, then

\frac{|b(s)|}{\lambda^{c}(s)}\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}).

From (
6.3
) and Lemma
6.1
, we obtain

\frac{\mathcal{N}_{i}(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}),

so, after choosing
\alpha_{0}
sufficiently small for the fixed

\mathcal{C}_{i}
, we obtain

\frac{|b(s)|+\mathcal{N}_{i}(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}<\frac{\mathcal{C}_{i}}{2},

which improves (
H2i
) on
[0,t^{**}].

Since
\phi_{K_{i}}
and
y_{1,+}^{K_{i}}
agree outside
[0,1]
and
have bounded difference there, integrating Lemma
6.4
gives

\displaystyle\iint y_{1,+}^{K_{i}}\varepsilon^{2}(s)\leq{}

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{K_{i}}(0)}{\lambda^{K_{i}}(s)}\iint y_{1,+}^{K_{i}}\varepsilon^{2}(0)+C\|\varepsilon(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\frac{\lambda^{K_{i}}(0)}{\lambda^{K_{i}}(s)}\|\varepsilon(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{s}\left(\frac{\lambda(\tau)}{\lambda(s)}\right)^{K_{i}}\bigl(b^{4}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\bigr)(\tau)\,d\tau.

By Lemma
6.3
,

\left(\frac{\lambda(\tau)}{\lambda(s)}\right)^{K_{i}}\leq C_{0}^{K_{i}}\left(1+\lambda(s)^{-K_{i}}\right).

Since
|b|\ll 1
, (
6.8
) gives

\int_{0}^{s}b^{4}\,ds^{\prime}\lesssim|b(s)|+|b(0)|+\mathcal{N}_{1}(0)\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0})+\delta(\alpha^{*}).

Likewise, (
6.2
) yields

\int_{0}^{s}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\,ds^{\prime}\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{1}(0)+|b(0)|^{3}+|b(s)|^{3}\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0})+\delta(\alpha^{*}).

The improved mass bound controls the
L^{2}
norm
at time
s
, and Lemma
6.1
controls its initial
counterpart. Together with
|\lambda(0)-1|\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0})

and the initial moment bound, these estimates imply

\iint y_{1,+}^{K_{i}}\varepsilon^{2}(s)\leq\bigl[1+\delta(\alpha_{0})+\delta(\alpha^{*})\bigr]\bigl[1+\lambda(s)^{-K_{i}}\bigr].

Here the fixed constants, including
C_{0}^{K_{i}}
, are included in the
smallness choices. Taking
\alpha^{*}
and then
\alpha_{0}
sufficiently
small makes the coefficient strictly less than
2
, improving
(
H3i
) on
[0,t_{i}^{**})
.
By improving (
H1i
), (
H2i
), (
H3i
) on
[0,t_{i}^{**}]
, we deduce that
t_{i}^{**}=t^{*}
by a continuity argument.
∎

Chapter 7
Rigidity near the soliton

We combine the modulation estimates and monotonicity properties of the
preceding chapters to classify the dynamics of solutions starting near
the soliton manifold.

Theorem
E.2
gives

\frac{21}{13}<c<\frac{5}{3}
, hence
K_{5}=21
. We write

\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{0}}:=\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{0}}^{(21)}
, fix

\mathcal{C}_{5}>1
, and use the following a priori conditions:

\displaystyle|b(s)|+\mathcal{N}_{5}(s)+\|\varepsilon(s)\|_{L^{2}}\leq\nu^{*},

(
H1’
)

\displaystyle\frac{|b(s)|+\mathcal{N}_{5}(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}\leq\mathcal{C}_{5},

(
H2’
)

\displaystyle\iint_{y_{1}>0}\varepsilon^{2}(s)y_{1}^{21}\leq 2\big(1+\lambda^{-21}(s)\big).

(
H3’
)

with
\nu^{*}
defined as in Proposition
5.6
. Recall
t^{*}
to be the exit time of the tube
\mathcal{T}_{\alpha^{*}}.

Theorem 7.1
.

There exist constants
0<\alpha_{0}^{*}\ll\alpha^{*}\ll\nu^{*}
, depending on the fixed
\mathcal{C}_{5}
, such that the following holds. Let
u_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{0}}
with
0<\alpha_{0}<\alpha_{0}^{*}
; then
u(t)
satisfies the assumptions (
H1’
)–(
H2’
)–(
H3’
) on
[0,t^{*})
.

Furthermore, the following trichotomy holds:

(Asymptotic Stability)
Suppose
t^{*}=T=+\infty
. There exists
\lambda_{\infty}
satisfying
|\lambda_{\infty}-1|\leq\delta(\alpha_{0})
,
x_{2,\infty}\in\mathbb{R}
, and
x_{1}(t)\in C^{1}
such that

\lambda_{\infty}u(t,\lambda_{\infty}\cdot+x_{1}(t),\lambda_{\infty}\cdot+x_{2,\infty})\rightarrow Q\quad\mbox{in }H^{1}_{loc}\mbox{ as }t\rightarrow+\infty.

Moreover,

\begin{split}&\mathcal{N}_{5}(t)\rightarrow 0,\quad b(t)\rightarrow 0\quad\text{as }t\rightarrow+\infty,\\
&\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\lambda(t)=\lambda_{\infty},\quad x_{1}(t)=\frac{t}{\lambda_{\infty}^{2}}(1+o_{t\rightarrow\infty}(1)),\quad\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}x_{2}(t)=x_{2,\infty}\in\mathbb{R}.\end{split}

Furthermore, there exists
C^{*}>0
such that
|b(t)|\lesssim C^{*}\mathcal{N}_{3}(t)
for all
t\geq 0
.

(Stable Blow-Up)
Suppose
t^{*}=T<+\infty
. There exists
0<\ell_{0}<\delta(\alpha_{0})
such that

\lim_{t\rightarrow T}\frac{\lambda(t)}{(T-t)^{\frac{1}{3-c}}}=\ell_{0},\quad\lim_{t\rightarrow T}\frac{b(t)}{(T-t)^{\frac{c}{3-c}}}=\frac{\ell_{0}^{3}}{3-c},\quad\lim_{t\rightarrow T}(T-t)^{\frac{c-1}{3-c}}x_{1}(t)=\frac{3-c}{c-1}\cdot\frac{1}{\ell_{0}^{2}},

\text{ and }\lim_{t\rightarrow T}x_{2}(t)=x_{2,\infty},\quad\text{for some }x_{2,\infty}\in\mathbb{R}.

Moreover, the following error bounds hold:

\|\nabla\varepsilon(t)\|_{L^{2}}\asymp\lambda^{\frac{c}{2}}(t),\quad\|\varepsilon(t)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}).

(Exit of Tube)
Suppose
t^{*}<T
. We have

\inf_{\lambda_{1}>0,x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}}\left\|u(t^{*},\cdot,\cdot)-\frac{1}{\lambda_{1}}Q\left(\frac{\cdot-x_{1}}{\lambda_{1}},\frac{\cdot-x_{2}}{\lambda_{1}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}=\alpha^{*}.

In addition,

\frac{(\alpha^{*})^{\frac{2}{c}}}{\delta(\alpha_{0})}\lesssim\lambda(t^{*}),\quad b(t^{*})\lesssim-(\alpha^{*})^{2}.

Remark 7.2
.

Since
c>1
by Proposition
D.1
, the stable blow-up
asymptotics imply
x_{1}(t)\to+\infty
as
t\uparrow T
.

Since
K_{5}=21,
by Lemma
6.5
we have
t_{5}^{**}=t^{*},
proving the first part of Theorem
7.1
. From now on, denote
t^{**}\coloneqq t_{5}^{**}>0,
and
s^{**}\coloneqq s_{5}^{**}=s(t_{5}^{**})=s(t^{*})\coloneqq s^{*}>0
.

We first record an elementary convergence criterion.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
m\in(0,+\infty]
and let
f\in AC_{\mathrm{loc}}([0,m))
. If

f^{\prime}\in L^{1}([0,m))
, then

\lim_{t\uparrow m}f(t)=f(0)+\int_{0}^{m}f^{\prime}(\tau)\,d\tau

exists and is finite.

Lemma
6.5
gives
t^{**}=t^{*}
, and therefore

s^{**}=s^{*}
. Applying (
6.6
) with

s_{1}=0
and
s_{2}=s<s^{*}
, and using (
H1’
), we obtain

\frac{|b(s)|}{\lambda^{c}(s)}\lesssim\frac{|b(0)|}{\lambda^{c}(0)}(1+|b(0)|)+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3}(0)}{\lambda^{c}(0)}.

Hence Lemma
6.1
gives

\limsup_{s\uparrow s^{*}}\frac{|b(s)|}{\lambda^{c}(s)}\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}).

Together with (
6.9
), (
6.5
), (
6.11
) and
(
H1’
), this yields

\int_{0}^{s^{*}}\left|\frac{d}{ds}\left(\frac{b}{\lambda^{c}}e^{J(s)}\right)\right|\,ds\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}).

Therefore, by Lemma
7.3
,

\frac{b(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}e^{J(s)}\longrightarrow c_{0}\in\mathbb{R}\qquad\text{as }s\uparrow s^{*},\qquad|c_{0}|\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}).

(7.1)

If
c_{0}\neq 0
, choose
s_{c}<s^{*}
so that

\left|\frac{b(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}e^{J(s)}-c_{0}\right|\leq\frac{|c_{0}|}{2},\qquad s_{c}\leq s<s^{*}.

7.1
Exit Case and the characterization
c_{0}<0

Exit implies
c_{0}<0

Assume that
t^{*}<T
. By continuity of the distance to the soliton
manifold and the definition of
t^{*}
,

\displaystyle(\alpha^{*})^{2}

\displaystyle=\inf_{\lambda_{1}>0,\,a_{1},a_{2}\in\mathbb{R}}\left\|u(t^{*})-\frac{1}{\lambda_{1}}Q\left(\frac{x_{1}-a_{1}}{\lambda_{1}},\frac{x_{2}-a_{2}}{\lambda_{1}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{x_{1}x_{2}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim|b(t^{*})|^{2-\gamma}+\|\varepsilon(t^{*})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

By (
4.34
) and

u_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{0}}
,

(\alpha^{*})^{2}\lesssim|b(t^{*})|+\alpha_{0}.

Since
\alpha_{0}\ll\alpha^{*}
,

(\alpha^{*})^{2}\lesssim|b(t^{*})|.

(7.2)

We claim that
b(t^{*})<0
. Suppose otherwise. Then
(
7.2
) gives
b(t^{*})>0
. Define

s_{0}^{*}=\begin{cases}0,&\text{if }b(s)>0\text{ on }[0,s^{*}],\\[2.84526pt]
\displaystyle\sup\{s<s^{*}:b(s)=0\},&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Thus
b\geq 0
on
[s_{0}^{*},s^{*}]
. If
s_{0}^{*}=0
, then

|\lambda(s_{0}^{*})-1|\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0})
. If

s_{0}^{*}>0
, then
b(s_{0}^{*})=0
. Applying
(
6.6
) with
s_{1}=s
and
s_{2}=s_{0}^{*}
, and
absorbing the
b^{2}(s)
-term by (
H1’
), gives

|b(s)|\leq C^{*}\mathcal{N}_{3}(s),\qquad 0\leq s\leq s_{0}^{*}.

(7.3)

Hence, by (
6.4
), (
7.3
), and
(
6.10
),

\int_{0}^{s_{0}^{*}}\left|\frac{(\tilde{\lambda})_{s}}{\tilde{\lambda}}\right|\,ds\lesssim\int_{0}^{s_{0}^{*}}\mathcal{N}_{3}(s)\,ds\lesssim\delta(\nu^{*}).

Using Lemma
6.1
and
|J_{1}|\ll 1
, we obtain

|\lambda(s_{0}^{*})-1|\lesssim\delta(\nu^{*}).

Next, (
6.6
) with
s_{1}=0
and

s_{2}=s\in[s_{0}^{*},s^{*}]
, together with (
H1’
), yields

\frac{|b(s)|}{\lambda^{c}(s)}\lesssim\frac{|b(0)|}{\lambda^{c}(0)}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3}(0)}{\lambda^{c}(0)}\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}).

(7.4)

Since
b\geq 0
on
[s_{0}^{*},s^{*}]
, (
6.4
)
gives

\frac{(\tilde{\lambda})_{s}}{\tilde{\lambda}}\leq-b(1-Cb)+C\mathcal{N}_{2}\leq C\mathcal{N}_{2}.

Integrating and using (
6.10
),

\log\frac{\tilde{\lambda}(s)}{\tilde{\lambda}(s_{0}^{*})}\leq\delta(\nu^{*}),

so that

\tilde{\lambda}(s)\leq 2\tilde{\lambda}(s_{0}^{*})\leq 3,\qquad s_{0}^{*}\leq s\leq s^{*}.

Together with
|J_{1}|\ll 1
and (
7.4
), this gives

|b(t^{*})|\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0})
, contradicting
(
7.2
). Therefore
b(t^{*})<0
.

Since the modulation parameters extend continuously to
t=t^{*}
,
(
7.1
) gives

c_{0}=\frac{b(t^{*})}{\lambda^{c}(t^{*})}e^{J(s^{*})}<0.

Moreover, (
7.2
) yields

b(t^{*})\lesssim-(\alpha^{*})^{2}.

Finally, (
6.3
) and (
H1’
) imply

\frac{|b(s)|}{\lambda^{c}(s)}\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}),\qquad 0\leq s\leq s^{*},

and hence, after redefining the generic function
\delta
,

\frac{(\alpha^{*})^{2/c}}{\delta(\alpha_{0})}\lesssim\lambda(t^{*}).

c_{0}<0
implies Exit

Assume that
c_{0}<0
and, by contradiction, that
t^{*}=T
. By
increasing
s_{c}
if necessary and using
|J|\ll 1
in
(
7.1
), we may assume

\frac{5c_{0}}{3}\leq\frac{b(t)}{\lambda^{c}(t)}\leq\frac{c_{0}}{3}<0,\qquad t_{c}\leq t<T,

(7.5)

where
s(t_{c})=s_{c}
.

Since
b<0
, (
6.4
) gives

\frac{(\tilde{\lambda})_{s}}{\tilde{\lambda}}\geq-b(1+Cb)-C\mathcal{N}_{2}\geq-C\mathcal{N}_{2}.

Thus, for
s_{c}\leq s_{1}\leq s_{2}<s(T)
,

\log\frac{\tilde{\lambda}(s_{2})}{\tilde{\lambda}(s_{1})}\geq-C\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\mathcal{N}_{2}(s)\,ds\geq-\delta(\nu^{*}).

Using
|J_{1}|\ll 1
, we obtain

\lambda(s_{2})\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda(s_{1}),\qquad s_{c}\leq s_{1}\leq s_{2}<s(T).

(7.6)

The identity
\tilde{\lambda}=\lambda(1-J_{1})
, the relation

ds/dt=\lambda^{-3}
, and (
6.4
) give

\left|\tilde{\lambda}^{2-c}(\tilde{\lambda})_{t}+\frac{b}{\lambda^{c}}\right|\lesssim\frac{b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{2}+|b||J_{1}|}{\lambda^{c}}.

Indeed,

\frac{1}{\lambda^{c}}\left(\frac{(\tilde{\lambda})_{s}}{\tilde{\lambda}}+b\right)=\frac{\tilde{\lambda}^{2-c}(\tilde{\lambda})_{t}}{(1-J_{1})^{3-c}}+\frac{b}{\lambda^{c}}.

By (
7.6
) and (
6.10
),

\int_{t_{c}}^{t}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{2}(t^{\prime})}{\lambda^{c}(t^{\prime})}\,dt^{\prime}=\int_{s_{c}}^{s(t)}\lambda^{3-c}(s^{\prime})\mathcal{N}_{2}(s^{\prime})\,ds^{\prime}\lesssim\delta(\nu^{*})\tilde{\lambda}^{3-c}(t).

Integrating the preceding differential estimate, using
(
7.5
), and absorbing this last term gives

\tilde{\lambda}^{3-c}(t)\geq\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\lambda}^{3-c}(t_{c})+\tilde{\kappa}|c_{0}|(t-t_{c})

for some
\tilde{\kappa}>0
. The same integrated identity gives

\sup_{t_{c}\leq t<T}\lambda(t)<+\infty
whenever
T<+\infty
.
Thus, if
T<+\infty
, (
7.6
) shows that

\lambda
is also bounded below on
[t_{c},T)
; then
(
7.5
),
(
4.34
), and (
4.35
) give a
uniform
H^{1}
-bound for
u(t)
, contradicting the maximality of

T
. If
T=+\infty
, the lower bound gives

\lambda(t)\to+\infty
, and (
7.5
) then implies

|b(t)|\gtrsim|c_{0}|\lambda^{c}(t)\to+\infty,

contradicting (
H1’
). Hence
t^{*}<T
.

We have proved

t^{*}<T\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad c_{0}<0.

7.2
Blow-Up – the case
c_{0}>0

Assume
c_{0}>0
. The preceding equivalence excludes Exit, so

t^{*}=T
. Increasing
s_{c}
, if necessary, we may assume

0<\frac{c_{0}}{3}\leq\frac{b(t)}{\lambda^{c}(t)}\leq\frac{5c_{0}}{3},\qquad t_{c}\leq t<T.

Since
b>0
, (
6.4
) gives

\frac{(\tilde{\lambda})_{s}}{\tilde{\lambda}}\leq-b(1-Cb)+C\mathcal{N}_{2}\leq C\mathcal{N}_{2}.

Consequently,

\lambda(s_{2})\leq 2\lambda(s_{1}),\qquad s_{c}\leq s_{1}\leq s_{2}<s(T).

(7.7)

As above,

\left|\tilde{\lambda}^{2-c}(\tilde{\lambda})_{t}+\frac{b}{\lambda^{c}}\right|\lesssim\frac{b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{2}+|b||J_{1}|}{\lambda^{c}}.

Moreover, (
7.7
) and
(
6.10
) give

\int_{t_{c}}^{t}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{2}(t^{\prime})}{\lambda^{c}(t^{\prime})}\,dt^{\prime}=\int_{s_{c}}^{s(t)}\lambda^{3-c}(s^{\prime})\mathcal{N}_{2}(s^{\prime})\,ds^{\prime}\lesssim\delta(\nu^{*})\lambda^{3-c}(t_{c}).

Integrating the differential estimate and decreasing
\nu^{*}
, we
obtain the signed upper bound

\tilde{\lambda}^{3-c}(t)\leq 2\tilde{\lambda}^{3-c}(t_{c})-\frac{3-c}{4}c_{0}(t-t_{c}).

If
T=+\infty
, the right-hand side becomes negative in finite time,
whereas the left-hand side is positive. Hence
T<+\infty
.

We claim that
\lambda(t)\to 0
as
t\uparrow T
. Otherwise,
(
7.7
) would give a positive lower bound for

\lambda
on
[t_{c},T)
. Since
b
,
\mathcal{N}_{5}
, and

\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}
remain controlled in the tube, while
(
4.35
) controls
\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}
,
the
H^{1}
-norm of
u(t)
would remain bounded, contradicting the
maximality of
T
. Thus

\lambda(t)\to 0,\qquad b(t)\to 0\qquad\text{as }t\uparrow T.

Fix
0<\eta<1
for which (
6.3
) is
available. For
s_{c}\leq s<s(T)
,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{N}_{5}(s)}{\lambda^{(3-\eta)c}(s)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{N}_{5}(s_{c})}{\lambda^{(3-\eta)c}(s_{c})}+\frac{b^{3}(s)}{\lambda^{(3-\eta)c}(s)}+\frac{b^{3}(s_{c})}{\lambda^{(3-\eta)c}(s_{c})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\lambda^{\eta c}(s)+\lambda^{\eta c}(s_{c})\lesssim 1.

Extending the constant over the compact initial interval gives

\mathcal{N}_{5}(s)\lesssim_{s_{c}}\lambda^{(3-\eta)c}(s),\qquad 0\leq s<s(T).

(7.8)

In particular,
\mathcal{N}_{5}(t)\to 0
as
t\uparrow T
.

Define

\ell_{0}:=\bigl((3-c)c_{0}\bigr)^{1/(3-c)}>0.

Since
|J(t)|\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{2}^{1/2}(t)\to 0
,
(
7.1
) gives

\frac{b(t)}{\lambda^{c}(t)}\longrightarrow c_{0}=\frac{\ell_{0}^{3-c}}{3-c},\qquad\ell_{0}\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}).

We next derive the sharp law for
\lambda
. From
(
6.4
),

\left|(\tilde{\lambda})_{t}\tilde{\lambda}^{2-c}+\frac{b}{\lambda^{c}}\right|\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{N}_{2}+b^{2}+|b||J_{1}|}{\lambda^{c}}.

Since
\tilde{\lambda}(t)\to 0
, integration on
[t,T)
gives

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\tilde{\lambda}^{3-c}(t)}{3-c}-\int_{t}^{T}\frac{b(t^{\prime})}{\lambda^{c}(t^{\prime})}\,dt^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{t}^{T}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{2}(t^{\prime})+o(|b(t^{\prime})|)}{\lambda^{c}(t^{\prime})}\,dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{3-c}(t)\int_{s(t)}^{s(T)}\mathcal{N}_{2}(s^{\prime})\,ds^{\prime}+o(T-t),

where (
7.7
) was used in the last line. Set

X(t):=\frac{\lambda^{3-c}(t)}{T-t},\qquad\beta(t):=\frac{1}{T-t}\int_{t}^{T}\frac{b(t^{\prime})}{\lambda^{c}(t^{\prime})}\,dt^{\prime}.

By (
6.10
), (
7.8
), and
b(t)\to 0
, there are
nonnegative functions
\omega_{1}(t),\omega_{2}(t)\to 0
such that

\left|\frac{(1-J_{1}(t))^{3-c}}{3-c}X(t)-\beta(t)\right|\leq\omega_{1}(t)X(t)+\omega_{2}(t).

Moreover, Cesàro averaging gives
\beta(t)\to c_{0}
. For
t
close
to
T
,

(1-J_{1}(t))^{3-c}\geq\frac{1}{2},\qquad\omega_{1}(t)\leq\frac{1}{4(3-c)}.

Hence

\frac{1}{4(3-c)}X(t)\leq|\beta(t)|+\omega_{2}(t),

so
X(t)
is bounded. Consequently
\omega_{1}(t)X(t)\to 0
, and
passing to the limit yields

\lim_{t\rightarrow T}\frac{\lambda^{3-c}(t)}{T-t}=\ell_{0}^{3-c},\qquad\lim_{t\rightarrow T}\frac{\lambda(t)}{(T-t)^{1/(3-c)}}=\ell_{0}.

(7.9)

Combining this with
b/\lambda^{c}\to\ell_{0}^{3-c}/(3-c)
, we obtain

\lim_{t\rightarrow T}\frac{b(t)}{(T-t)^{c/(3-c)}}=\frac{\ell_{0}^{3}}{3-c}.

(7.10)

The law (
7.9
) implies
s(T)=+\infty
. Since

\left|\frac{(x_{1})_{s}}{\lambda}-1\right|\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{2}^{1/2}+b^{2}\longrightarrow 0,

we obtain

(T-t)^{2/(3-c)}(x_{1})_{t}(t)\longrightarrow\frac{1}{\ell_{0}^{2}}.

Because
c>1
,

x_{1}(t)=\frac{3-c}{c-1}\frac{1}{\ell_{0}^{2}}(T-t)^{(1-c)/(3-c)}\bigl(1+o_{t\rightarrow T}(1)\bigr).

The sharp equation for
x_{2}
in
Lemma
4.5
gives

\int_{0}^{\infty}|(x_{2}-\lambda J_{2})_{s}|\,ds\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda(s)(\mathcal{N}_{2}(s)+b^{2}(s))\,ds<+\infty.

Since
J_{2}(s)\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{2}^{1/2}(s)\to 0
,
Lemma
7.3
yields

x_{2}(t)\longrightarrow x_{2,\infty}\in\mathbb{R}\qquad\text{as }t\uparrow T.

In the
s
-variable,

s=\frac{3-c}{c}\frac{1}{\ell_{0}^{3}}(T-t)^{-c/(3-c)}\bigl(1+o(1)\bigr),

\lambda(s)=\left(\frac{3-c}{c}\ell_{0}^{c-3}\right)^{1/c}s^{-1/c}\bigl(1+o(1)\bigr),\qquad b(s)=\frac{1}{cs}\bigl(1+o(1)\bigr),

x_{1}(s)=\frac{3-c}{c-1}\left(\frac{c}{3-c}\right)^{(c-1)/c}\ell_{0}^{(c-3)/c}s^{(c-1)/c}\bigl(1+o(1)\bigr),

and
x_{2}(s)\to x_{2,\infty}
.

From (
4.34
), (
4.35
),
(
H1’
), (
7.8
), and
c<2
, we obtain

\|\varepsilon(t)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}),

(7.11)

and

\|\nabla\varepsilon(t)\|_{L^{2}}\asymp\lambda^{c/2}(t)\qquad\text{as }t\uparrow T.

(7.12)

A change of variables gives

\|\partial_{x_{1}}u(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}=\frac{\|\partial_{y_{1}}(Q_{b(t)}+\varepsilon(t))\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}(t)}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y_{1}}(b\chi_{b}P)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=O(b^{2}),

\displaystyle\left|\iint\partial_{y_{1}}Q\,\partial_{y_{1}}(b\chi_{b}P)\right|

\displaystyle=O(|b|),

\displaystyle\left|\iint\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon\,\partial_{y_{1}}Q\right|

\displaystyle=O(\lambda^{c/2}),

\displaystyle\left|\iint\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon\,\partial_{y_{1}}(b\chi_{b}P)\right|

\displaystyle=O(\lambda^{c/2}|b|).

Thus (
7.9
) gives

\lim_{t\rightarrow T}(T-t)^{1/(3-c)}\|\partial_{x_{1}}u(t)\|_{L^{2}}=\frac{\|\partial_{x_{1}}Q\|_{L^{2}}}{\ell_{0}(u_{0})}.

(7.13)

The same calculation in the
x_{2}
-direction yields

\lim_{t\rightarrow T}(T-t)^{1/(3-c)}\|\nabla u(t)\|_{L^{2}}=\frac{\|\nabla Q\|_{L^{2}}}{\ell_{0}(u_{0})}.

(7.14)

7.3
Asymptotic Stability – the case

c_{0}=0

Assume
c_{0}=0
. The Exit characterization gives
t^{*}=T
. Since

e^{J(s)}
is bounded above and below, letting
s_{2}\uparrow s(T)

in (
6.6
) yields a constant
C^{*}>0
such that

|b(s_{1})|\leq C^{*}\mathcal{N}_{3}(s_{1}),\qquad 0\leq s_{1}<s(T).

(7.15)

By (
6.4
), (
7.15
), and
(
6.10
),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s_{1}}\left|\frac{(\tilde{\lambda})_{s}}{\tilde{\lambda}}\right|\,ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{s_{1}}(|b|+\mathcal{N}_{2})\,ds\lesssim\int_{0}^{s_{1}}\mathcal{N}_{3}(s)\,ds\lesssim\delta(\nu^{*}).

Hence

\left|\log\frac{\tilde{\lambda}(s)}{\tilde{\lambda}(0)}\right|\lesssim\delta(\nu^{*}).

Since
|\tilde{\lambda}/\lambda-1|=|J_{1}|\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{2}^{1/2}
,
Lemma
6.1
gives

|\lambda(s)-1|\lesssim\delta(\nu^{*}),\qquad 0\leq s<s(T).

(7.16)

Mass conservation also gives

\|\varepsilon(s)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\delta(\nu^{*}),\qquad 0\leq s<s(T).

(7.17)

By (
4.35
), (
H1’
),
(
7.16
), and (
7.17
),

\|\nabla\varepsilon(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq 2|E_{0}|+1,\qquad 0\leq t<T.

Thus
\|u(t)\|_{H^{1}}
is uniformly bounded, so
T=+\infty
.
Since
\lambda
is bounded above and below,

s(T)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda(t)^{-3}\,dt=+\infty.

From (
7.15
) and (
6.10
),

\int_{0}^{\infty}|b(s)|\,ds<+\infty.

Moreover, (
4.24
), (
5.29
), and
(
6.5
) give

\int_{0}^{\infty}|b_{s}(s)|\,ds\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}(b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{1})\,ds<+\infty.

Hence Lemma
7.3
gives a finite limit for
b(s)
,
and the integrability of
b
forces this limit to be zero:

b(t)\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{as }t\rightarrow+\infty.

(7.18)

Similarly,

\int_{0}^{\infty}\left|\frac{(\tilde{\lambda})_{s}}{\tilde{\lambda}}\right|\,ds\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}(|b|+\mathcal{N}_{2})\,ds<+\infty.

Lemma
7.3
and (
7.16
) imply

\tilde{\lambda}(s)\longrightarrow\lambda_{\infty}\in(0,+\infty),\qquad|\lambda_{\infty}-1|\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0}).

From (
6.2
) with
i=5
,

\int_{0}^{\infty}\iint(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})(\phi_{5,B})_{y_{1}}\,ds\lesssim\delta(\nu^{*}).

Hence there exists
s_{n}\to+\infty
, with
t_{n}=t(s_{n})
, such that

\iint(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})(t_{n})(\phi_{5,B})_{y_{1}}\longrightarrow 0.

By (
5.15
),
(
5.7
),
K_{5}=21
,
(
H3’
), and (
7.16
),

\iint_{y_{1}<B}\varepsilon^{2}(t_{n})\phi_{5,B}\longrightarrow 0,

\displaystyle\iint_{y_{1}>B}\varepsilon^{2}(t_{n})\phi_{5,B}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\iint_{y_{1}>B}\varepsilon^{2}(t_{n})(\phi_{5,B})_{y_{1}}\right)^{16/17}\left(\iint_{y_{1}>B}y_{1}^{21}\varepsilon^{2}(t_{n})\right)^{1/17}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow 0,

and

\iint|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}(t_{n})\psi_{B}\lesssim\iint|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}(t_{n})(\phi_{5,B})_{y_{1}}\longrightarrow 0.

Therefore
\mathcal{N}_{5}(t_{n})\to 0
. Using
(
6.2
) and (
7.18
),

\mathcal{N}_{5}(t)\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{5}(t_{n})+|b^{3}(t_{n})|+|b^{3}(t)|\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{as }t\rightarrow+\infty.

(7.19)

Since
\tilde{\lambda}(t)\to\lambda_{\infty}
, (
7.19
) gives

\lambda(t)\longrightarrow\lambda_{\infty}.

From (
4.8
),

\left|\frac{(x_{1})_{s}}{\lambda}-1\right|\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{2}^{1/2}+b^{2}\longrightarrow 0.

Thus

(x_{1})_{t}(t)\longrightarrow\frac{1}{\lambda_{\infty}^{2}},\qquad x_{1}(t)=\frac{t}{\lambda_{\infty}^{2}}\bigl(1+o_{t\rightarrow+\infty}(1)\bigr).

The sharp modulation equation for
x_{2}
gives

\int_{0}^{\infty}|(x_{2}-\lambda J_{2})_{s}|\,ds\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda(s)(\mathcal{N}_{2}(s)+b^{2}(s))\,ds<+\infty.

Together with Lemma
7.3
and (
7.19
), this
implies

x_{2}(t)\longrightarrow x_{2,\infty}\in\mathbb{R}.

Finally,

\displaystyle\left\|\lambda(t)u\bigl(t,\lambda(t)y_{1}+x_{1}(t),\lambda(t)y_{2}+x_{2}(t)\bigr)-Q(y_{1},y_{2})\right\|_{H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{2}^{1/2}(t)+|b(t)|^{1-\gamma/2}\longrightarrow 0.

Since
\lambda(t)\to\lambda_{\infty}
and

x_{2}(t)\to x_{2,\infty}
,

\left\|\lambda_{\infty}u\bigl(t,\lambda_{\infty}y_{1}+x_{1}(t),\lambda_{\infty}y_{2}+x_{2,\infty}\bigr)-Q(y_{1},y_{2})\right\|_{H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}}\longrightarrow 0.

Remark 7.4
.

The rigidity argument uses only the level
i=4
in
(
H1i
)–(
H3i
), which suffices for
Lemma
6.3
. We retain
i=5
because the
zero-energy blow-up argument uses

\mathcal{N}_{5}(t)\to 0
in the asymptotic-stability regime; see
Lemma
10.5
.

Remark 7.5
.

In the stable blow-up regime of Theorem
7.1
, the
initial mass is strictly greater than the ground-state mass:

\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}}>\|Q\|_{L^{2}}
. Indeed, local well-posedness,
conservation of energy, and the sharp Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality
exclude finite-time blow-up below the ground-state mass. Suppose now
that
\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}}=\|Q\|_{L^{2}}
and that the solution belongs to
the stable blow-up regime. Mass conservation and

(\varepsilon,Q)=0
give

0=2b(P,Q)+o(b)+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2b(\varepsilon,\chi_{b}P)+b^{2}\|\chi_{b}P\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

as
t\uparrow T
, where
b(t)>0
and
b(t)\to 0
.
Since
\gamma<1
, the estimates

b^{2}\|\chi_{b}P\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim b^{2-\gamma}=o(b),\qquad 2b|(\varepsilon,\chi_{b}P)|\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+Cb^{2-\gamma}

imply

0\geq 2b(P,Q)+\frac{1}{2}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+o(b).

Dividing by
b>0
and letting
t\uparrow T
contradicts

(P,Q)>0
.

Chapter 8
Openness of the blow-up branch

For a solution in the tube, write

u(t,x)=\frac{1}{\lambda(t)}(Q_{b(t)}+\varepsilon(t))\left(\frac{x_{1}-x_{1}(t)}{\lambda(t)},\frac{x_{2}-x_{2}(t)}{\lambda(t)}\right),\qquad\frac{ds}{dt}=\frac{1}{\lambda^{3}(t)},

with the orthogonality conditions of the modulation lemma. We set

A(s)=\frac{b(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}.

We use the following consequences of the previous chapters. First, the modulation
map is unique and
C^{1}
in a neighborhood of the soliton manifold. Second, for
solutions in the bootstrap regime, we have from (
6.2
) and (
6.6
) that

\mathcal{N}_{4}(s_{2})\leq C\bigl(\mathcal{N}_{4}(s_{1})+|b(s_{1})|^{3}+|b(s_{2})|^{3}\bigr),\qquad s_{2}\geq s_{1},

(8.1)

while

\left|A(s_{2})-A(s_{1})\right|\leq C_{0}\left\{\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3}(s_{1})+b^{2}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{1})}+\frac{b^{2}(s_{2})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{2})}\right\}.

(8.2)

Finally, the rigidity theorem says that the branch is determined by the sign of

\lim A(s)
; in particular
\liminf A(s)>0
implies stable finite-time blow-up.

Lemma 8.1
.

Let
v_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{0}}
and let
v(t)
remain in
\mathcal{T}_{\alpha^{*}}
on
[0,t_{0}]
. Let

w_{0,n}\in\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{0}}
and
w_{0,n}\to v_{0}
in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. Then, after discarding
finitely many indices,
w_{n}
is modulated on
[0,t_{0}]
and

\lambda_{n}(t_{0})\to\lambda_{v}(t_{0}),\qquad b_{n}(t_{0})\to b_{v}(t_{0}),\qquad x_{j,n}(t_{0})\to x_{j,v}(t_{0}),\quad j=1,2.

Moreover,

\varepsilon_{n}(t_{0})\rightarrow\varepsilon_{v}(t_{0})\text{ in }H^{1}.

Furthermore, with
s_{0,n}=s_{n}(t_{0})
and
s_{0,v}=s_{v}(t_{0})
,

\mathcal{N}_{3,n}(s_{0,n})\to\mathcal{N}_{3,v}(s_{0,v}).

(8.3)

Proof.

Local well-posedness gives
w_{n}\to v
in
C([0,t_{0}];H^{1})
. Since

v([0,t_{0}])
is compactly contained in the tube
\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\alpha}}
for some
\tilde{\alpha}<\alpha^{*}
, then
w_{n}([0,t_{0}])\in\mathcal{T}_{\alpha^{*}}
for
all large
n
. Applying the
C^{1}
modulation map at the fixed time

t_{0}
gives the convergence of the parameters. The formula defining the error
then gives
\varepsilon_{n}(t_{0})\to\varepsilon_{v}(t_{0})
in
H^{1}
.

It remains only to justify the weighted part of (
8.3
). The
condition
w_{0,n}\in\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{0}}
gives uniformly

\mathcal{N}_{4,n}(0)+|b_{n}(0)|\leq C.

Since
w_{n}
stays in the bootstrap regime on
[0,t_{0}]
,
(
8.1
) yields

\sup_{n}\sup_{0\leq t\leq t_{0}}\mathcal{N}_{4,n}(s_{n}(t))<\infty.

(8.4)

Set
f_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}(t_{0})-\varepsilon_{v}(t_{0})
. Then
f_{n}\to 0
in
L^{2}
, and
(
8.4
) gives

\sup_{n}\int_{y_{1}>0}y_{1}^{4}|f_{n}|^{2}<\infty.

Hence, for each
0\leq p<4
,

\int_{y_{1}>0}y_{1}^{p}|f_{n}|^{2}\leq\left(\int|f_{n}|^{2}\right)^{1-p/4}\left(\int_{y_{1}>0}y_{1}^{4}|f_{n}|^{2}\right)^{p/4}\to 0.

Taking the exponents appearing in
\mathcal{N}_{3}
gives
\mathcal{N}_{3,n}(s_{0,n})\to\mathcal{N}_{3,v}(s_{0,v})
; the gradient part follows from strong
H^{1}
convergence.
∎

Lemma 8.2
.

There are universal constants
K_{*}>1
and
\sigma_{*}>0
with the following
property. Suppose that, at some renormalized time
s_{0}
in the bootstrap
regime,

A(s_{0})\geq 4\sigma,\qquad\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3}(s_{0})+b^{2}(s_{0})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{0})}\leq\frac{\sigma}{K_{*}},\qquad 0<\sigma<\sigma_{*}.

(8.5)

Then, as long as the solution remains in the tube for
s\geq s_{0}
,

A(s)\geq 2\sigma.

Consequently this solution belongs to the stable blow-up branch.

Proof.

Let
I=[s_{0},S]
be any compact subinterval of
[0,s^{*}]
, where
s^{*}
is the exit time, and set
M_{I}=\sup_{I}|A|
. Since
|b|\leq\nu^{*}
in this regime,

\frac{b^{2}(s)}{\lambda^{c}(s)}\leq\nu^{*}|A(s)|.

From (
8.2
), for
s\in I
,

|A(s)|\leq|A(s_{0})|+C_{0}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3}(s_{0})+b^{2}(s_{0})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{0})}+C_{0}\nu^{*}M_{I}.

Taking
\nu^{*}
so small that
C_{0}\nu^{*}\leq 1/2
gives

M_{I}\leq 2\left(|A(s_{0})|+C_{0}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3}(s_{0})+b^{2}(s_{0})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{0})}\right).

(8.6)

Using (
8.2
) again, together with (
8.6
),

A(s)\geq A(s_{0})-C_{0}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3}(s_{0})+b^{2}(s_{0})}{\lambda^{c}(s_{0})}-C_{0}\nu^{*}M_{I}.

Choose
K_{*}\geq 16C_{0}
and reduce
\nu^{*}
so that
2C_{0}\nu^{*}\leq 1/4
. Then
(
8.5
) implies

A(s)\geq\frac{3}{4}A(s_{0})-\frac{5}{4}\frac{C_{0}\sigma}{K_{*}}>2\sigma.

(8.7)

Since
S\leq s^{*}
was arbitrary, the lower bound holds for the whole future interval in
the tube. By Theorem
7.1
, the lower bound
(
8.7
) excludes the exit and asymptotic-stability
alternatives. Hence the solution belongs to the stable blow-up branch.
∎

Proposition 8.3

(Openness of the stable blow-up branch)
.

Let
v_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{0}}
generate a solution
v(t)
which blows up in finite time
T_{v}
and
satisfies
v(t)\in\mathcal{T}_{\alpha^{*}}
for
0\leq t<T_{v}
. Then there exists
\rho>0
such
that, if
w_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{0}}
and
\|w_{0}-v_{0}\|_{H^{1}}<\rho
, the corresponding solution

w(t)
is in the stable blow-up branch.

Proof.

For the base solution, the blow-up asymptotics give

A_{v}(s)=\frac{b_{v}(s)}{\lambda_{v}^{c}(s)}\to c_{0}>0,\qquad\mathcal{N}_{3,v}(s)\lesssim\lambda_{v}^{(3-\eta)c}(s)

for some small fixed
\eta>0
. Hence

\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3,v}(s)+b_{v}^{2}(s)}{\lambda_{v}^{c}(s)}\to 0.

Choose
0<\sigma<\min\{\sigma_{*},c_{0}/16\}
and then choose a time

t_{0}<T_{v}
so close to
T_{v}
that, with
s_{0,v}=s_{v}(t_{0})
,

A_{v}(s_{0,v})\geq 8\sigma,\qquad\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3,v}(s_{0,v})+b_{v}^{2}(s_{0,v})}{\lambda_{v}^{c}(s_{0,v})}\leq\frac{\sigma}{2K_{*}}.

Assume there are
w_{0,n}\in\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{0}}
,
w_{0,n}\to v_{0}
in
H^{1}
. Lemma
8.1
, applied at the fixed time
t_{0}
(and the associated time
s_{0,n}=s_{n}(t_{0}))
, implies for all large
n
,

A_{n}(s_{0,n})\geq 4\sigma,\qquad\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3,n}(s_{0,n})+b_{n}^{2}(s_{0,n})}{\lambda_{n}^{c}(s_{0,n})}\leq\frac{\sigma}{K_{*}}.

Lemma
8.2
therefore places
w_{n}
in the stable blow-up branch. This proves the relative openness in
\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{0}}
for the

H^{1}
topology.
∎

Chapter 9
Asymptotic Radiation in the Blow-Up Regime

9.1
Strong Convergence in
L^{p}
of the Asymptotic Profile

Assume that the solution belongs to the stable blow-up branch of
Theorem
7.1
. We prove that there exists a nonzero function

u^{*}\in\bigcap_{2\leq p<\infty}L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})

such that, for every fixed
2\leq p<\infty
,

u(t,x_{1},x_{2})-\frac{1}{\lambda(t)}Q\left(\frac{x_{1}-x_{1}(t)}{\lambda(t)},\frac{x_{2}-x_{2}(t)}{\lambda(t)}\right)\longrightarrow u^{*}\quad\text{in }L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\quad\text{as }t\uparrow T.

The endpoint
p=2
is treated by the refined inhomogeneous
Strichartz estimate, whereas the extension to
2<p<\infty
uses the
interpolated dispersive estimate on a shrinking time interval.

Write

u=\mathcal{Q}+\widetilde{u},

where, with

y_{i}=\frac{x_{i}-x_{i}(t)}{\lambda(t)},\qquad i=1,2,

we set

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}(t,x_{1},x_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda(t)}Q_{b(t)}(y_{1},y_{2}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{u}(t,x_{1},x_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda(t)}\varepsilon(t,y_{1},y_{2}).

Denote the exact modulated ground state by

S(t,x_{1},x_{2}):=\frac{1}{\lambda(t)}Q(y_{1},y_{2}).

For every fixed
2\leq p<\infty
, the change of variables from

x=(x_{1},x_{2})
to
y=(y_{1},y_{2})
, the bound

\|\chi_{b}P\|_{L^{p}_{y}}\lesssim|b|^{-\gamma/p}
, and
(
7.10
) give

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{Q}(t)-S(t)\|_{L^{p}_{x}}

\displaystyle=\lambda(t)^{\frac{2}{p}-1}|b(t)|\|\chi_{b}P\|_{L^{p}_{y}}\lesssim\lambda(t)^{\frac{2}{p}-1}|b(t)|^{1-\frac{\gamma}{p}}

(9.1)

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda(t)^{c-1+\frac{2-c\gamma}{p}}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{as }t\uparrow T.

The exponent is positive because
c>1
and
c\gamma<2
. It remains
to prove that
\widetilde{u}(t)
converges in every finite
L^{p}
.

The physical radiation satisfies

\partial_{t}\widetilde{u}+\partial_{x_{1}}\Delta\widetilde{u}+f(\widetilde{u})_{x_{1}}+\mathfrak{F}=0,

where

f(\widetilde{u}):=(\mathcal{Q}+\widetilde{u})^{3}-\mathcal{Q}^{3}

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}(t,x_{1},x_{2})={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\lambda^{4}(t)}\Psi_{b(t)}(y_{1},y_{2})+\frac{b_{t}(t)}{\lambda(t)}\zeta_{b(t)}(y_{1})P(y_{1},y_{2})

(9.2)

\displaystyle-\frac{\lambda^{2}(t)\lambda_{t}(t)+b(t)}{\lambda^{4}(t)}\Lambda Q_{b(t)}(y_{1},y_{2})

\displaystyle-\frac{\lambda^{2}(t)(x_{1})_{t}(t)-1}{\lambda^{4}(t)}(Q_{b(t)})_{y_{1}}(y_{1},y_{2})

\displaystyle-\frac{\lambda^{2}(t)(x_{2})_{t}(t)}{\lambda^{4}(t)}(Q_{b(t)})_{y_{2}}(y_{1},y_{2}).

Here

\zeta_{b}P=\frac{\partial Q_{b}}{\partial b},\qquad\Psi_{b}=\bigl(-\Delta Q_{b}+Q_{b}-Q_{b}^{3}\bigr)_{y_{1}}-b\Lambda Q_{b}.

For
0<\tau<T-t_{0}
, define

v^{\tau}(t):=\widetilde{u}(t+\tau)-\widetilde{u}(t),\qquad t\in[t_{0},T-\tau).

Then

\partial_{t}v^{\tau}+\partial_{x_{1}}\Delta v^{\tau}+G^{\tau}+H^{\tau}=0,

where

\displaystyle G^{\tau}(t)

\displaystyle=f(\widetilde{u})_{x_{1}}(t+\tau)-f(\widetilde{u})_{x_{1}}(t),

\displaystyle H^{\tau}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{F}(t+\tau)-\mathfrak{F}(t).

Let
U(t)
be the unitary group associated with the linear ZK equation:

\displaystyle U(t)g_{0}(x_{1},x_{2})=(2\pi)^{-2}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}

\displaystyle e^{i\left[t(\xi_{1}^{3}+\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{2})+x_{1}\xi_{1}+x_{2}\xi_{2}\right]}\widehat{g}_{0}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\,d\xi_{1}d\xi_{2}.

(9.3)

Duhamel’s formula gives, for
t_{0}\leq t<T-\tau
,

v^{\tau}(t)=U(t-t_{0})v^{\tau}(t_{0})-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}U(t-s)\bigl(G^{\tau}(s)+H^{\tau}(s)\bigr)\,ds.

(9.4)

Linear and refined Strichartz estimates

Following the strategy used in
[
33
]
and

[
46
]
, we compare two time translates of the
radiation and control their difference through Duhamel’s formula. We
first recall the abstract inhomogeneous estimate of Foschi

[
22
]
.

Theorem 9.1

(Foschi’s inhomogeneous Strichartz estimate)
.

Let
(X,d\mu)
be a measure space, let
H
be a Hilbert space, and
let
V(t):H\to L^{2}_{X}
,
t\in\mathbb{R}
, be a family of linear
operators satisfying

\|V(t)h\|_{L^{2}_{X}}\lesssim\|h\|_{H}

for every
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
h\in H
, and

\|V(t)V(s)^{*}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{X}}\lesssim|t-s|^{-\sigma}\|g\|_{L^{1}_{X}}

for every
t\neq s
and
g\in L^{1}_{X}\cap L^{2}_{X}
, where

0<\sigma<1
. A pair
(q,r)\in[1,\infty]^{2}
is called

\sigma
-acceptable if

\frac{1}{q}<2\sigma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{r}\right),

or if
(q,r)=(\infty,2)
. If
(q_{1},r_{1})
and

(q_{2},r_{2})
are
\sigma
-acceptable and satisfy

\frac{1}{q_{1}}+\frac{\sigma}{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{q_{2}}+\frac{\sigma}{r_{2}}=\sigma,

then

\left\|\int_{s<t}V(t)V(s)^{*}F(s)\,ds\right\|_{L_{t}^{q_{1}}L_{X}^{r_{1}}}\lesssim\|F\|_{L_{t}^{q_{2}^{\prime}}L_{X}^{r_{2}^{\prime}}}.

We also recall the linear estimate for the ZK group, stated as
Lemma 2.3 in
[
35
]
.

Theorem 9.2

(Linear ZK estimates)
.

Let
U(t)
be the group defined in (
9.3
). Then

\|U(t)h\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|h\|_{L^{2}_{x}},

and, for
t\neq 0
,

\|U(t)h\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{2}{3}}\|h\|_{L^{1}_{x}}.

Since
U(t)U(s)^{*}=U(t-s)
, Foschi’s theorem with

\sigma=2/3
gives the following consequence.

Corollary 9.3

(Refined inhomogeneous Strichartz estimate)
.

Let
(q_{1},r_{1})
and
(q_{2},r_{2})
be

\frac{2}{3}
-acceptable pairs satisfying

\frac{1}{q_{1}}+\frac{2}{3r_{1}}+\frac{1}{q_{2}}+\frac{2}{3r_{2}}=\frac{2}{3}.

Then

\left\|\int_{0}^{t}U(t-s)h(s)\,ds\right\|_{L_{t}^{q_{1}}L_{x}^{r_{1}}}\lesssim\|h\|_{L_{t}^{q_{2}^{\prime}}L_{x}^{r_{2}^{\prime}}}.

(9.5)

From now on, fix
0<\eta<1
for which (
7.8
) holds. By the
hierarchy of the weighted norms,

\mathcal{N}_{i}(t)\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{5}(t)\lesssim\lambda(t)^{(3-\eta)c},\qquad 1\leq i\leq 5.

(9.6)

The restriction
\eta<1
gives

(3-\eta)/2>1
, which is the strict decay margin required below. It
also permits the choice of a finite exponent

r_{2}>2\gamma/(1-\eta)
in the
L^{2}
-argument.

Pointwise bounds for the forcing

We first establish bounds that are valid for every fixed
r>2
. Set

a_{r}:=\min\left\{\frac{3-\eta}{2},1+\gamma,1+\frac{1}{r}\right\},\qquad d_{r}:=\min\left\{\frac{3-\eta}{2},1+\frac{\gamma}{r}\right\}.

Since
0<\eta<1
, we have
a_{r}>1
and
d_{r}>1
.

Step 1: Estimate of
f(\widetilde{u})_{x_{1}}
.

The change of variables from
x=(x_{1},x_{2})
to
y=(y_{1},y_{2})
gives

\|f(\widetilde{u})_{x_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}_{x}}=\lambda(t)^{-2-\frac{2}{r}}\left\|\partial_{y_{1}}\bigl[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q_{b}^{3}\bigr]\right\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}_{y}}.

The complete expansion is

\displaystyle\partial_{y_{1}}\bigl[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q_{b}^{3}\bigr]={}

\displaystyle 3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}+6Q_{b}(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}\varepsilon+3(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle+6Q_{b}\varepsilon\varepsilon_{y_{1}}+3\varepsilon^{2}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}.

Choose

R=\frac{2r}{r-2},\qquad\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{R}.

The exponent
R
is only an auxiliary Hölder exponent; the target
space for the nonlinear terms is
L^{r^{\prime}}
.

For the term involving
(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}\varepsilon^{2}
, write

(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}=A_{b}+B_{b},

where

A_{b}:=Q_{y_{1}}+b\chi_{b}P_{y_{1}},\qquad B_{b}:=b|b|^{\gamma}\chi^{\prime}\bigl(|b|^{\gamma}y_{1}\bigr)P.

Hölder’s inequality, the weighted Sobolev estimate from Lemma
4.3
and (
9.6
) give

\displaystyle\|A_{b}\varepsilon^{2}\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\varepsilon^{2}\sqrt{|A_{b}|}\right\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\left\|\sqrt{|A_{b}|}\right\|_{L^{R}_{y}}\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{1}^{1/2}(t)\lesssim\lambda(t)^{\frac{(3-\eta)c}{2}}.

On the other hand, since
P\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, together with (
7.11
),
(
7.12
), and
b(t)\sim c_{0}\lambda^{c}(t)
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\|B_{b}\varepsilon^{2}\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}_{y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|b|^{1+\gamma}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2r^{\prime}}_{y}}^{2}\lesssim|b|^{1+\gamma}\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2/r}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2-2/r}\lesssim\lambda(t)^{c\left(1+\gamma+\frac{1}{r}\right)}.

Consequently,

\|(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}\varepsilon^{2}\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}_{y}}\lesssim\lambda(t)^{c\min\left\{\frac{3-\eta}{2},1+\gamma+\frac{1}{r}\right\}}.

The other two terms containing only one factor of
\varepsilon
or

\varepsilon_{y_{1}}
are estimated in the same way. It follows that

\displaystyle\|Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}_{y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda(t)^{c\frac{3-\eta}{2}},

\displaystyle\|Q_{b}(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}\varepsilon\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}_{y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda(t)^{c\min\left\{\frac{3-\eta}{2},1+\gamma\right\}}.

Also, by the two-dimensional Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality,

\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{R}_{y}}\lesssim\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2/r}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{1-2/r}\lesssim\lambda^{c/r}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|Q_{b}\varepsilon\varepsilon_{y_{1}}\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}_{y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{1}(t)+|b|\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{R}_{y}}\lesssim\lambda^{c\min\left\{3-\eta,\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{r}\right\}}.

Finally,

\displaystyle\|\varepsilon^{2}\varepsilon_{y_{1}}\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}_{y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2R}_{y}}^{2}\lesssim\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2+2/r}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{1-2/r}\lesssim\lambda^{c\left(1+\frac{1}{r}\right)}.

Collecting the five estimates yields

\|f(\widetilde{u})_{x_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}_{x}}\lesssim\lambda(t)^{-2-\frac{2}{r}+ca_{r}}.

(9.7)

Step 2: Estimate of
\mathfrak{F}
.

The profile bounds give

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{b}P\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}_{y}}\lesssim|b|^{-\gamma/r^{\prime}},\qquad\|\Psi_{b}\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}_{y}}\lesssim|b|^{1+\gamma/r},

\displaystyle\|\Lambda Q_{b}\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}_{y}}+\|(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}_{y}}+\|(Q_{b})_{y_{2}}\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}_{y}}\lesssim 1.

Moreover,

\lambda^{3}b_{t}=b_{s},\quad\lambda^{2}\lambda_{t}+b=\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b,\quad\lambda^{2}(x_{1})_{t}-1=\frac{(x_{1})_{s}}{\lambda}-1,\quad\lambda^{2}(x_{2})_{t}=\frac{(x_{2})_{s}}{\lambda}.

Hence (
4.8
) imply

|b_{s}|\lesssim b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{1},\qquad\left|\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right|+\left|\frac{(x_{1})_{s}}{\lambda}-1\right|+\left|\frac{(x_{2})_{s}}{\lambda}\right|\lesssim b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{1}^{1/2}.

Using
b\sim\lambda^{c}
and (
9.6
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{F}(t)\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda(t)^{-2-\frac{2}{r}}\Bigl[|b|^{1+\gamma/r}+(b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{1})|b|^{-\gamma/r^{\prime}}+b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{1}^{1/2}\Bigr]\lesssim\lambda(t)^{-2-\frac{2}{r}+cd_{r}}.

Indeed, the powers of
\lambda^{c}
inside the brackets are

1+\frac{\gamma}{r},\qquad 2-\gamma+\frac{\gamma}{r},\qquad 3-\eta-\gamma+\frac{\gamma}{r},\qquad 2,\qquad\frac{3-\eta}{2},

and their minimum is
d_{r}
, because
0<\gamma,\eta<1
. Therefore,

\|\mathfrak{F}(t)\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}_{x}}\lesssim\lambda(t)^{-2-\frac{2}{r}+cd_{r}}.

(9.8)

The
L^{2}
-limit

Choose

(q_{1},r_{1})=(\infty,2),\qquad\frac{1}{q_{2}}+\frac{2}{3r_{2}}=\frac{1}{3},

with

r_{2}>\max\left\{2,\frac{2\gamma}{1-\eta}\right\}.

The condition
r_{2}>2
makes
(q_{2},r_{2})

\frac{2}{3}
-acceptable, while

r_{2}>2\gamma/(1-\eta)
gives

\frac{3-\eta}{2}>1+\frac{\gamma}{r_{2}},\qquad d_{r_{2}}=1+\frac{\gamma}{r_{2}}.

Moreover,

\frac{3}{q_{2}^{\prime}}=2+\frac{2}{r_{2}},

and
a_{r_{2}},d_{r_{2}}>1>1/q_{2}^{\prime}
. From
(
9.7
),
(
9.8
), and

\lambda(t)\sim(T-t)^{1/(3-c)}
, we find

\displaystyle\|f(\widetilde{u})_{x_{1}}\|_{L^{q_{2}^{\prime}}([t,T);L^{r_{2}^{\prime}}_{x})}\quad\lesssim\left(\int_{t}^{T}\lambda(t^{\prime})^{-q_{2}^{\prime}\left(\frac{3}{q_{2}^{\prime}}-ca_{r_{2}}\right)}dt^{\prime}\right)^{1/q_{2}^{\prime}}\quad\lesssim(T-t)^{\frac{c}{3-c}\left(a_{r_{2}}-\frac{1}{q_{2}^{\prime}}\right)},

and similarly for
\mathfrak{F}
. Consequently,

\|f(\widetilde{u})_{x_{1}}\|_{L^{q_{2}^{\prime}}([t,T);L^{r_{2}^{\prime}}_{x})}\lesssim(T-t)^{\frac{c}{3-c}\left(a_{r_{2}}-\frac{1}{q_{2}^{\prime}}\right)}\longrightarrow 0,

(9.9)

and

\|\mathfrak{F}\|_{L^{q_{2}^{\prime}}([t,T);L^{r_{2}^{\prime}}_{x})}\lesssim(T-t)^{\frac{c}{3-c}\left(d_{r_{2}}-\frac{1}{q_{2}^{\prime}}\right)}\longrightarrow 0.

(9.10)

For
0\leq t^{\prime}<T-\tau-t
, rewrite (
9.4
) with base time

t
as

\displaystyle v^{\tau}(t+t^{\prime})={}

\displaystyle U(t^{\prime})v^{\tau}(t)-\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}U(t^{\prime}-s)\bigl(G^{\tau}(t+s)+H^{\tau}(t+s)\bigr)\,ds.

Using (
9.5
) and the unitarity of
U(t)
on
L^{2}
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\|v^{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}([t,T-\tau);L^{2}_{x})}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\|v^{\tau}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

(9.11)

\displaystyle+\|G^{\tau}\|_{L^{q_{2}^{\prime}}([t,T-\tau);L^{r_{2}^{\prime}}_{x})}+\|H^{\tau}\|_{L^{q_{2}^{\prime}}([t,T-\tau);L^{r_{2}^{\prime}}_{x})}.

By the triangle inequality and translation in time,

\displaystyle\|G^{\tau}\|_{L^{q_{2}^{\prime}}([t,T-\tau);L^{r_{2}^{\prime}}_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq\|f(\widetilde{u})_{x_{1}}\|_{L^{q_{2}^{\prime}}([t,T);L^{r_{2}^{\prime}}_{x})}+\|f(\widetilde{u})_{x_{1}}\|_{L^{q_{2}^{\prime}}([t+\tau,T);L^{r_{2}^{\prime}}_{x})},

\displaystyle\|H^{\tau}\|_{L^{q_{2}^{\prime}}([t,T-\tau);L^{r_{2}^{\prime}}_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathfrak{F}\|_{L^{q_{2}^{\prime}}([t,T);L^{r_{2}^{\prime}}_{x})}+\|\mathfrak{F}\|_{L^{q_{2}^{\prime}}([t+\tau,T);L^{r_{2}^{\prime}}_{x})}.

Thus (
9.9
) and (
9.10
) imply that, for
every
\epsilon>0
, there exists
t_{\epsilon}<T
such that,
uniformly in
0<\tau<T-t_{\epsilon}
,

\displaystyle\|G^{\tau}\|_{L^{q_{2}^{\prime}}([t_{\epsilon},T-\tau);L^{r_{2}^{\prime}}_{x})}+\|H^{\tau}\|_{L^{q_{2}^{\prime}}([t_{\epsilon},T-\tau);L^{r_{2}^{\prime}}_{x})}\leq\epsilon.

(9.12)

By (
9.1
), after increasing
t_{\epsilon}
if
necessary,

\|\mathcal{Q}(t)-S(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq\epsilon,\qquad t_{\epsilon}\leq t<T.

(9.13)

We next estimate
v^{\tau}(t_{\epsilon})
in
L^{2}_{x}
. The triangle
inequality gives

\displaystyle\|v^{\tau}(t_{\epsilon})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq{}

\displaystyle\|u(t_{\epsilon}+\tau)-u(t_{\epsilon})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathcal{Q}(t_{\epsilon}+\tau)-S(t_{\epsilon}+\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle+\|\mathcal{Q}(t_{\epsilon})-S(t_{\epsilon})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|S(t_{\epsilon}+\tau)-S(t_{\epsilon})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.

Since
u\in C([0,T);H^{1}_{x})
, there exists

\tau_{1}=\tau_{1}(t_{\epsilon})>0
such that

\|u(t_{\epsilon}+\tau)-u(t_{\epsilon})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq\epsilon,\qquad 0<\tau\leq\tau_{1}.

(9.14)

It remains to control the last term. On every compact interval

I\Subset[0,T)
, the parameters
\lambda,x_{1},x_{2}
are absolutely
continuous and
\lambda
is bounded away from zero. Hence

\frac{\lambda_{t}}{\lambda^{2}},\quad\frac{(x_{1})_{t}}{\lambda^{2}},\quad\frac{(x_{2})_{t}}{\lambda^{2}}\in L^{1}(I).

Differentiating
S
gives, in
L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
,

\partial_{t}S=-\frac{\lambda_{t}}{\lambda^{2}}\Lambda Q(y_{1},y_{2})-\frac{(x_{1})_{t}}{\lambda^{2}}Q_{y_{1}}(y_{1},y_{2})-\frac{(x_{2})_{t}}{\lambda^{2}}Q_{y_{2}}(y_{1},y_{2}).

After changing variables from
x=(x_{1},x_{2})
to
y=(y_{1},y_{2})
,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}S(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq{}

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\lambda_{t}}{\lambda}\right|\|\Lambda Q\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\left|\frac{(x_{1})_{t}}{\lambda}\right|\|Q_{y_{1}}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\left|\frac{(x_{2})_{t}}{\lambda}\right|\|Q_{y_{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{y}},

which belongs to
L^{1}(I)
. Therefore

S\in AC(I;L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
. The fundamental theorem of calculus
in
L^{2}_{x}
and Minkowski’s integral inequality give

\displaystyle\|S(t_{\epsilon}+\tau)-S(t_{\epsilon})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}=\left\lVert\int_{t_{\epsilon}}^{t_{\epsilon}+\tau}\partial_{t}S(t^{\prime})\,dt^{\prime}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t_{\epsilon}}^{t_{\epsilon}+\tau}\|\partial_{t}S(t^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\,dt^{\prime}\longrightarrow 0

as
\tau\to 0
. Thus, after decreasing
\tau_{1}
if necessary,

\|S(t_{\epsilon}+\tau)-S(t_{\epsilon})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq\epsilon,\qquad 0<\tau\leq\tau_{1}.

(9.15)

Combining (
9.13
), (
9.14
), and
(
9.15
), we obtain

\|v^{\tau}(t_{\epsilon})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq 4\epsilon,\qquad 0<\tau\leq\tau_{1}.

(9.16)

Applying (
9.11
) with base time
t=t_{\epsilon}
, and using
(
9.12
) and (
9.16
), yields

\|v^{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}([t_{\epsilon},T-\tau);L^{2}_{x})}\lesssim\epsilon.

Choose
t_{0}\in(\max\{T-\tau_{1},t_{\epsilon}\},T)
. For

t_{0}<t_{1}<t_{2}<T
, set
\tau=t_{2}-t_{1}
. Then

0<\tau<\tau_{1}
and
t_{1}<T-\tau
, so

\|\widetilde{u}(t_{2})-\widetilde{u}(t_{1})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}=\|v^{\tau}(t_{1})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon.

Thus there exists
u^{*}\in L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
such that

\widetilde{u}(t)\longrightarrow u^{*}\quad\text{in }L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\quad\text{as }t\uparrow T.

Extension to every
2<p<\infty

Fix
2<p<\infty
, set
p^{\prime}=p/(p-1)
, and define

\delta_{p}:=\frac{2}{3}\left(1-\frac{2}{p}\right).

For fixed
t\neq 0
, interpolate by the Riesz–Thorin theorem to obtain

\|U(t)h\|_{L^{p}_{x}}\lesssim|t|^{-\delta_{p}}\|h\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{x}},\qquad t\neq 0.

(9.17)

Apply the pointwise forcing bounds with
r=p
. Since

d_{p}=\min\left\{\frac{3-\eta}{2},1+\frac{\gamma}{p}\right\}\leq a_{p},

we have

\displaystyle\|f(\widetilde{u})_{x_{1}}(t)\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{x}}+\|\mathfrak{F}(t)\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda(t)^{-2-\frac{2}{p}+cd_{p}}\lesssim(T-t)^{-\beta_{p}},

where

\beta_{p}:=\max\left\{0,\frac{2+\frac{2}{p}-cd_{p}}{3-c}\right\}.

We claim that

\beta_{p}<1-\delta_{p}.

(9.18)

If
2+2/p-cd_{p}\leq 0
, this is immediate. Otherwise,
(
9.18
) is equivalent to

3cd_{p}>3+c+\frac{4c-6}{p}.

(9.19)

If
d_{p}=(3-\eta)/2
, then, because
c>3/2
(see Theorem
E.2
),
p\geq 2
, and

\eta<1
,

3+c+\frac{4c-6}{p}\leq 3+c+\frac{4c-6}{2}=3c<3c\frac{3-\eta}{2}.

If
d_{p}=1+\gamma/p
, then

\displaystyle 3c\left(1+\frac{\gamma}{p}\right)-\left(3+c+\frac{4c-6}{p}\right)=2c-3+\frac{3c\gamma-4c+6}{p}.

If
3c\gamma-4c+6\geq 0
, this is at least
2c-3>0
. If

3c\gamma-4c+6<0
, then
p\geq 2
gives

2c-3+\frac{3c\gamma-4c+6}{p}\geq 2c-3+\frac{3c\gamma-4c+6}{2}=\frac{3c\gamma}{2}>0.

Thus (
9.19
), and hence
(
9.18
), holds for every finite
p>2
.

Choose
q=q(p)>1
such that

\frac{1}{1-\delta_{p}}<q<\frac{1}{\beta_{p}},

where the upper bound is omitted when
\beta_{p}=0
. This is possible
by (
9.18
). Then

\delta_{p}q^{\prime}<1,\qquad\beta_{p}q<1.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\|f(\widetilde{u})_{x_{1}}\|_{L^{q}([t,T);L^{p^{\prime}}_{x})}+\|\mathfrak{F}\|_{L^{q}([t,T);L^{p^{\prime}}_{x})}\lesssim(T-t)^{\frac{1}{q}-\beta_{p}}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{as }t\uparrow T.

(9.20)

The corresponding difference terms satisfy, by the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\|G^{\tau}\|_{L^{q}([t,T-\tau);L^{p^{\prime}}_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq\|f(\widetilde{u})_{x_{1}}\|_{L^{q}([t,T);L^{p^{\prime}}_{x})}+\|f(\widetilde{u})_{x_{1}}\|_{L^{q}([t+\tau,T);L^{p^{\prime}}_{x})},

\displaystyle\|H^{\tau}\|_{L^{q}([t,T-\tau);L^{p^{\prime}}_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathfrak{F}\|_{L^{q}([t,T);L^{p^{\prime}}_{x})}+\|\mathfrak{F}\|_{L^{q}([t+\tau,T);L^{p^{\prime}}_{x})}.

We now estimate the Duhamel formula with base time
t_{\epsilon}
. By
Sobolev embedding and the unitarity of
U(t)
on
H^{1}_{x}
,

\displaystyle\|U(t-t_{\epsilon})v^{\tau}(t_{\epsilon})\|_{L^{p}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{p}\|U(t-t_{\epsilon})v^{\tau}(t_{\epsilon})\|_{H^{1}_{x}}=\|v^{\tau}(t_{\epsilon})\|_{H^{1}_{x}}.

For the inhomogeneous term, Minkowski’s integral inequality and
(
9.17
) give

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{t_{\epsilon}}^{t}U(t-s)\bigl(G^{\tau}(s)+H^{\tau}(s)\bigr)\,ds\right\|_{L^{p}_{x}}\quad

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t_{\epsilon}}^{t}\left\|U(t-s)\bigl(G^{\tau}(s)+H^{\tau}(s)\bigr)\right\|_{L^{p}_{x}}\,ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{t_{\epsilon}}^{t}(t-s)^{-\delta_{p}}\bigl(\|G^{\tau}(s)\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{x}}+\|H^{\tau}(s)\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{x}}\bigr)\,ds.

Thus (
9.4
) yields

\displaystyle\|v^{\tau}(t)\|_{L^{p}_{x}}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\|v^{\tau}(t_{\epsilon})\|_{H^{1}_{x}}+\int_{t_{\epsilon}}^{t}(t-s)^{-\delta_{p}}\bigl(\|G^{\tau}(s)\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{x}}+\|H^{\tau}(s)\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{x}}\bigr)\,ds.

Since
\delta_{p}q^{\prime}<1
, Hölder’s inequality in time gives

\displaystyle\int_{t_{\epsilon}}^{t}(t-s)^{-\delta_{p}}\bigl(\|G^{\tau}(s)\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{x}}+\|H^{\tau}(s)\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{x}}\bigr)\,ds

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left(\int_{t_{\epsilon}}^{t}(t-s)^{-\delta_{p}q^{\prime}}\,ds\right)^{1/q^{\prime}}\bigl(\|G^{\tau}\|_{L^{q}([t_{\epsilon},t);L^{p^{\prime}}_{x})}+\|H^{\tau}\|_{L^{q}([t_{\epsilon},t);L^{p^{\prime}}_{x})}\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim(T-t_{\epsilon})^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}-\delta_{p}}\bigl(\|G^{\tau}\|_{L^{q}([t_{\epsilon},T-\tau);L^{p^{\prime}}_{x})}+\|H^{\tau}\|_{L^{q}([t_{\epsilon},T-\tau);L^{p^{\prime}}_{x})}\bigr).

Taking the supremum over
t_{\epsilon}\leq t<T-\tau
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|v^{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}([t_{\epsilon},T-\tau);L^{p}_{x})}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\|v^{\tau}(t_{\epsilon})\|_{H^{1}_{x}}

(9.21)

\displaystyle+(T-t_{\epsilon})^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}-\delta_{p}}\bigl(\|G^{\tau}\|_{L^{q}([t_{\epsilon},T-\tau);L^{p^{\prime}}_{x})}+\|H^{\tau}\|_{L^{q}([t_{\epsilon},T-\tau);L^{p^{\prime}}_{x})}\bigr).

Choose
t_{\epsilon}<T
sufficiently close to
T
so that
(
9.20
) makes the second term on the right of
(
9.21
) smaller than
\epsilon
, uniformly
in
0<\tau<T-t_{\epsilon}
. Since

\widetilde{u}\in C_{\mathrm{loc}}([0,T);H^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
, there
exists
\tau_{p}=\tau_{p}(t_{\epsilon})>0
such that

\|v^{\tau}(t_{\epsilon})\|_{H^{1}_{x}}\leq\epsilon,\qquad 0<\tau\leq\tau_{p}.

Therefore,

\|v^{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}([t_{\epsilon},T-\tau);L^{p}_{x})}\lesssim\epsilon,\qquad 0<\tau\leq\tau_{p}.

Choose
t_{0}\in(\max\{T-\tau_{p},t_{\epsilon}\},T)
. If

t_{0}<t_{1}<t_{2}<T
and
\tau=t_{2}-t_{1}
, then

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{u}(t_{2})-\widetilde{u}(t_{1})\|_{L^{p}_{x}}

\displaystyle=\|v^{\tau}(t_{1})\|_{L^{p}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon.

Thus
\widetilde{u}(t)
is a Cauchy family in
L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
as

t\uparrow T
. Let its limit be
u_{p}^{*}
. Since the already proved

L^{2}_{x}
-convergence and the
L^{p}_{x}
-convergence both imply convergence
in distributions, uniqueness of the distributional limit gives

u_{p}^{*}=u^{*}.

Together with (
9.1
), this proves that, for every

2\leq p<\infty
,

u(t)-S(t)\longrightarrow u^{*}\qquad\text{strongly in }L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\quad\text{as }t\uparrow T.

(9.22)

It remains to identify the mass of the limiting radiation. Since

(\varepsilon,Q)=0
,

\displaystyle(\mathcal{Q}(t),\widetilde{u}(t))_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle=(Q_{b},\varepsilon)_{L^{2}_{y}}=b(\chi_{b}P,\varepsilon),

and therefore

|(\mathcal{Q}(t),\widetilde{u}(t))|\lesssim|b(t)|^{1-\gamma/2}\|\varepsilon(t)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\longrightarrow 0.

Mass conservation and (
9.1
) with
p=2
give

\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}=\|Q\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}+\|u^{*}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}.

By Remark
7.5
,

\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}>\|Q\|_{L^{2}_{y}}
, and consequently

u^{*}\not\equiv 0.

Finally, the convergence cannot be strong in
H^{1}_{x}
. Set

r(t,x_{1},x_{2}):=u(t,x_{1},x_{2})-S(t,x_{1},x_{2}).

Then

r(t,x_{1},x_{2})=\frac{1}{\lambda(t)}\bigl[\varepsilon(t,y_{1},y_{2})+b(t)\chi_{b}(y_{1})P(y_{1},y_{2})\bigr].

By the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\|\nabla r(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\lambda(t)}\left(\|\nabla\varepsilon(t)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}-\|\nabla(b\chi_{b}P)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\right)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\lambda(t)^{\frac{c}{2}-1}-\lambda(t)^{c(1-\gamma/2)-1}

\displaystyle=\lambda(t)^{\frac{c-2}{2}}\left[1-O\left(\lambda(t)^{\frac{c(1-\gamma)}{2}}\right)\right]\longrightarrow+\infty,

where we used (
7.12
),

b(t)\sim c_{0}\lambda^{c}(t)
,
c<2
, and
\gamma<1
.

Hence the convergence in (
9.22
) is not strong in

H^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. The next section proves the stronger statement
that the limiting profile itself satisfies

u^{*}\notin H^{\varsigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\qquad\text{for every }\varsigma\geq\frac{c}{2}.

9.2
Sobolev Obstruction for the Limiting Radiation

The following lemma quantifies the left-hand convergence of
P
and is needed below to isolate the leading cut-off transition term in
\Psi_{b}
and control the remaining profile error.

Lemma 9.4

(Quantitative left-tail expansion of
P
)
.

Recall from (
4.9
) that

F(y_{2}):=\lim_{y_{1}\to-\infty}P(y_{1},y_{2})

and from (
4.10
)

-F_{y_{2}y_{2}}+F=-\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Lambda Q\,dy_{1}=-g_{Q}(y_{2}).

There exist constants
C,\kappa>0
such that, for every multi-index

\beta
with
|\beta|\leq 3
,

\left|\partial^{\beta}\bigl(P(y_{1},y_{2})-F(y_{2})\bigr)\right|\leq Ce^{\kappa y_{1}}e^{-\kappa|y_{2}|}

for all
y_{1}\leq-1
. Consequently,

\left|P-P_{y_{2}y_{2}}+g_{Q}\right|+|P_{y_{1}}|+|P_{y_{1}y_{1}}|\leq Ce^{\kappa y_{1}}e^{-\kappa|y_{2}|}\qquad(y_{1}\leq-1).

Proof.

From the construction of
P
,

P-F=\widetilde{P}+\int_{-\infty}^{y_{1}}U(z,y_{2})\,dz+\beta_{P}Q_{y_{1}},\qquad\beta_{P}=\frac{(U,Q)}{\|Q_{y_{1}}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}}.

The functions
U,\widetilde{P}
, and
Q_{y_{1}}
, together with all their
derivatives, belong to
\mathcal{Y}
. The stated estimate therefore
follows after slightly decreasing the exponential decay rate. Finally,

P-P_{y_{2}y_{2}}+g_{Q}=(P-F)-(P_{y_{2}y_{2}}-F^{\prime\prime}),

and the last assertion follows.
∎

Theorem 9.5
.

Assume that
u
belongs to the stable blow-up regime of
Theorem
7.1
, and let
u^{*}
be the asymptotic radiation
constructed in Chapter
9
.
Then

u^{*}\notin H^{\varsigma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\qquad\text{for every }\varsigma\geq\frac{c}{2}.

In particular, since
c<2
, we have
u^{*}\notin H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.

Proof.

For
t
sufficiently close to
T
, we have
b(t)>0
and
\lambda
is
strictly decreasing. Set

S(t,x)=\frac{1}{\lambda(t)}Q\left(\frac{x-x(t)}{\lambda(t)}\right),\qquad\mathcal{Q}(t,x)=\frac{1}{\lambda(t)}Q_{b(t)}\left(\frac{x-x(t)}{\lambda(t)}\right),

\widetilde{u}(t,x)=u(t,x)-\mathcal{Q}(t,x)=\frac{1}{\lambda(t)}\varepsilon\left(t,\frac{x-x(t)}{\lambda(t)}\right),\qquad x(t)=(x_{1}(t),x_{2}(t)).

By the strong
L^{2}
convergence proved in
Chapter
9
and
(
9.1
),

\widetilde{u}(t)\longrightarrow u^{*}\qquad\text{strongly in }L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\quad\text{as }t\uparrow T.

(9.23)

We use the Fourier convention of (
9.3
) and write

\omega(\xi)=\xi_{1}^{3}+\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{2}.

Step 1: asymptotics of the geometric parameters.

Fix
0<\eta<1
for which (
7.8
) holds, and set

a=\frac{3-\eta}{2},\qquad\nu_{m}=a-1=\frac{1-\eta}{2}>0.

By (
7.8
), the hierarchy of the
\mathcal{N}_{i}
, and
(
5.29
),

\mathcal{M}^{1/2}\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{1}^{1/2}\lesssim\lambda^{ac}\sim b^{a},\qquad\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{1}\lesssim\lambda^{(3-\eta)c}\sim b^{3-\eta}.

Since
|J|\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{2}^{1/2}\to 0
, the convergence in
(
7.1
) implies

\frac{b(t)}{\lambda^{c}(t)}\longrightarrow c_{0}>0.

Hence (
4.8
) and (
7.11
) give

\left|\lambda^{2}\lambda_{t}+b\right|+\left|\lambda^{2}(x_{1})_{t}-1\right|+\left|\lambda^{2}(x_{2})_{t}\right|\lesssim b^{1+\nu_{m}},\qquad|b_{s}|\lesssim b^{2}.

(9.24)

In particular,

\lambda_{t}=-c_{0}\lambda^{c-2}(1+o(1)).

Integrating this identity, and then dividing the equation for
(x_{1})_{t}

by that for
\lambda_{t}
, yields

T-t=\frac{\lambda^{3-c}(t)}{(3-c)c_{0}}(1+o(1)),\qquad x_{1}(t)=\frac{\lambda^{1-c}(t)}{(c-1)c_{0}}(1+o(1)).

(9.25)

Moreover, by (
9.24
),

|(x_{2})_{t}|\lesssim\lambda^{-2}b^{1+\nu_{m}},\qquad-\lambda_{t}\sim b\lambda^{-2},

and therefore

\left|\frac{dx_{2}}{d\lambda}\right|=\left|\frac{(x_{2})_{t}}{\lambda_{t}}\right|\lesssim b^{\nu_{m}}\lesssim\lambda^{c\nu_{m}}.

Since
x_{2}(t)\to x_{2,\infty}
,

|x_{2}(t)-x_{2,\infty}|\lesssim\int_{0}^{\lambda(t)}\mu^{c\nu_{m}}\,d\mu\lesssim\lambda^{1+c\nu_{m}}(t).

(9.26)

Step 2: transition source and time-integrable remainders.

By Lemma
9.4
, on the transition region

-2b^{-\gamma}\leq y_{1}\leq-b^{-\gamma}
,

|P-P_{y_{2}y_{2}}+g_{Q}|+|P_{y_{1}}|+|P_{y_{1}y_{1}}|\lesssim e^{-\kappa b^{-\gamma}}e^{-\kappa|y_{2}|}.

Using the exact expansion (
4.3
), we obtain

\Psi_{b}(y)=-b^{1+\gamma}\chi^{\prime}(b^{\gamma}y_{1})g_{Q}(y_{2})+\Psi_{b}^{\rm rem}(y).

(9.27)

More precisely,

\|\Psi_{b}^{\rm rem}\|_{L^{1}_{y}}\lesssim be^{-\kappa b^{-\gamma}}+b^{1+2\gamma}+b^{2-\gamma}+b^{2}.

Here the first term contains the contributions of
P-F
,
P_{y_{1}}
,

P_{y_{1}y_{1}}
,
Q^{2}P
, and the localization error; the term

b(\chi_{b})_{y_{1}y_{1}y_{1}}P
is
O(b^{1+2\gamma})
in
L^{1}_{y}
,

b^{2}\Lambda(\chi_{b}P)
is
O(b^{2-\gamma})
, and the remaining nonlinear
terms are
O(b^{2})
. Thus, with

\nu_{\rm tr}=\min\{2\gamma,1-\gamma\}>0,

\|\Psi_{b}^{\rm rem}\|_{L^{1}_{y}}\lesssim b^{1+\nu_{\rm tr}}.

(9.28)

Recall the forcing
\mathfrak{F}

introduced in (
9.2
). We write

\mathfrak{F}=\mathfrak{F}_{\rm tr}+\mathfrak{F}_{\rm rem},

where

\mathfrak{F}_{\rm tr}(t,x)=\frac{b^{1+\gamma}(t)}{\lambda^{4}(t)}\chi^{\prime}\bigl(b^{\gamma}(t)y_{1}\bigr)g_{Q}(y_{2}).

(9.29)

A change of variables gives

\widehat{\mathfrak{F}_{\rm tr}}(t,\xi)=b(t)\lambda^{-2}(t)e^{-ix(t)\cdot\xi}\widehat{\chi^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\lambda(t)\xi_{1}}{b^{\gamma}(t)}\right)\widehat{g_{Q}}\bigl(\lambda(t)\xi_{2}\bigr),

(9.30)

and
\widehat{\chi^{\prime}}(0)=\int\chi^{\prime}=1
.

The nonlocalized
y_{1}
-tail has effective length
O(b^{-\gamma})
,
every
y_{1}
-derivative of
P
is localized, and
P
and its derivatives
decay exponentially in
y_{2}
. Hence

\|\zeta_{b}P\|_{L^{1}_{y}}\lesssim b^{-\gamma},\qquad\|\Lambda Q_{b}\|_{L^{1}_{y}}+\|(Q_{b})_{y_{1}}\|_{L^{1}_{y}}+\|(Q_{b})_{y_{2}}\|_{L^{1}_{y}}\lesssim 1.

Using (
9.28
), (
9.24
), and

|b_{t}|=|b_{s}|/\lambda^{3}\lesssim b^{2}/\lambda^{3}
, we find

\displaystyle\|\lambda^{-4}\Psi_{b}^{\rm rem}(y)\|_{L^{1}_{x}}\lesssim\lambda^{-2}\|\Psi_{b}^{\rm rem}\|_{L^{1}_{y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{-2}b^{1+\nu_{\rm tr}},

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{b_{t}}{\lambda}\zeta_{b}P(y)\right\|_{L^{1}_{x}}\lesssim|b_{t}||\lambda|\|\zeta_{b}P\|_{L^{1}_{y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{-2}b^{2-\gamma},

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\lambda^{2}\lambda_{t}+b}{\lambda^{4}}\Lambda Q_{b}(y)\right\|_{L^{1}_{x}}+\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\|\frac{\lambda^{2}(x_{j})_{t}-\delta_{1j}}{\lambda^{4}}(Q_{b})_{y_{j}}(y)\right\|_{L^{1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{-2}b^{1+\nu_{m}}.

Set

\nu_{\text{prof}}=\min\{\nu_{\rm tr},\nu_{m}\}>0.

Since

dt=-\frac{1}{c_{0}}\lambda^{2-c}(1+o(1))\,d\lambda,

(9.31)

or, in estimates,
|dt|\lesssim\lambda^{2-c}|d\lambda|
, we obtain

\int_{t_{\star}}^{T}\|\mathfrak{F}_{\rm rem}(t)\|_{L^{1}_{x}}\,dt\lesssim\lambda^{1+c\nu_{\text{prof}}}(t_{\star}).

(9.32)

We next estimate the nonlinearity

f(\widetilde{u})=\lambda^{-3}[(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q_{b}^{3}](y)
.
By (
7.8
) and (
7.12
),

\|Q\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\lesssim\lambda^{ac},\qquad\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{4}_{y}}\lesssim\lambda^{c/4},\qquad\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{8/3}_{y}}^{2}\lesssim\lambda^{c/4}.

Expanding
Q_{b}=Q+b\chi_{b}P
, we have

\displaystyle\|Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon\|_{L^{4/3}_{y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|Q^{2}\varepsilon\|_{L^{4/3}_{y}}+b\|Q\chi_{b}P\varepsilon\|_{L^{4/3}_{y}}+b^{2}\|\chi_{b}^{2}P^{2}\varepsilon\|_{L^{4/3}_{y}}\lesssim\lambda^{ac}+b^{2-\gamma/4}.

Indeed,

\|Q^{2}\varepsilon\|_{L^{4/3}_{y}}\leq\|Q\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\|Q\|_{L^{4}_{y}}
.
Moreover, the same weighted estimate gives

\|\sqrt{Q}\,\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{1}^{1/2}(t)\lesssim\lambda^{ac}
, while

\|\sqrt{Q}\,\chi_{b}P\|_{L^{4}_{y}}\lesssim 1
; hence

\|Q\chi_{b}P\varepsilon\|_{L^{4/3}_{y}}\lesssim\lambda^{ac}
. Finally,

\|\chi_{b}^{2}P^{2}\|_{L^{4}_{y}}\lesssim b^{-\gamma/4}
.
Similarly,

\|Q_{b}\varepsilon^{2}\|_{L^{4/3}_{y}}\lesssim\|Q\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{4}_{y}}+b\|P\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{8/3}_{y}}^{2}\lesssim\lambda^{ac+c/4}+b\lambda^{c/4},

while from Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality and (
7.12
),

\|\varepsilon^{3}\|_{L^{4/3}_{y}}=\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{4}_{y}}^{3}\lesssim\lambda^{3c/4}.

Since
a>1
,
0<\gamma<1
,
b\lesssim\lambda^{c}
, and

0<\lambda\leq 1
, all these terms are bounded by

C\lambda^{3c/4}
. Therefore

\|(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q_{b}^{3}\|_{L^{4/3}_{y}}\lesssim\lambda^{3c/4}.

(9.33)

After returning to physical variables and using the preceding time
change,

\int_{t_{R}}^{T}\|f(\widetilde{u})(t)\|_{L^{4/3}_{x}}\,dt\lesssim\lambda^{3/2-c/4}(t_{R}).

(9.34)

Choose

\max\left\{\frac{1}{1+c\nu_{\text{prof}}},\frac{3(2-c)}{6-c}\right\}<\varkappa<1,

(9.35)

and, for
R
sufficiently large, define
t_{R}<T
by

\lambda(t_{R})=R^{-\varkappa}.

For a fixed compact interval
K\Subset\mathbb{R}
, set

\sigma(\xi)=\frac{\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{c-1}}{c_{0}},\qquad\Gamma_{R}^{K}=\{\xi:\ R\leq\xi_{2}\leq 2R,\ \sigma(\xi)\in K\}.

For
t_{R}<t_{1}<T
, the local
H^{1}
theory and the equation for

\widetilde{u}
give the finite-time Duhamel formula

\displaystyle\widehat{\widetilde{u}}(t_{1})={}

\displaystyle e^{i(t_{1}-t_{R})\omega}\widehat{\widetilde{u}}(t_{R})-\int_{t_{R}}^{t_{1}}e^{i(t_{1}-t)\omega}\left[i\xi_{1}\widehat{f(\widetilde{u})}(t)+\widehat{\mathfrak{F}}(t)\right]dt.

For
t_{1}<T
, Duhamel’s formula holds in
H^{-2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.
Let
\Pi_{R}^{K}
denote the Fourier multiplier with symbol

\mathbf{1}_{\Gamma_{R}^{K}}
. Since
\Gamma_{R}^{K}
is bounded for fixed

R
,
\Pi_{R}^{K}:H^{-2}_{x}\to L^{2}_{x}
is bounded. Applying
\Pi_{R}^{K}
to
Duhamel’s formula gives

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\Gamma_{R}^{K}}\widehat{\widetilde{u}}(t_{1})={}

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\Gamma_{R}^{K}}e^{i(t_{1}-t_{R})\omega}\widehat{\widetilde{u}}(t_{R})-\mathbf{1}_{\Gamma_{R}^{K}}\int_{t_{R}}^{t_{1}}e^{i(t_{1}-t)\omega}\left[i\xi_{1}\widehat{f(\widetilde{u})}(t)+\widehat{\mathfrak{F}}(t)\right]dt

(9.36)

in

L^{2}_{\xi}
.

Define

\mathcal{G}_{R}(t,\xi)=\mathbf{1}_{\Gamma_{R}^{K}}(\xi)\left[i\xi_{1}\widehat{f(\widetilde{u})}(t,\xi)+\widehat{\mathfrak{F}}(t,\xi)\right].

We claim that

\mathcal{G}_{R}\in L^{1}_{t}([t_{R},T);L^{2}_{\xi}).

(9.37)

For the transition source,

\|\mathfrak{F}_{\rm tr}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\lambda^{-3}b^{1+\gamma/2},

and therefore, using (
9.31
),

\int_{t_{R}}^{T}\|\mathfrak{F}_{\rm tr}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\,dt\lesssim\int_{0}^{\lambda(t_{R})}\mu^{-3+c(1+\gamma/2)}\mu^{2-c}\,d\mu=\int_{0}^{\lambda(t_{R})}\mu^{-1+c\gamma/2}\,d\mu<\infty.

For the remainder,

\|\mathbf{1}_{\Gamma_{R}^{K}}\widehat{\mathfrak{F}_{\rm rem}}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\leq|\Gamma_{R}^{K}|^{1/2}\|\mathfrak{F}_{\rm rem}(t)\|_{L^{1}_{x}},

which is time-integrable by (
9.32
). Finally,
Hausdorff–Young and Hölder on the finite-measure set
\Gamma_{R}^{K}

give

\|\mathbf{1}_{\Gamma_{R}^{K}}i\xi_{1}\widehat{f(\widetilde{u})}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\lesssim\|\xi_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\xi}(\Gamma_{R}^{K})}|\Gamma_{R}^{K}|^{1/4}\|f(\widetilde{u})(t)\|_{L^{4/3}_{x}},

which is time-integrable by (
9.34
). This
proves (
9.37
).

We can now let
t_{1}\uparrow T
in
(
9.36
). Indeed,
(
9.23
) and Plancherel give convergence of the
left-hand side in
L^{2}_{\xi}(\Gamma_{R}^{K})
. Since
\Gamma_{R}^{K}
is bounded,

e^{i(t_{1}-T)\omega}\to 1
uniformly there. Moreover, if

I_{t_{1}}=\int_{t_{R}}^{t_{1}}e^{i(t_{1}-t)\omega}\mathcal{G}_{R}(t)\,dt,\qquad I_{T}=\int_{t_{R}}^{T}e^{i(T-t)\omega}\mathcal{G}_{R}(t)\,dt,

then

\displaystyle\|I_{t_{1}}-I_{T}\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\Gamma_{R}^{K}}|e^{i(t_{1}-T)\omega}-1|\int_{t_{R}}^{t_{1}}\|\mathcal{G}_{R}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\,dt+\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\|\mathcal{G}_{R}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\,dt\longrightarrow 0.

Also,

\left\lVert 1_{\Gamma_{R}^{K}}\big[e^{i(t_{1}-t_{R})\omega}-e^{i(T-t_{R})\omega}\big]\widehat{\widetilde{u}}(t_{R})\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\leq\sup_{\Gamma_{R}^{K}}|e^{i(t_{1}-T)\omega}-1|\|\widetilde{u}(t_{R})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\rightarrow 0.

Finally, by the strong
L^{2}
convergence of
\widetilde{u}
to
u^{*}
we have that

\|1_{\Gamma_{R}^{K}}(\widehat{\widetilde{u}}(t_{1})-\widehat{u^{*}})\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\rightarrow 0.

Thus, for every fixed
R
and
K
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\Gamma_{R}^{K}}\widehat{u^{*}}={}

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\Gamma_{R}^{K}}e^{i(T-t_{R})\omega}\widehat{\widetilde{u}}(t_{R})-\mathbf{1}_{\Gamma_{R}^{K}}\int_{t_{R}}^{T}e^{i(T-t)\omega}\left[i\xi_{1}\widehat{f(\widetilde{u})}(t)+\widehat{\mathfrak{F}}(t)\right]dt

(9.38)

in
L^{2}_{\xi}(\Gamma_{R}^{K})
.

Step 3: the transition-frequency contribution.

For
q>0
, define

\Theta(q)=\frac{q^{3-c}}{3-c}-\frac{q^{1-c}}{c-1}.

Then

\Theta^{\prime}(q)=q^{-c}(1+q^{2})>0,

and
\Theta
maps
(0,\infty)
bijectively onto
\mathbb{R}
. Set

\mathscr{A}(\sigma)=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{i\sigma\Theta(q)}\widehat{g_{Q}}(q)\,dq.

(9.39)

Since
g_{Q}\in W^{2,1}(\mathbb{R})
,

|\widehat{g_{Q}}(q)|\lesssim(1+q^{2})^{-1},

and hence
\widehat{g_{Q}}\in L^{1}_{q}
. The function
g_{Q}
is real and even.
Hence
\widehat{g_{Q}}
is real and even. Since
g_{Q}\not\equiv 0
from (
4.26
), its Fourier
transform cannot vanish identically on
(0,\infty)
. After the change
of variables
z=\Theta(q)
,

\mathscr{A}(\sigma)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{i\sigma z}H(z)\,dz,\qquad H(z)=\frac{\widehat{g_{Q}}(\Theta^{-1}(z))}{\Theta^{\prime}(\Theta^{-1}(z))}.

Here
H\in L^{1}_{z}
and
H\not\equiv 0
, thus Fourier injectivity
implies
\mathscr{A}\not\equiv 0
. By continuity, there exist a compact
interval
I\Subset\mathbb{R}
and
a_{0}>0
such that

|\mathscr{A}(\sigma)|\geq 2a_{0}\qquad(\sigma\in I).

(9.40)

Let

\mathcal{T}_{R}(\xi)=-\int_{t_{R}}^{T}e^{i(T-t)\omega(\xi)}\widehat{\mathfrak{F}_{\rm tr}}(t,\xi)\,dt.

(9.41)

Lemma 9.6
.

Let
K\Subset\mathbb{R}
. Then

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}R\leq\xi_{2}\leq 2R\\
\sigma(\xi)\in K\end{subarray}}\left|\xi_{2}e^{ix_{2,\infty}\xi_{2}}\mathcal{T}_{R}(\xi)+\mathscr{A}(\sigma(\xi))\right|\longrightarrow 0

as
R\to\infty
.

Proof.

Using (
9.30
), changing variables from
t
to

\lambda
, and then setting
q=\lambda\xi_{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\xi_{2}e^{ix_{2,\infty}\xi_{2}}\mathcal{T}_{R}(\xi)={}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{\lambda(t_{R})\xi_{2}}e^{i\Upsilon_{R}(q,\xi)}\frac{b}{-\lambda^{2}\lambda_{t}}\widehat{\chi^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\lambda\xi_{1}}{b^{\gamma}}\right)\widehat{g_{Q}}(q)\,dq,

(9.42)

where
t=t(q,\xi_{2})
is determined by

\lambda(t)=q/\xi_{2}
, and

\Upsilon_{R}(q,\xi)=(T-t)\omega(\xi)-x_{1}(t)\xi_{1}-(x_{2}(t)-x_{2,\infty})\xi_{2}.

Fix
\epsilon>0
. Since
\widehat{g_{Q}}\in L^{1}_{q}
, choose

0<\delta<1<M
such that

\int_{0}^{\delta}|\widehat{g_{Q}}(q)|\,dq+\int_{M}^{\infty}|\widehat{g_{Q}}(q)|\,dq<\epsilon.

For
R
large,

\lambda(t_{R})\xi_{2}\geq R^{1-\varkappa}>M.

The ratio
b/(-\lambda^{2}\lambda_{t})
is uniformly bounded near
T
, and

|\widehat{\chi^{\prime}}|\leq\|\chi^{\prime}\|_{L^{1}_{y_{1}}}.

Hence the contributions of

(0,\delta)\cup(M,\infty)
to the difference between
(
9.42
) and

-\mathscr{A}(\sigma)
are
O(\epsilon)
, uniformly in the stated region.

It remains to consider
q\in[\delta,M]
. There

\lambda=\frac{q}{\xi_{2}}\longrightarrow 0

uniformly for
R\leq\xi_{2}\leq 2R
. If
\sigma(\xi)\in K
, then

\xi_{1}=c_{0}\sigma(\xi)\xi_{2}^{1-c}.

Using (
9.25
), uniformly on

K\times[\delta,M]
,

(T-t)\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{2}=\sigma(\xi)\frac{q^{3-c}}{3-c}(1+o_{R}(1)),

x_{1}(t)\xi_{1}=\sigma(\xi)\frac{q^{1-c}}{c-1}(1+o_{R}(1)).

Moreover,

|(T-t)\xi_{1}^{3}|\lesssim_{K,M}R^{-2c}\longrightarrow 0,

|(x_{2}(t)-x_{2,\infty})\xi_{2}|\lesssim q^{1+c\nu_{m}}\xi_{2}^{-c\nu_{m}}\longrightarrow 0,

and

\frac{\lambda\xi_{1}}{b^{\gamma}}=C_{\sigma,q}\xi_{2}^{-c(1-\gamma)}(1+o_{R}(1))\longrightarrow 0

uniformly. Finally,

\frac{b}{-\lambda^{2}\lambda_{t}}\longrightarrow 1

uniformly for
0<\lambda\leq M/R
. Hence the integrand in
(
9.42
) converges uniformly on

K\times[\delta,M]
to

e^{i\sigma\Theta(q)}\widehat{g_{Q}}(q).

The compact part therefore converges uniformly, and the integrable tails
complete the proof.
∎

Define

\Gamma_{R}=\left\{\xi:\ R\leq\xi_{2}\leq\frac{3}{2}R,\quad\sigma(\xi)\in I\right\}.

(9.43)

For fixed
\xi_{2}
, the admissible
\xi_{1}
-interval has length
comparable to
\xi_{2}^{1-c}
, and hence

|\Gamma_{R}|\asymp\int_{R}^{\frac{3R}{2}}\xi_{2}^{1-c}d\xi_{2}\asymp R^{2-c}.

(9.44)

By Lemma
9.6
and
(
9.40
), for all sufficiently large
R
,

\left|\xi_{2}e^{ix_{2,\infty}\xi_{2}}\mathcal{T}_{R}(\xi)+\mathscr{A}(\sigma(\xi))\right|\leq a_{0}\qquad(\xi\in\Gamma_{R}).

Thus

|\mathcal{T}_{R}(\xi)|\geq\frac{a_{0}}{\xi_{2}}\gtrsim R^{-1}\qquad(\xi\in\Gamma_{R}),

and therefore

\|\mathbf{1}_{\Gamma_{R}}\mathcal{T}_{R}\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}^{2}\gtrsim R^{-2}|\Gamma_{R}|\gtrsim R^{-c}.

(9.45)

Step 4: comparison with the remaining Duhamel terms.

Since
|\xi|\gtrsim R
on
\Gamma_{R}
, Plancherel and
(
7.12
) give

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{\Gamma_{R}}\widehat{\widetilde{u}}(t_{R})\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\lesssim R^{-1}\|\nabla\widetilde{u}(t_{R})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

(9.46)

\displaystyle=R^{-1}\lambda^{-1}(t_{R})\|\nabla\varepsilon(t_{R})\|_{L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim R^{-1+\varkappa(1-c/2)}=o(R^{-c/2}),

because
c<2
and
\varkappa<1
.

By (
9.32
) and
(
9.44
),

\left\|\mathbf{1}_{\Gamma_{R}}\int_{t_{R}}^{T}e^{i(T-t)\omega}\widehat{\mathfrak{F}_{\rm rem}}(t)\,dt\right\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\lesssim R^{(2-c)/2-\varkappa(1+c\nu_{\text{prof}})}=o(R^{-c/2})

(9.47)

by (
9.35
). Finally, Hausdorff–Young,

|\xi_{1}|\lesssim R^{1-c}
on
\Gamma_{R}
, and
(
9.34
) yield

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{1}_{\Gamma_{R}}\int_{t_{R}}^{T}e^{i(T-t)\omega}i\xi_{1}\widehat{f(\widetilde{u})}(t)\,dt\right\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}

(9.48)

\displaystyle\lesssim R^{1-c+(2-c)/4-\varkappa(3/2-c/4)}=o(R^{-c/2}),

again by (
9.35
).

Using the identity
(
9.38
) with
K=I
, restricting it to

\Gamma_{R}
, and applying the triangle inequality, we conclude from
(
9.45
)–(
9.48
)
that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{\Gamma_{R}}\widehat{u^{*}}\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\geq\|\mathbf{1}_{\Gamma_{R}}\mathcal{T}_{R}\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}-\|\mathbf{1}_{\Gamma_{R}}\widehat{\widetilde{u}}(t_{R})\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle-\left\|\mathbf{1}_{\Gamma_{R}}\int_{t_{R}}^{T}e^{i(T-t)\omega}\widehat{\mathfrak{F}_{\rm rem}}(t)\,dt\right\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle-\left\|\mathbf{1}_{\Gamma_{R}}\int_{t_{R}}^{T}e^{i(T-t)\omega}i\xi_{1}\widehat{f(\widetilde{u})}(t)\,dt\right\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim R^{-c/2}.

Consequently,

\int_{\Gamma_{R}}|\widehat{u^{*}}(\xi)|^{2}\,d\xi\gtrsim R^{-c}.

(9.49)

Let
R_{n}=4^{n}
. The sets
\Gamma_{R_{n}}
are pairwise disjoint and

|\xi|\asymp R_{n}
on
\Gamma_{R_{n}}
. Therefore, for every

\varsigma\geq c/2
,

\displaystyle\|u^{*}\|_{\dot{H}^{\varsigma}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{n\gg 1}\int_{\Gamma_{R_{n}}}|\xi|^{2\varsigma}|\widehat{u^{*}}(\xi)|^{2}\,d\xi\gtrsim\sum_{n\gg 1}R_{n}^{2\varsigma-c}=+\infty.

Since
u^{*}\in L^{2}_{x}
, this proves

u^{*}\notin H^{\varsigma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\qquad\text{for every }\varsigma\geq\frac{c}{2}.

In particular,
u^{*}\notin H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.
∎

Chapter 10
Blow-Up for
E_{0}\leq 0

We start with an orbital stability result that appears in
[
50
]
(Lemma 1) and
[
44
]
(Lemma 1) for the one-dimensional case regarding the gKdV equation. We reiterate the result for the two-dimensional case.

Theorem 10.1
.

There exists
\alpha_{\text{orb}}>0
such that the following holds. For all
0<\alpha^{\prime}\leq\alpha_{\text{orb}},
there exists
\delta=\delta(\alpha^{\prime})>0,
with
\delta(\alpha^{\prime})\rightarrow 0
as
\alpha^{\prime}\rightarrow 0,
such that for all
u\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),u\not\equiv 0,
if

\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\|Q\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\alpha(u)\leq\alpha^{\prime}\quad\text{and}\quad E(u)\leq\alpha^{\prime}\iint|\nabla u|^{2},

then there exists
\tilde{y}_{1},\tilde{y}_{2}

\in\mathbb{R}
and
\epsilon_{0}\in\{-1,1\}
such that

\|Q-\epsilon_{0}\lambda_{0}u(\lambda_{0}y_{1}+\tilde{y}_{1},\lambda_{0}y_{2}+\tilde{y}_{2})\|_{H^{1}}\leq\delta(\alpha^{\prime}),

with

\lambda_{0}=\frac{\|\nabla Q\|_{L^{2}}}{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}}.

We include the proof of this theorem in Appendix
C
for the sake of completeness.

Let
u_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{0}}
satisfy
E(u_{0})\leq 0
. Choose the
orbital-stability threshold below
\alpha^{*}
. The orbital-stability theorem
10.1

then keeps the solution in
\mathcal{T}_{\alpha^{*}}
throughout its lifespan,
so the exit alternative is excluded. Let
(\lambda(t),b(t),x_{1}(t),x_{2}(t))
be the geometrical quantities arising from a decomposition of
u(t)
as in Lemma
4.4
.

Case 1:
E_{0}<0
.

Suppose, by contradiction, that the solution belongs to the
Asymptotic Stability branch. Then

b(s)\to 0,\qquad\mathcal{N}_{2}(s)\to 0,\qquad\lambda(s)\to\lambda_{\infty}>0.

On the other hand, conservation of energy (
4.35
) gives

\lambda^{2}(s)|E_{0}|+\|\nabla\varepsilon(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim|b(s)|+\mathcal{N}_{2}(s)\to 0,

so
\lambda(s)\to 0
, a contradiction.

Case 2:

E_{0}=0
.

Suppose, for a contradiction, that the solution belongs to the
asymptotic-stability branch of Theorem
7.1
and that

u_{0}
is not equivalent to
Q
up to symmetries. Then

b(s)\to 0
,
\mathcal{N}_{2}(s)\to 0
, and

\lambda(s)\to\lambda_{\infty}>0
. The energy estimate
(
4.35
) gives

\|\nabla\varepsilon(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim|b(s)|+\mathcal{N}_{2}(s)\rightarrow 0\mbox{ as }s\rightarrow+\infty.

(10.1)

The mass estimate (
4.34
) also gives

\|\varepsilon(t)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0})
for all

t\geq 0
. Moreover, by (
7.15
), there exists

C^{*}>0
such that

|b(t)|\leq C^{*}\mathcal{N}_{3}(t)

(10.2)

for all
t\in[0,+\infty)
.
Thus, by the bounds (
10.1
), (
10.2
) and the estimate (
6.10
), we obtain

\int_{t}^{+\infty}\|\nabla\varepsilon(t^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt^{\prime}\lesssim\int_{t}^{+\infty}|b(t^{\prime})|dt^{\prime}+\int_{t}^{+\infty}\mathcal{N}_{2}(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{4}(t)\rightarrow 0,\mbox{ as }t\rightarrow+\infty.

(10.3)

We begin by giving a rough bound for the localized mass. The next lemma appears in Appendix C,
[
49
]
, and here it is adapted to our setting.

Lemma 10.2
.

Let
w\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
D\geq 1
. Then we have the following estimate:

\iint_{|y_{1}|\leq D}w^{2}\leq CD^{2}\left(\iint w^{2}e^{-|y_{1}|}+\iint|\nabla w|^{2}\right).

Proof.

Suppose first that
v\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
. Averaging over

[0,1]
gives a point
y_{0}\in[0,1]
such that

|v(y_{0})|^{2}\leq 3\int v^{2}(y)e^{-|y|}\,dy
.
The fundamental theorem of calculus and Cauchy–Schwarz yield

|v(y)|^{2}\leq 2|v(y_{0})|^{2}+2|y-y_{0}|\int_{\min\{y,y_{0}\}}^{\max\{y,y_{0}\}}|v_{y}(x)|^{2}\,dx.

Since
D\geq 1
and
y_{0}\in[0,1]
, integration over

[-D,D]
gives

\int_{|y|\leq D}v^{2}(y)\,dy\leq CD^{2}\left(\int v^{2}e^{-|y|}+\int v_{y}^{2}\right).

Density extends this estimate to
H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
. Apply it to

w(\cdot,y_{2})
for almost every
y_{2}
, and integrate in
y_{2}
,
to obtain the lemma.
∎

The weighted mass term in the lemma is bounded by

C\mathcal{N}_{1}(t)
. In the present zero-energy
asymptotic-stability regime, (
10.1
),
(
10.2
), and the hierarchy of the
\mathcal{N}_{i}
give

\|\nabla\varepsilon(t)\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim|b(t)|+\mathcal{N}_{2}(t)\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{3}(t).

Consequently, for every
D\geq 1
,

\iint_{|y_{1}|\leq D}\varepsilon^{2}(t)\leq CD^{2}\mathcal{N}_{3}(t),

(10.4)

where
C
is independent of
D
and
t
.

Lemma 10.3
.

Let
\varrho\in C_{c}^{4}(\mathbb{R})
be nonnegative, with

\operatorname{supp}\varrho\subset[-1,1]
, and define

\varrho_{D}(y_{1}):=\varrho\left(\frac{y_{1}}{D}\right),\qquad D\geq 1.

There exist
T_{0}>0
and
C>0
, independent of
D\geq 1
, such that, for every

t_{0}\geq T_{0}
,

\displaystyle\iint\varepsilon^{2}(t_{0},y_{1},y_{2})\varrho_{D}(y_{1})\,dy_{1}dy_{2}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\mathcal{N}_{4}(t_{0})+C\min\left(\mathcal{N}_{2}^{1/2}(t_{0}),D\mathcal{N}_{4}(t_{0})\right)

\displaystyle+C\int_{t_{0}}^{\infty}\left|\iint\varepsilon^{2}(t,y_{1},y_{2})(\varrho_{D})_{y_{1}}(y_{1})\,dy_{1}dy_{2}\right|dt.

The constant
C
may depend on
\varrho
, but not on
D
or
t_{0}
.

Proof.

Throughout the proof, all spatial integrals are over
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. In the
asymptotic-stability regime, (
7.16
) gives
\lambda\sim 1
;
therefore
dt=\lambda^{3}ds\sim ds
, and we use (
6.10
) in either
time variable without further comment.

Recall that

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{t}(t,y_{1},y_{2})={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\lambda^{3}}\partial_{y_{1}}\Delta\varepsilon-\mathfrak{H}-b_{t}\zeta_{b}P-\frac{1}{\lambda^{3}}f(\varepsilon)_{y_{1}}+\frac{\lambda_{t}}{\lambda}\Lambda\varepsilon
