within the knot rectangle and derivatives of
W
and
f_{a}
up to order
two; the latter are obtained from the radial ODE and the enclosed
Q
,

Q_{r}
, so that no unknown Taylor-model remainder is differentiated. We
then integrate over all knot rectangles in
[-L-h,L+h]^{2}
. Outside that
square,
u_{h,a}=0
and
r_{a}=f_{a}
, so that (
E.61
)
with
L+h
supplies the remaining residual and constraint norm bounds,
while the central integrals
B_{ab}
vanish outside the spline
support. Symmetric entry enclosures are intersected, and the exact
mixed even–odd entries are zero.

The Recorded Constraint Certificate

The compact-spline computation uses the following inputs. The spline
grid is given by
L=20
,
N=512
, and
h=2L/N=5/64
, so that the
support is contained in
[-20-5/64,20+5/64]^{2}
; the coefficients are
four
(N-1)\times(N-1)
arrays in
coefficients.f64
,
interpreted as exact dyadic numbers; the profile is the validated input
of Section
E.1
, with
R_{Q}=15
,
z=4
, and the tail
constants above; the quadrature uses adaptive subdivision at the square
radii
3,6,10
, with respective factors
8,4,2,1
per coordinate; and
the inverse bound is
\delta=31/50
, justified by
Proposition
E.7
. The recorded coefficient file has
SHA-256

4c2b8a95dd992e7d8778e88c88a7a592d4ac78261733246949225eccea01cf19.

The recorded integration uses
1\,022\,644
rectangles, obtained by
subdividing each knot cell into
8^{2}
,
4^{2}
,
2^{2}
, or one rectangle,
according as the maximum absolute coordinate of its center is below

3
, between
3
and
6
, between
6
and
10
, or at least
10
. The
executed residual integration required approximately
437
seconds in
the recorded environment. The outward-rounded upper bounds for the
whole-space residuals and for the constraint norms are

\displaystyle\rho_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 0.110293,

\displaystyle\rho_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 0.111000,

\displaystyle\rho_{3}

\displaystyle\leq 0.220809,

\displaystyle\rho_{o}

\displaystyle\leq 0.117280,

\displaystyle\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 4.048337,

\displaystyle\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2.908460,

\displaystyle\|f_{3}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 3.853534,

\displaystyle\|f_{o}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2.609398.

All the profile uncertainty, the finite quadrature remainders, and the
exterior tails are included. The finite integration uses directly the
true interval potential and constraints, so that this certificate needs
no further approximation of the constraints or of the potential. Using

\delta=31/50
in (
E.64
) gives the following
conservatively widened enclosure of the even block:

[A]=\begin{pmatrix}[2.830253,2.869958]&[0.560263,0.600181]&[-4.367974,-4.288499]\\
[0.560263,0.600181]&[1.825078,1.865248]&[2.556290,2.636233]\\
[-4.367974,-4.288499]&[2.556290,2.636233]&[12.562538,12.721890]\end{pmatrix}.

For every real symmetric matrix in this enclosure,

\det A\in[-7.946966743614993375,-1.232927442846774158]<0.

The odd pairing is enclosed separately by

m_{oo}\in[-0.669373,-0.624397]<0.

All displayed decimal endpoints are exact rationals. To prove the
determinant enclosure, we write

A=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b&c\\
b&d&e\\
c&e&f\end{smallmatrix}\right)
,
so that

\det A=adf+2bce-ae^{2}-dc^{2}-fb^{2}.

Throughout the displayed box,
b,d,e>0>c
, and

df-e^{2}
,
af-c^{2}
, and
ad-b^{2}
have positive lower bounds;
consequently, the determinant increases in
a,c,d,f
and decreases in

b,e
, and evaluating the two corresponding corners with exact rational
arithmetic gives precisely the stated endpoints. Moreover,

A_{11}>0,\qquad A_{11}A_{22}-A_{12}^{2}\in[4.805215251973,5.039288790415].

The leading
2\times 2
block is therefore positive definite, and its
Schur complement in
A
, which equals
\det A
divided by this positive
principal minor, is negative. Completing the square then proves that
every symmetric member of
[A]
has one negative and two positive
eigenvalues.

The executed source
constraints/spline_verify.cpp
integrates
all
1\,022\,644
rectangles using the validated
Q
profile earlier computed from CAPD. Its
integral matrix, residuals, and inverse matrix enclosure are
recorded, together with a separate hexadecimal endpoint file, in

constraints/results/constraint_certificate.json
.

The displayed matrix widens these computed
intervals outward. An exact rational calculation verifies the determinant
monotonicity and corner values on this displayed box; a separate C++
replay also checks the matrix formula and the signed pivots
from the stored interval endpoints. The inverse norm assumption in that
residual calculation is discharged by the spectral certificate of
Section
E.3
.

These are precisely the numerical inputs of Lemma
B.8
:
there,
\det A<0
and the symmetry of
A
give, through
Lemma
B.6
, an even combination
f_{e}
of
f_{1},f_{2},f_{3}
with

(\mathcal{L}^{-1}f_{e},f_{e})<0
, parity gives

(\mathcal{L}^{-1}f_{e},f_{o})=0
, and
m_{oo}<0
completes a negative
definite
2\times 2
inverse Gram matrix; since

\operatorname{ind}_{-}(\mathcal{L})=2
and
0\in\rho(\mathcal{L})
,
Proposition
B.1
then yields the
coercivity of the virial form on the four-constraint subspace. We do
not repeat that argument here.

E.5
The Constant Governing the Blow-Up Rate

We return to the variables
(x_{1},x_{2})
and to the function
g_{Q}

defined in (
2.1
). In this section,
A
denotes the
one-dimensional differential operator below, and all inner products
involving
g_{Q}
are taken in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Writing
z=x_{2}
, we have

g_{Q}(z)=z\int_{\mathbb{R}}Q_{x_{2}}(x_{1},z)\,dx_{1},\qquad c=\frac{2(A^{-1}g_{Q},g_{Q})}{(g_{Q},g_{Q})},\qquad A=-\frac{d^{2}}{dz^{2}}+1.

(E.67)

The Fourier transform sends
A
to the multiplication by
1+\xi^{2}
;
thus
A
is self-adjoint on
H^{2}(\mathbb{R})
and

\|A^{-1}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}=1
. The function
k(z)=e^{-|z|}/2
solves

Ak=0
away from zero and has derivative jump
-1
there, so that
Ak

is the unit point mass at zero and its translates give the Green kernel

e^{-|z-t|}/2
. Plancherel’s identity identifies this quotient with
(
2.2
), the Fourier normalization cancelling. For
later use, we check the elementary analytic properties directly.
Radiality gives

g_{Q}(z)=2z^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{Q^{\prime}(\sqrt{z^{2}+t^{2}})}{\sqrt{z^{2}+t^{2}}}\,dt,

(E.68)

where the quotient
Q^{\prime}(r)/r
is defined at zero by its limit
Q^{\prime\prime}(0)
.
This formula proves that
g_{Q}
is even and, since
Q^{\prime}(r)<0
for
r>0
,
that
g_{Q}(z)<0
for
z\neq 0
; in particular,
g_{Q}
is not identically
zero. Smoothness near the origin and exponential decay at infinity give

g_{Q}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
: for instance, for large
|z|
, the bound

|Q^{\prime}(r)|\leq Ce^{-r}
and
r\geq(|z|+|x_{1}|)/\sqrt{2}
give

|g_{Q}(z)|\leq 2\sqrt{2}C|z|e^{-|z|/\sqrt{2}}
. Integration by parts in
x_{1}

also gives the exact identity

g_{Q}(z)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Lambda Q(x_{1},z)\,dx_{1},

(E.69)

because
\int(Q+x_{1}Q_{x_{1}})\,dx_{1}=[x_{1}Q]_{-\infty}^{\infty}=0
. The
multiplier
(1+\xi^{2})^{-1}
is positive and strictly below one away
from
\xi=0
, and since a nonzero
L^{2}
Fourier transform cannot be
supported at a single point, (
E.67
) implies
0<c<2
.
Proposition
D.1
in Appendix
D
establishes the
stronger analytic inequality
c>1
. The remaining numerical task is to
enclose this quotient with enough precision for the choice of the
polynomial weight.

Radial Quadrature and Tails

The CAPD calculation uses the soliton
Q
profile validated in
Section
E.1
. With

R_{Q}=15
and fixed-point radius
4
, the tail construction gives

|Q(r)|\leq C_{0}r^{-1/2}e^{-r}
with
C_{0}=4
, and

|Q^{\prime}(r)|\leq C_{1}r^{-1/2}e^{-r},\qquad C_{1}<3.694973,\qquad r\geq 15.

Set
F(s)=Q^{\prime}(\sqrt{s})/\sqrt{s}
, with its continuous value at
s=0
,
so that

g_{Q}(z)=2z^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}F(z^{2}+t^{2})\,dt.

The numerical inputs are the profile tail radius
R_{Q}=15
, the tail
derivative bound
C_{1}<3.694973
, the support radius
L_{g}=16

and the radial cutoff
R_{c}=24
, the radial and transverse spacings

h_{r}=h_{z}=2^{-8}
, and the origin series of order
N=40
on the region

0\leq r\leq 1/2
. The radial nodes are
r_{i}=ih_{r}
and
s_{i}=r_{i}^{2}
. At
each radial node, a binary64 midpoint
f_{i}
of the validated enclosure
of
F(s_{i})
is fixed as an exact real number, and we let

\ell_{i}(s)=a_{i}+b_{i}s
interpolate
f_{i},f_{i+1}
on
[s_{i},s_{i+1}]
. If

\nu_{i}
bounds the two nodal errors and
B_{i}
bounds

\sup_{[s_{i},s_{i+1}]}|F^{\prime\prime}|
, then

|F(s)-\ell_{i}(s)|\leq e_{i}:=\nu_{i}+\frac{(s_{i+1}-s_{i})^{2}}{8}B_{i};

this is the usual interpolation remainder applied to the exact
function, followed by the linear interpolation of the nodal errors.
For
r\leq 1/2
, the calculation uses the origin recurrence with
N=40
:

F(s)=2\sum_{n\geq 1}nA_{n}s^{n-1},\qquad|A_{n}|\leq a_{*}b^{n},\quad b=(1+a_{*}^{2})/4.

All the needed derivatives of this series have explicit geometric
remainder bounds. Specifically, for
d=0,1,2
, we set
n_{0}=N+1
and

\rho_{d}=bs_{*}\left(\frac{n_{0}+1}{n_{0}-d}\right)^{d+1}<1.

The first omitted majorant term for the
d
th derivative in
s
,
divided by
1-\rho_{d}
, bounds the entire remainder on
0\leq s\leq s_{*}
;
this ratio bound is conservative, since the exact ratio of consecutive
coefficient factors is
(n+1)/(n-d)
, and here
s_{*}\leq 1/4
. For

r\geq 1/2
, we use
s=r^{2}
and
Q_{r}=rF(s)
, so that
Q_{rr}=F+2sF^{\prime}
and

\partial_{s}[Q(\sqrt{s})]=F/2
. Substitution into the radial equation,
followed by differentiation in
s
, gives

F^{\prime}(s)=\frac{Q-Q^{3}-2F}{2s},\qquad F^{\prime\prime}(s)=\frac{(1-3Q^{2})F-8F^{\prime}(s)}{4s}.

These identities enclose the derivatives from
Q
and
Q_{r}
without
differentiating a Taylor-model remainder, and they also give explicit
exterior bounds. Set

\displaystyle C_{F^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{0}+C_{0}^{3}e^{-2R_{c}}/R_{c}+2C_{1}/R_{c}}{2},

\displaystyle C_{F^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1+3C_{0}^{2}e^{-2R_{c}}/R_{c})C_{1}+8C_{F^{\prime}}/R_{c}}{4}.

For
r\geq R_{c}
, the profile bounds and the two identities imply

|F(r^{2})|\leq C_{1}\frac{e^{-r}}{r^{3/2}},\quad|F^{\prime}(r^{2})|\leq C_{F^{\prime}}\frac{e^{-r}}{r^{5/2}},\quad|F^{\prime\prime}(r^{2})|\leq C_{F^{\prime\prime}}\frac{e^{-r}}{r^{7/2}}.

(E.70)

For instance, one divides the three terms in
Q-Q^{3}-2F
by
2r^{2}
and
bounds the remaining decreasing factors by their values at
R_{c}
; the
estimate for
F^{\prime\prime}
follows in the same way.

At a fixed
z
node, the radial shell corresponds to a
t
interval

[u,v]
, obtained by intersecting
[s_{i},s_{i+1}]
with
[z^{2},R_{c}^{2}]

and taking
t=\sqrt{s-z^{2}}
. Its central integral is exactly

(v-u)\left[a_{i}+b_{i}\left(z^{2}+\frac{v^{2}+uv+u^{2}}{3}\right)\right],

and its error is at most
e_{i}(v-u)
. Every square root, product, sum,
and division in these formulas is evaluated by CAPD interval
arithmetic. If
T=\sqrt{R_{c}^{2}-z^{2}}
, the omitted radial tail is
bounded by

\int_{T}^{\infty}|F(z^{2}+t^{2})|\,dt\leq\frac{C_{1}}{R_{c}^{3/2}}(R_{c}-T+1)e^{-R_{c}}.

Indeed,
r\geq R_{c}
throughout that integral and
r\geq t
, and one
splits the integral at
t=R_{c}
. At the nodes
z_{j}=jh_{z}
,

0\leq j\leq L_{g}/h_{z}
, we choose the binary64 midpoint of the enclosure
of
-g_{Q}(z_{j})
. These values define a piecewise linear function
p_{h}

on
[0,L_{g}]
, which we extend evenly to
[-L_{g},L_{g}]
and by zero
outside, and we set
g_{h}=-p_{h}
. The origin series and
(
E.70
) provide integrable majorants for the first two

z
derivatives, uniformly on compact
z
intervals, so that
differentiation under the integral sign is justified. Differentiating

2z^{2}F(z^{2}+t^{2})
twice gives

g_{Q}^{\prime\prime}(z)=4\int_{0}^{\infty}F\,dt+20z^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}F^{\prime}\,dt+8z^{4}\int_{0}^{\infty}F^{\prime\prime}\,dt.

For a cell
[z_{j},z_{j+1}]\subset[0,L_{g}]
, we partition the
t
axis
using the radial shells at
z_{j}
. The map
z\mapsto\sqrt{z^{2}+t^{2}}
is

1
-Lipschitz, so that the radius increases by at most
h_{z}
; we thus
evaluate
F(r^{2}),F^{\prime}(r^{2}),F^{\prime\prime}(r^{2})
for
r\in[r_{i},r_{i+1}+h_{z}]
on the
corresponding portion of the cell, and we integrate the absolute bounds
over these finite portions. For the remaining

t\geq T_{j}:=\sqrt{R_{c}^{2}-z_{j}^{2}}
, the same split at
R_{c}
and
(
E.70
) give the explicit contribution

(R_{c}-T_{j}+1)e^{-R_{c}}\left(\frac{4C_{1}}{R_{c}^{3/2}}+\frac{20z_{j+1}^{2}C_{F^{\prime}}}{R_{c}^{5/2}}+\frac{8z_{j+1}^{4}C_{F^{\prime\prime}}}{R_{c}^{7/2}}\right).

If
m_{j}\geq\sup|g_{Q}^{\prime\prime}|
on that
z
cell and
v_{j}
bounds its two nodal
errors, the local error is at most
v_{j}+h_{z}^{2}m_{j}/8
. The remaining
transverse tail also has an explicit bound: for
z\geq L_{g}\geq 15
,
radiality and
r\geq z
give

|g_{Q}(z)|\leq 2C_{1}\sqrt{z}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\sqrt{z^{2}+t^{2}}}\,dt\leq 2C_{1}\sqrt{z}\,(z+1)e^{-z},

where, for the last inequality, we split at
t=z
and use

\sqrt{z^{2}+t^{2}}\geq z
on the first interval and

\sqrt{z^{2}+t^{2}}\geq t
on the second. Let

J_{n}=\int_{L_{g}}^{\infty}z^{n}e^{-2z}\,dz
, so that
J_{0}=e^{-2L_{g}}/2
and

J_{n}=L_{g}^{n}e^{-2L_{g}}/2+nJ_{n-1}/2
. The total error bound is

\varepsilon_{g}^{2}=2\sum_{j}h_{z}\left(v_{j}+\frac{h_{z}^{2}m_{j}}{8}\right)^{2}+8C_{1}^{2}(J_{3}+2J_{2}+J_{1}).

The Quotient Certificate

For the exact piecewise linear approximation just defined, we set

\displaystyle D_{h}

\displaystyle=\int_{-L_{g}}^{L_{g}}g_{h}(z)^{2}\,dz,

(E.71)

\displaystyle N_{h}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{-L_{g}}^{L_{g}}\int_{-L_{g}}^{L_{g}}e^{-|z-t|}g_{h}(z)g_{h}(t)\,dt\,dz.

Then
N_{h}=(A^{-1}g_{h},g_{h})
for the whole-line inverse, and since

g_{h}=-p_{h}
, the same integrals are obtained with
p_{h}
. For this
integration, we index the full symmetric mesh by
z_{j}=-L_{g}+jh_{z}
,

0\leq j\leq 2L_{g}/h_{z}
, and on a cell we write
p_{h}(z_{j}+u)=a+bu
,

0\leq u\leq h_{z}
. The function

S(z)=\int_{-L_{g}}^{z}e^{-(z-t)}p_{h}(t)\,dt

satisfies
S^{\prime}+S=p_{h}
, and if
S_{j}=S(z_{j})
, its exact solution there is

S(z_{j}+u)=a+b(u-1)+(S_{j}-a+b)e^{-u}.

Thus, starting from
S_{0}=0
,

S_{j+1}=a+b(h_{z}-1)+(S_{j}-a+b)e^{-h_{z}}.

Integrating
p_{h}^{2}
and
p_{h}S
on the cell gives, respectively,

a^{2}h_{z}+abh_{z}^{2}+b^{2}h_{z}^{3}/3

and

a^{2}h_{z}+ab(h_{z}^{2}-h_{z})+b^{2}(h_{z}^{3}/3-h_{z}^{2}/2)+(S_{j}-a+b)\{a(1-e^{-h_{z}})+b[1-(1+h_{z})e^{-h_{z}}]\}.

The symmetry of the Green kernel gives

N_{h}=\int p_{h}(z)\int_{-L_{g}}^{z}e^{-(z-t)}p_{h}(t)\,dt\,dz.

All polynomial and exponential evaluations use outward arithmetic.
This evaluates the whole-line inverse without introducing a finite-box
boundary condition.

Proposition E.9
.

Suppose that

\|g_{Q}-g_{h}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\varepsilon_{g},\qquad\|g_{h}\|_{L^{2}}\leq G_{h},\qquad D_{h}\in[D_{h}^{-},D_{h}^{+}],\quad N_{h}\in[N_{h}^{-},N_{h}^{+}].

Set

\displaystyle E_{g}

\displaystyle=2G_{h}\varepsilon_{g}+\varepsilon_{g}^{2},

(E.72)

\displaystyle D_{-}

\displaystyle=D_{h}^{-}-E_{g},

\displaystyle D_{+}

\displaystyle=D_{h}^{+}+E_{g},

\displaystyle N_{-}

\displaystyle=\max\{0,N_{h}^{-}-E_{g}\},

\displaystyle N_{+}

\displaystyle=N_{h}^{+}+E_{g}.

If
D_{-}>0
, then

c\in\left[\frac{2N_{-}}{D_{+}},\frac{2N_{+}}{D_{-}}\right].

(E.73)

In particular, the strict finite inequality

2(N_{h}^{-}-E_{g})-\frac{21}{13}(D_{h}^{+}+E_{g})>0

(E.74)

implies
c>21/13
. The additional inequality

\frac{5}{3}(D_{h}^{-}-E_{g})-2(N_{h}^{+}+E_{g})\geq 0

(E.75)

implies
c\leq 5/3
.

Proof.

Let
e_{g}=g_{Q}-g_{h}
,
D=(g_{Q},g_{Q})
, and

N=(A^{-1}g_{Q},g_{Q})
. Expansion and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality give

|D-D_{h}|\leq 2\|g_{h}\|_{L^{2}}\|e_{g}\|_{L^{2}}+\|e_{g}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq E_{g},

and self-adjointness together with
\|A^{-1}\|=1
likewise implies

\displaystyle|N-N_{h}|

\displaystyle=|2(A^{-1}g_{h},e_{g})+(A^{-1}e_{g},e_{g})|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|g_{h}\|_{L^{2}}\|e_{g}\|_{L^{2}}+\|e_{g}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq E_{g}.

Since
N>0
and
D>0
, these inequalities yield
N\in[N_{-},N_{+}]
and

D\in[D_{-},D_{+}]
, and division by the positive denominator proves
(
E.73
). The last two claims follow by cross
multiplication with the same positive bounds.
∎

The norm bound
G_{h}
is obtained by outward evaluation of

\sqrt{D_{h}^{+}}
. The recorded CAPD output gives the following
conservative decimal enclosures, all margins being evaluated from the
stored interval endpoints before the decimal bounds are widened for
display:

\displaystyle D_{h}

\displaystyle\in[28.6713738040094,\ 28.6713738040246],

\displaystyle N_{h}

\displaystyle\in[23.8185433397371,\ 23.8185433397610],

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{g}

\displaystyle\leq 0.000162843515960,\qquad E_{g}\leq 0.001743939127094,

D_{h}^{-}-E_{g}>28.6696298648823;

the lower margin in (
E.74
) is greater than

1.3154855238212
, the upper margin in (
E.75
) is
greater than
0.1421418836944
, and

c\in[1.66126332085379,\ 1.66170874135113]\subset(\frac{21}{13},\frac{5}{3}).

We note here that the quotient calculation depends only on the independently validated
Q
profile. Its source is
quotient/constant_verify.cpp
, and

quotient/results/quotient.json
records the exact hexadecimal
interval endpoints.

The file
quotient/results/approximant.hex

specifies every binary64 node of
p_{h}
; its digest is included in the
package manifest.

For the exponent
K_{i}=\lfloor c(i+3)/(c-1)\rfloor+1
of the polynomial
weight introduced in Chapter
5
, the precise
role of the threshold is

K_{5}\leq 21\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad c>\frac{21}{13},\qquad K_{5}=21\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\frac{21}{13}<c\leq\frac{5}{3},

(E.76)

where
c>1
is understood. Indeed,
8c/(c-1)=8+8/(c-1)
is decreasing
on
(1,\infty)
, and
K_{5}=21
means that
20\leq 8c/(c-1)<21
. Thus
(
E.74
) makes the polynomial weight of order
21

admissible, while (
E.75
) also identifies the integer

K_{5}
with
21
; the strict positivity of the left-hand side of
(
E.75
) is a sufficient test for its nonnegative
version.

Remark E.10

(Assembly of the certificates and numerical data)
.

The soliton
Q
construction, the potential comparison, the finite
spectral tests, the compact-spline residuals, and the quotient bounds
are executed in the companion package

Chapter_E_CAPD_fresh
. The complete source code and implementation files are available in the public GitHub repository at
https://github.com/kk10-genius/ZK2d3p-blowup
. The ground-state validation uses
only the origin recurrence, CAPD Taylor integration, and the Bessel
tail construction.
The common exported profile is then used in every potential, residual,
and quotient calculation.

The potential comparison transfers the finite spectral bounds to the
whole space. The resulting gap controls the quadratic inverse-residual
correction and supplies the constraint signs used in
Proposition
B.1
.

The package contains the C++ sources, the pinned CAPD source archive,
the exact profile coefficients and interval remainders, all numerical
witnesses, and the executed output of each stage. A replay script
repeats the stages in their dependency order, and a manifest gives
SHA-256 digests. Binary interval endpoints are retained in hexadecimal
form; the decimal bounds printed in this appendix are widened
outward.
